Dynamics of the combined nonlinear Schrödinger equation with inverse-square potential
Zuyu Ma, Yilin Song, Jiqiang Zheng

Abstract
. We consider the long-time dynamics of focusing energy-critical Schrödinger equation perturbed by the H ˙ 1 2 \dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}} -critical nonlinearity and with inverse-square potential(CNLS a ) in dimensions d ∈ { 3 , 4 , 5 } d\in\{3,4,5\} (CNLS a ) i ​ ∂ t u − ℒ a ​ u = − | u | 4 d − 2 ​ u + | u | 4 d − 1 ​ u , ( t , x ) ∈ ℝ × ℝ d , \displaystyle i\partial_{t}u-\mathcal{L}_{a}u=-|u|^{\frac{4}{d-2}}u+|u|^{\frac{4}{d-1}}u,\quad(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}, where ℒ a = − Δ + a ​ | x | − 2 \mathcal{L}_{a}=-\Delta+a|x|^{-2} and the energy is below and equal to the threshold m a m_{a} , which is given by the ground state W a W_{a} satisfying ℒ a ​ W a = | W a | 4 d − 2 ​ W a \mathcal{L}_{a}W_{a}=|W_{a}|^{\frac{4}{d-2}}W_{a} . When the energy is below the threshold, we utilize the concentration-compactness argument as well as the variatonal analysis to characterize the scattering and blow-up region. When the energy is equal to the threshold, we use the modulation analysis associated to the equation ( CNLSa ) to classify the dynamics of H a 1 H_{a}^{1} -solution. In both regimes of scattering results, we do not need the radial assumption in d = 4 , 5 d=4,5 . Our result generalize the scattering results of [ 31 , 32 , 33 ] and [ 3 ] in the setting of standard combined NLS.



2010 Mathematics Subject Classification
35Q55

1.
Introduction

In this article, we investigate the global well-posedness and the asymptotic behavior of solutions to the focusing-defocusing combined nonlinear Schrödinger equation(NLS) with an inverse-square potential:

(CNLS
a
)

\begin{cases}(i\partial_{t}-\mathcal{L}_{a})u=-|u|^{\frac{4}{d-2}}u+|u|^{\frac{4}{d-1}}u,\\
u|_{t=0}=u_{0}\in H_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\end{cases}

where
u:\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
, and the operator

\mathcal{L}_{a}=-\Delta+a|x|^{-2}

is defined via the Friedrichs extension of the following quadratic form
Q(f)
on
C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\backslash\left\{0\right\})

Q(f)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\nabla f|^{2}+\frac{a}{|x|^{2}}|f(x)|^{2}dx

with
a>-\tfrac{(d-2)^{2}}{4}
and
d\geqslant 3
, which guarantees the positivity of
\mathcal{L}_{a}
and the equivalence of Sobolev norms

(1.1)

\|u\|_{\dot{H}^{1}}\sim\|u\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}:=\|\mathcal{L}_{a}^{\frac{1}{2}}u\|_{L^{2}}.

from the sharp Hardy inequality. The operator
\mathcal{L}_{a}^{\frac{1}{2}}
is defined via the standard functional calculus. By using the inner-product of
L^{2}
, we can rewrite the
\dot{H}_{a}^{1}
as

\displaystyle\|u\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}=\|\mathcal{L}_{a}^{\frac{1}{2}}u\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}=\langle\mathcal{L}_{a}^{\frac{1}{2}}u,\mathcal{L}_{a}^{\frac{1}{2}}u\rangle_{L^{2}}=Q(u).

The equation (
CNLSa
) has two conserved quantities, namely, the mass and energy:

Mass

\displaystyle:M(u)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|u|^{2}\,dx,

Energy

\displaystyle:E_{a}(u)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\frac{1}{2}|\nabla u|^{2}+\frac{a}{2|x|^{2}}|u|^{2}+\frac{d-1}{2(d+1)}|u|^{\frac{2(d+1)}{d-1}}-\frac{d-2}{2d}|u|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}\right)\,dx.

By (
1.1
) and Sobolev embedding, we see that
M(u_{0})
,
|E_{a}(u_{0})|<\infty
if
u_{0}\in H_{a}^{1}
.

An important model related to (
CNLSa
) is the focusing energy-critical NLS with inverse-square potential:

(NLS
a
)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}i\partial_{t}u-\mathcal{L}_{a}u=-|u|^{\frac{4}{d-2}}u,&(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d},\\
u(0,x)=u_{0}\in\dot{H}_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).\end{cases}

The solution to
(
NLSa
)
enjoys two conservation laws

\displaystyle M(u)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|u|^{2}dx,

\displaystyle E_{a}^{c}(u)=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\nabla u|^{2}dx+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{a|u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}dx-\frac{d-2}{2d}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|u|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}dx.

The equation
(
NLSa
)
has radial positive static solution
W_{a}(x)
, where
W_{a}
is the solution to the following elliptic equation

(1.2)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{a}W_{a}(x)=|W_{a}|^{\frac{4}{d-2}}W_{a}.

More specifically, a radial explicit solution of (
1.2
) is given by

(1.3)

W_{a}(x):=[d(d-2)\beta^{2}]^{\frac{d-2}{4}}\biggl[\frac{|x|^{\beta-1}}{1+|x|^{2\beta}}\biggr]^{\frac{d-2}{2}},

where
1\geqslant\beta>0
and
a=(\frac{d-2}{2})^{2}[\beta^{2}-1]
. We refer to this solution as the
ground state
.

In particular, when
a=0
, the equation (
CNLSa
) turns to the classical focusing-defocusing combined NLS:

(CNLS)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}i\partial_{t}u+\Delta u=-|u|^{\frac{4}{d-2}}u+|u|^{\frac{4}{d-1}}u,&(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d},\\
u(0,x)=u_{0}\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\end{cases}

where its solution enjoys two conservation laws as

\displaystyle M_{0}(u)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|u|^{2}dx

\displaystyle E_{0}(u)=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\nabla u|^{2}dx-\frac{d-2}{2d}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|u|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}dx+\frac{d-1}{2(d+1)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|u|^{\frac{2(d+1)}{d-1}}dx.

The combined NLS can be understood as the perturbation of the standard focusing energy-critical NLS:

(NLS)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}i\partial_{t}u+\Delta u=-|u|^{\frac{4}{d-2}}u,&(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d},\\
u(0,x)=u_{0}\in\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\end{cases}

with associated mass and energy

\displaystyle M(u)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|u|^{2}dx,

\displaystyle E_{0}^{c}(u)=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\nabla u|^{2}dx-\frac{d-2}{2d}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|u|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}dx.

The equation
(
NLS
)
also has a radial positive static solution which satisfies

\displaystyle-\Delta W_{0}=|W_{0}|^{\frac{4}{d-2}}W_{0}.

The explicit solution of (
1.2
) is given by

\displaystyle W_{0}(x):=[d(d-2)]^{\frac{d-2}{4}}\biggl(\frac{1}{1+|x|^{2}}\biggr)^{\frac{d-2}{2}}.

We call
W_{0}
as the
ground state
related to (
NLS
).

1.1.
History

1.1.1.
Sub-threshold scattering/blow-up dichotomy

In this part, we will survey the related research in the study of the scattering/blow-up dichotomy of the focusing energy-critical NLS with or without potential and the focusing-defocusing combined NLS below the energy threshold.

For equation
(
NLS
)
(i.e. (
NLSa
) with
a=0
), the global well-posedness and scattering/blow-up dichotomy below the energy threshold
E_{0}^{c}(W_{0})
is highly
developed. Kenig and Merle
[
18
]
developed the concentration-compactness argument
[
19
]
to establish the scattering/blow-up dichotomy of radial solutions in dimension
d\in\{3,4,5\}
. Later, Killip and Visan
[
25
]
used the double-Duhamel trick to remove the radial assumption of scattering theory in higher dimensions
d\geqslant 5
. However, their method fails when
d=3,4
because of the weaker dispersive estimates. Inspired by the work on solving the mass-critical problem
[
10
,
11
,
12
]
, Dodson
[
13
]
overcame the logarithmic loss in double-Duhamel argument and prove the scattering result for non-radial
\dot{H}^{1}
initial data in four dimension. But, the global well-posedness and scattering for
(
NLS
)
for non-radial
\dot{H}^{1}
initial data in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
is still open. We summary above to the following theorem(see also in Figure 1):

Theorem 1.1

(Scattering/blow-up dichotomy for
(
NLS
)
,
[
13
,
18
,
25
]
)
.

Let
d\geqslant 3
. Suppose that
u_{0}\in\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
u_{0}
is radial if
d=3
. Let
u
be the corresponding maximal-lifespan solution to
(
NLS
)
with
u|_{t=0}=u_{0}
.

(1)

Scattering part:

If
u_{0}
obeys

(1.4)

\displaystyle E_{0}^{c}(u_{0})<E_{0}^{c}(W_{0}),\quad\|u_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}<\|W_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},

then
u
is global. Moreover,
u\in L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
and scatters in
\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
when
t\to\pm\infty
, i.e. there exist
u_{\pm}\in\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
such that

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\pm\infty}\big\|u-e^{it\Delta}u_{\pm}\big\|_{\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}=0.

(2)

Blow-up part:
If
u_{0}\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
is radial and obeys

(1.5)

\displaystyle E_{0}^{c}(u_{0})<E_{0}^{c}(W_{0}),\quad\|u_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}>\|W_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},

then, the solution
u
blows up in finite time in both time direction.

) (solutions scatter). The region BDE corresponds to (

(blow-up in finite time).

For the standard combined NLS, i.e.
(
CNLS
)
,
Miao-Xu-Zhao
[
31
]
first characterized the scattering and blow-up regions for focusing energy-critical NLS with
\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}
perturbation where the
H^{1}
-initial data is radial and energy is below
E_{0}^{c}(W_{0})
by the variation method and concentration-compactness argument
in dimension three. Xu-Yang
[
39
]
extended the result to any mass-supercritical and energy-subcritical perturbation, i.e.
\frac{4}{3}<p<5
. Later, Miao-Xu-Zhao
[
32
]
and Miao-Zhao-Zheng
[
33
]
gives the scattering/blow-up dichotomy for general
\dot{H}^{1}
initial values in four and higher dimensions. But the scattering/blow-up dichotomy for general
\dot{H}^{1}
initial values in three dimension is still open. In general, we have:

Theorem 1.2

(Scattering/blow-up dichotomy for for
(
CNLS
)
,
[
31
,
32
,
33
,
39
]
)
.

Let
d\geqslant 3
. Suppose that
u_{0}\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
u_{0}
is radial when
d=3
. Let
u
be the corresponding maximal-lifespan solution to
(
NLS
)
with
u|_{t=0}=u_{0}
.

(1)

Scattering part:

If
u_{0}
obeys

(1.6)

\displaystyle E_{0}(u_{0})<E_{0}^{c}(W_{0}),\quad\|u_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}<\|W_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},

then
u
is global. Moreover,
u\in L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
and scatters in
\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
when
t\to\pm\infty
, i.e. there exist
u_{\pm}\in{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
such that

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\pm\infty}\big\|u-e^{it\Delta}u_{\pm}\big\|_{{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}=0.

(2)

Blow-up part:
If
u_{0}
is radial and obeys

(1.7)

\displaystyle E_{0}^{c}(u_{0})<E_{0}^{c}(W_{0}),\quad\|u_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}>\|W_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},

then, the solution
u
blows up in finite time in both time direction.

Next, we recall the result of the energy-critical NLS with inverse-square potential, i.e. (
NLSa
) with
a<0
.
Killip-Visan-Miao-Zhang-Zheng
[
20
]
first studied the global well-posedness and scattering of defocusing energy-critical NLS with inverse-square potential

(DNLS
a
)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}i\partial_{t}u-\mathcal{L}_{a}u=|u|^{4}u,&(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\\
u(0,x)=u_{0}\in\dot{H}_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\end{cases}

by developing the linear profile decomposition in
\dot{H}_{a}^{1}
and using Kenig-Merle’s concentration-compactness argument. They also carry out the variational analysis needed to treat the equation
(
NLSa
)
. Moreover, they give the proof of finite-time blow-up when energy below that of the ground state
W_{a}
and
\|u_{0}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}>\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}
.
For the focusing energy-critical NLS with inverse-square potential, i.e. (
NLSa
), Yang
[
40
]
proved the global well-posedness and scattering for Cauchy problem
(
NLSa
)
with energy below that of ground state and
\|u_{0}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}<\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}
when
3\leqslant d\leqslant 6
.
1
1

1

When
d\geq 7
, the stability lemma is difficult to establish due to the lack of the
L^{\infty}-L^{1}
dispersive estimate. We also remark that it is possible to derive the stablity lemma by using the
L^{p}-L^{p^{\prime}}
dispersive estimate with
p>1
, see Miao-Su-Zheng
[
30
]
for details. This is also the unique reason why we restrict the dimension
d\in\{3,4,5\}
.
It is worth to note that if
d=3
, they also assume
u_{0}
is radial.
2
2

2

When
d=3
, the scattering result of the (
NLS
) for general initial data is
still open. This is the unique reason why he restrict the radial assumption in three-dimension.

We summarize Yang’s works in the following theorem:

Theorem 1.3

(Global well-posedness and scattering for
(
NLSa
)
,
[
20
,
40
]
)
.

Let
3\leqslant d\leqslant 6
and
0>a>-\frac{(d-2)^{2}}{4}+\left(\frac{d-2}{d+2}\right)^{2}
. Suppose that
u_{0}\in\dot{H}_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
u_{0}
is radial when
d=3
. Let
u
be the corresponding maximal-lifespan solution to
(
NLSa
)
with
u|_{t=0}=u_{0}
.
If
u_{0}
obeys

(1.8)

\displaystyle E_{a}^{c}(u_{0})<E_{a}^{c}(W_{a}),\quad\|u_{0}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}<\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},

then
u
is global. Moreover,
u\in L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
and scatters in
\dot{H}_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
when
t\to\pm\infty
, i.e. there exist
u_{\pm}\in\dot{H}_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
such that

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\pm\infty}\big\|u-e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}u_{\pm}\big\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}=0.

Remark 1.4
.

In fact, Yang
[
40
]
also treated the case of
a>0
in
[
40
]
. But the energy threshold is
E_{0}^{c}(W_{0})
.

1.1.2.
Dynamic classification of threshold solution

Regarding the threshold dynamics, i.e. the energy of
u_{0}
is equal to that of ground state. Duckyaerts-Merle
[
14
]
classified all possible behaviors for
(
NLS
)
with radial
\dot{H}^{1}
data in
d=3,4,5
. Besides scattering and blowing up, the authors also constructed two special solutions
W^{\pm}
that are the “heteroclinic orbits” connecting the ground state to blowing up/scattering. It is worth mentioning that Su and Zhao
[
36
]
gave the sub-threshold dynamics classification with general
\dot{H}^{1}
initial values for
d\geq 5
. We summary their results into the following two theorems.

Theorem 1.5

(Existence of heteroclinic orbits for
(
NLS
)
,
[
14
,
26
]
)
.

Let
d\geqslant 3
. There exist radial solutions
W_{0}^{-}
with maximal life span
(-\infty,+\infty)
and
W_{0}^{+}
with maximal life span
(T_{+}(W_{0}^{+}),+\infty)
of (
NLS
) such that

(1.9)

\displaystyle E_{0}^{c}(W_{0})=E_{0}^{c}(W_{0}^{+})=E_{0}^{c}(W_{0}^{-}),\lim_{t\rightarrow+\infty}W_{0}^{\pm}(t)=W_{0}\text{ in }\dot{H}^{1},

(1.10)

\displaystyle\big\|W_{0}^{-}\big\|_{\dot{H}^{1}}<\|W_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{1}},\quad\|W_{0}^{-}\|_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}((-\infty,0])}<\infty,

(1.11)

\displaystyle\big\|W_{0}^{+}\big\|_{\dot{H}^{1}}>\|W_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{1}},\text{ and, if }d\geqslant 5,\;T_{-}(W_{0}^{+})<+\infty.

Theorem 1.6

(Threshold dynamics of
(
NLS
)
,
[
14
,
26
,
36
]
)
.

Let
d\geqslant 3
and
u_{0}\in\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
such that

(1.12)

E_{0}^{c}(u_{0})=E_{0}^{c}(W_{0})

Suppose that
u
is the solution of (
NLS
) with initial condition
u_{0}

and
I
is the maximal lifespan of
u
. Then the followings hold:

(1)

If
\|u_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{1}}<\|W_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{1}}
and
u_{0}
is radial when
d=3,4
then
I=\mathbb{R}
. Furthermore, either

u(t,x)\in\bigg\{\frac{e^{i\theta_{0}}}{\lambda_{0}^{(d-2)/2}}W_{0}^{-}\Big(\frac{t_{0}+t}{\lambda_{0}^{2}},\frac{x-x_{0}}{\lambda_{0}}\Big),\quad\frac{e^{i\theta_{0}}}{\lambda_{0}^{(d-2)/2}}\overline{W_{0}^{-}}\Big(\frac{t_{0}-t}{\lambda_{0}^{2}},\frac{x-x_{0}}{\lambda_{0}}\Big)\bigg\}

for some parameters
\theta_{0}\in\mathbb{S}^{1}
,
t_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
,
x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\lambda_{0}\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}
,
or
u
scatters in
\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
when
t\to\pm\infty
, i.e. there exist
u_{\pm}\in\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
such that

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\pm\infty}\big\|u-e^{it\Delta}u_{\pm}\big\|_{\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}=0.

(2)

If
\|u_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{1}}=\|W_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{1}}
then

u(t,x)=\frac{e^{i\theta_{0}}}{\lambda_{0}^{(d-2)/2}}W_{0}\Big(\frac{x-x_{0}}{\lambda_{0}}\Big)

for some parameters
\theta_{0}\in\mathbb{S}^{1}
,
x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\lambda_{0}\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}
,

(3)

If
\|u_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{1}}>\|W_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{1}}
, and
u_{0}\in L^{2}
is radial, then either

u(t,x)\in\bigg\{\frac{e^{i\theta_{0}}}{\lambda_{0}^{(d-2)/2}}W_{0}^{+}\Big(\frac{t_{0}+t}{\lambda_{0}^{2}},\frac{x}{\lambda_{0}}\Big),\quad\frac{e^{i\theta_{0}}}{\lambda_{0}^{(d-2)/2}}\overline{W_{0}^{+}}\Big(\frac{t_{0}-t}{\lambda_{0}^{2}},\frac{x}{\lambda_{0}}\Big)\bigg\}

for some parameters
\theta_{0}\in\mathbb{S}^{1}
,
t_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
and
\lambda_{0}\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}
,
or
I
is finite.

For the threshold dynamics of equation (
NLSa
), Yang-Zeng-Zhang
[
41
]
developed the modulation analysis near the ground state
W_{a}
and then using the concentration-compactness argument to classificate the dynamics with radial data in
d=3
and non-radial data in
d=4,5
. Now we invoke the result of
[
41
]
as the following theorem:

Theorem 1.7

(Existence of heteroclinic orbits for
(
NLSa
)
,
[
41
]
)
.

Let
d\in\{3,4,5\}
and
0>a>-\frac{(d-2)^{2}}{4}+\left(\frac{2(d-2)}{d+2}\right)^{2}
. There exist radial solutions
W_{a}^{-}
with maximal life span
(-\infty,+\infty)
and
W_{a}^{+}
with maximal life span
(T_{+}(W_{a}^{+}),+\infty)
of (
NLSa
) such that

(1.13)

\displaystyle E_{a}^{c}(W_{a})=E_{a}^{c}(W_{a}^{\pm}),\quad\lim_{t\rightarrow+\infty}W_{a}^{\pm}(t)=W_{a}\text{ in }\dot{H}_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),

(1.14)

\displaystyle\big\|W_{a}^{-}\big\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}<\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}},\quad\|W_{a}^{-}\|_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}((-\infty,0])}<\infty,

(1.15)

\displaystyle\big\|W_{a}^{+}\big\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}>\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}},\text{ and, if }d=5,\;T_{-}(W_{a}^{+})<+\infty.

Theorem 1.8

(Threshold dynamics of
(
NLSa
)
,
[
41
]
)
.

Let
d\in\{3,4,5\}
and
0>a>-\frac{(d-2)^{2}}{4}+\left(\frac{2(d-2)}{d+2}\right)^{2}
. Suppose that
u_{0}\in\dot{H}^{1}_{a}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
such that

(1.16)

E_{a}^{c}(u_{0})=E_{a}^{c}(W_{a})

We assume that
u
is the solution of (
NLSa
) with initial data
u_{0}

and
I
is the maximal lifespan of
u
. Then the following holds:

(1)

If
\|u_{0}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}<\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
and
u_{0}
is radial when
d=3
, then
I=\mathbb{R}
. Furthermore, either

u(t,x)\in\bigg\{\frac{e^{i\theta_{0}}}{\lambda_{0}^{(d-2)/2}}W_{a}^{-}\Big(\frac{t_{0}+t}{\lambda_{0}^{2}},\frac{x}{\lambda_{0}}\Big),\quad\frac{e^{i\theta_{0}}}{\lambda_{0}^{(d-2)/2}}\overline{W_{a}^{-}}\Big(\frac{t_{0}-t}{\lambda_{0}^{2}},\frac{x}{\lambda_{0}}\Big)\bigg\}

for some parameters
\theta_{0}\in\mathbb{S}^{1}
,
t_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
, and
\lambda_{0}\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}
,
or
u
scatters in
\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
when
t\to\pm\infty
, i.e. there exist
u_{\pm}\in\dot{H}_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
such that

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\pm\infty}\big\|u-e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}u_{\pm}\big\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}=0.

(2)

If
\|u_{0}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}=\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
, then

u(t,x)=\frac{e^{i\theta_{0}}}{\lambda_{0}^{(d-2)/2}}W_{a}\Big(\frac{x}{\lambda_{0}}\Big)

for some parameters
\theta_{0}\in\mathbb{S}^{1}
and
\lambda_{0}\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}
.

(3)

If
\|u_{0}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}>\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
, and
u_{0}\in L^{2}
is radial, then either

u(t,x)\in\bigg\{\frac{e^{i\theta_{0}}}{\lambda_{0}^{(d-2)/2}}W_{a}^{+}\Big(\frac{t_{0}+t}{\lambda_{0}^{2}},\frac{x}{\lambda_{0}}\Big),\quad\frac{e^{i\theta_{0}}}{\lambda_{0}^{(d-2)/2}}\overline{W_{a}^{+}}\Big(\frac{t_{0}-t}{\lambda_{0}^{2}},\frac{x}{\lambda_{0}}\Big)\bigg\}

for some parameters
\theta_{0}\in\mathbb{S}^{1}
,
t_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
and
\lambda_{0}\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}
,
or
I
is finite.

For the threshold dynamics of the energy-critical focusing-defocusing perturbed equation, i.e.
E_{0}(u_{0})=E_{0}^{c}(W_{0})
, Ardila-Murphy-Zheng
[
3
]
solved the scattering/blow-up dichotomy for radial
H^{1}
initial data in three dimension. Compared to the energy-critical threshold problem, the heteroclinic orbits no longer exist. However, as pointed out by
[
14
]
, if one considers the non-homogeneous space
H^{1}
, heteroclinic orbits do not exist naturally.

Theorem 1.9

(Threshold dynamics of
(
CNLS
)
,
[
3
]
)
.

Let
u_{0}\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
be radial. Suppose that
u
is the corresponding maximal-lifespan solution to (
CNLS
) with
u|_{t=0}=u_{0}
.

(1)

If
u_{0}
obeys

E_{0}(u_{0})=E_{0}^{c}(W_{0})\quad\text{and}\quad\|u_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}<\|W_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})},

then
u
is global,
u\in L_{t,x}^{10}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})
, and scatters in
{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
when
t\to\pm\infty
, i.e. there exist
u_{\pm}\in\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
such that

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\pm\infty}\big\|u-e^{it\Delta}u_{\pm}\big\|_{{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}=0.

(2)

If
u_{0}
is radial and obeys

E_{0}(u_{0})=E_{0}^{c}(W_{0})\quad\text{and}\quad\|u_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}>\|W_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})},

then
u
blows up in both time directions in finite time.

Remark 1.10
.

We expect that Theorem
1.9
holds in higher-dimensions
d\geqslant 4
without radial assumption. We shall study this issue in the future work.

In this paper, we extend Theorem
1.2
and
1.9
to the case of inverse-square potential. Compared to these theorems(cf. Theorem
1.2
and
1.9
), the new ingredient in our paper is that the additional potential breaks down the translation-invariance with respect to the spatial variable. It needs more delicate analysis in the construction of nonlinear profile decomposition.

1.2.
Main results and discussion

In this section, we present our main results and give the outline of the proof.

Our first result is to give the scattering/blow-up dichotomy under the energy threshold, which extend Theorem
1.2
(
a=0
) to the case with inverse-square potential. We state it as the following theorem.

Theorem 1.11

(Sub-threshold solution)
.

Let
d\in\{3,4,5\}
and
-\tfrac{(d-2)^{2}}{4}+\big(\tfrac{d-2}{d+2}\big)^{2}<a<0
. Suppose that
u_{0}\in H_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and let
u
be the corresponding maximal-lifespan solution to
(
CNLSa
)
with
u|_{t=0}=u_{0}
.

(i)

Scattering part:
If
u_{0}
obeys

(1.17)

\displaystyle E_{a}(u_{0})<E_{a}^{c}(W_{a}),\quad\|u_{0}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}<\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},

and we further assume that
u_{0}
is radial when
d=3
,
then
u
is global. Moreover,
u\in L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
and scatters in
{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
when
t\to\pm\infty
, i.e. there exist
u_{\pm}\in\dot{H}_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
such that

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\pm\infty}\big\|u-e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}u_{\pm}\big\|_{{H}_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}=0.

(ii)

Blow-up part:
If
u_{0}
is radial and obeys

(1.18)

\displaystyle E_{a}(u_{0})<E_{a}^{c}(W_{a}),\quad\|u_{0}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}>\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}},

then
u
blow up in both time directions in finite time.

Our second result is to give the scattering/blow-up dichotomy at the energy threshold, which extend Theorem
1.9
(
a=0
) to the case with inverse-square potential. We state it as the following theorem.

Theorem 1.12

(Threshold solution)
.

Let
d\in\{3,4,5\}
and
-\frac{(d-2)^{2}}{4}+\left(\frac{2(d-2)}{d+2}\right)^{2}<a<0
. Suppose that
u_{0}\in H_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and let
u
be the corresponding maximal-lifespan solution to
(
CNLSa
)
with
u|_{t=0}=u_{0}
.

(i)

If
u_{0}
obeys

(1.19)

\displaystyle E_{a}(u_{0})=E_{a}^{c}(W_{a}),\quad\|u_{0}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}<\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}},

and we further assume that
u_{0}
is radial when
d=3
, then
u
is global. Moreover,
u\in L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
and scatters in
{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
when
t\to\pm\infty
, i.e. there exist
u_{\pm}\in\dot{H}_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
such that

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\pm\infty}\big\|u-e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}u_{\pm}\big\|_{{H}_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}=0.

(ii)

If
u_{0}
is radial and obeys

(1.20)

\displaystyle E_{a}(u_{0})=E_{a}^{c}(W_{a}),\quad\|u_{0}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}>\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}},

then
u
blows up in both time direction in finite time.

Remark 1.13
.

Compared to Theorem
1.11
, we further need that
-\left(\frac{d-2}{2}\right)^{2}+\left(\frac{2(d-2)}{d+2}\right)^{2}<a<0
in Theorem
1.12
where such restriction is to ensure better regularity of the ground state
W_{a}(x)
. We refer to
[
41
]
for details.

In the remainder of this introduction, we outline the structure of the paper and the arguments used to prove Theorem
1.11
and
1.12
.

In Section
2
, we revisit some harmonic analysis tools associated to the operator
\mathcal{L}_{a}
, including the followings: the equivalence of Sobolev spaces and the Littlewood-Paley theory along with Bernstein inequalities; Strichartz estimates; Kato’s local smoothing estimates. Next, we study the local theory for equation
(
CNLSa
)
, which contains the local well-posedness, stability lemma, small-data scattering and the persistence of regularity. We also show the linear profile decomposition for bounded sequence
\{f_{n}\}\subset H_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. We end up this section by establishing the local virial identity, which is important in both proofs of Theorem
1.11
and
1.12
.

In Section
3
, we first collect the sharp Sobolev embedding associated to
\mathcal{L}_{a}
. By using this sharp inequality, we establish the energy trapping lemma, which is crucial in the proof of Theorem
1.11
. We associate the energy threshold
E_{a}^{c}(W_{a})
to a following minimization problem(see
(
3.8
)
for details). The scattering and blow-up regimes can be rewritten to the two regions associated to this minimization problem. Then we establish the energy trapping lemma for these two regions.

In Section
4
, we give the modulation analysis around the ground state
W_{a}
, which is essential in the proof of Theorem
1.12
(1). We emphasize that the mass conservation plays an important role in establishing the modulation analysis.

In Section
5
and Section
6
, we give the proof of the blow up part in Theorem
1.11
and
1.12
throughout the local virial argument. For the threshold case, we need the additional modulation analysis. These tools are already established in previous sections.

In Section
7
, we construct solutions to (
CNLSa
) associated to profiles
\phi_{n}
living either at small length scales (i.e. in the regime
\lambda_{n}\to 0
) or far from the origin relative to their length scale (i.e. in the regime
|\tfrac{x_{n}}{\lambda_{n}}|\to\infty
) or both. These solutions are also called the nonlinear profiles. The challenge lies in the fact that the translation and scaling symmetries in (
CNLSa
) are broken by the potential and the nonlinearity, respectively. In particular, we must consider several limiting regimes and use approximation by the suitable underlying model in each case. Using the nonlinear profiles, we can prove that the solution
u(t)
is pre-compact in
H_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. In Section
8
, we preclude the possibility of minimal blow-up solution. We use the local virial argument to preclude the infinite-time blow-up and the continuous method to preclude the possibility of finite-time blow-up.

In Section
9
, we demonstrate that the failure of Theorem
1.12
(i) implies the existence of a non-scattering solution
u
to
(
CNLSa
)
satisfying
(
9.2
)
such that

\displaystyle\bigg\{\lambda(t)^{-\frac{d-2}{2}}u\left(t,\frac{x}{\lambda(t)}\right):t\in[0,T_{max})\bigg\}\mbox{ is pre-compact in }\dot{H}_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),

where
\lambda(t)\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}
is the scaling parameter.
In Section
10
, we preclude the possibility of finite-time blow-up and infinite-time blow-up separately.
In the case of finite-time blowup, we find that the solution must move to arbitrarily small spatial scales as one approaches the finite blowup time via the local virial identity. In this regime, we are able to prove that the
L^{2}
norm of the solution is identically zero, which leads to a contradiction.

In the case of infinite-time blowup, we briefly give the sketch of the proof here. Using the standard localized virial argument, we can find a sequence of times along which the solution approaches the orbit of the ground state. This is made precise using the quantity

\delta(t)=\big|\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{2}-\|u(t)\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{2}\big|.

We can further show that if
\delta(t_{n})\to 0
for some
t_{n}\to\infty
, then we have
\lambda(t_{n})\rightarrow\infty
, which implies that the solution is in small spacial scales. However, the modulation analysis indicates that the difference in spatial scale may be controlled by the integral of
\delta(t)
. We control such time integrals by the value of
\delta(\cdot)
by using the virial estimates. It follows that the solution cannot move to arbitrarily small scales, and hence we obtain a contradiction.

1.3.
Notations

We write
A\lesssim B
or
A=O(B)
when
A\leq CB
for some
C>0
. If
A\lesssim B\lesssim A
, we write
A\sim B
. We also write
A\ll 1
in the sense that there exists
\varepsilon>0
sufficiently small such that
A<\varepsilon
.

2.
Preliminaries

In this section, we collect the harmonic analysis tools associated with
\mathcal{L}_{a}
, the local well-posedness/stablity result, the linear profile decomposition of the bounded sequence in
{H}_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and local virial identities.

For a function

u:I\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
we use the notation

\|u\|_{L_{t}^{q}L^{r}_{x}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}=\|\|u(t)\|_{L^{r}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|_{L^{q}_{t}(I)}

with
1\leq q\leq r\leq\infty
. When
q=r
we abbreviate
L_{t}^{q}L^{r}_{x}
by
L_{t,x}^{q}
.

We define the Sobolev spaces associated with
\mathcal{L}_{a}
via

\|f\|_{\dot{H}^{s,r}_{a}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}=\|(\mathcal{L}_{a})^{\frac{s}{2}}f\|_{L^{r}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\quad\text{and}\quad\|f\|_{{H}^{s,r}_{a}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}=\|(1+\mathcal{L}_{a})^{\frac{s}{2}}f\|_{L^{r}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

We abbreviate
\dot{H}^{s}_{a}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=\dot{H}^{s,2}_{a}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
{H}^{s}_{a}(\mathbb{R}^{d})={H}^{s,2}_{a}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

Given a function
u\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, we defined the mass quantity

M(u)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|u|^{2}\,dx.

If
u\in\dot{H}_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, we define four energy quantities

(2.1)

\displaystyle E_{0}(u)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\bigg(\frac{1}{2}|\nabla u|^{2}+\frac{d-1}{2d+2}|u|^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}-\frac{d-2}{2d}|u|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}\bigg)\,dx,

(2.2)

\displaystyle E_{a}(u)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\bigg(\frac{1}{2}|\nabla u|^{2}+\frac{a}{2|x|^{2}}|u|^{2}+\frac{d-1}{2d+2}|u|^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}-\frac{d-2}{2d}|u|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}\bigg)\,dx,

(2.3)

\displaystyle E_{a}^{c}(u)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\bigg(\frac{1}{2}|\nabla u|^{2}+\frac{a}{2|x|^{2}}|u|^{2}-\frac{d-2}{2d}|u|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}\bigg)\,dx,

(2.4)

\displaystyle E_{0}^{c}(u)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\bigg(\frac{1}{2}|\nabla u|^{2}-\frac{d-2}{2d}|u|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}\bigg)\,dx.

As mentioned in the introduction,
(
2.3
)
and
(
2.4
)
are conserved energies for focusing energy-critical problem
(
NLSa
)
and
(
NLS
)
while
(
2.1
)
and
(
2.2
)
are conserved energies for the focusing-defocusing NLS with combined-power nonlinearity
(
CNLS
)
and
(
CNLSa
)
.

We summarize the equivalence of Sobolev spaces in the following lemma, which was first proved in
[
21
]
.

Lemma 2.1
.

Fix
d\geq 3
,
a\geq-\left(\frac{d-2}{2}\right)^{2}
and
0<s<2
. We let

\displaystyle\sigma=\frac{d-2}{2}-\big[\big(\frac{d-2}{2}\big)^{2}+a\big]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

If
1<p<\infty
satisfies

\frac{\sigma+s}{d}<\frac{1}{p}<\min\left\{1,\frac{d-\sigma}{d}\right\}
, then

\|(-\Delta)^{\frac{s}{2}}f\|_{L_{x}^{p}}\lesssim_{d,p,s}\|\mathcal{L}_{a}^{\frac{s}{2}}f\|_{L_{x}^{p}}\quad\text{for $f\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\left\{{0}\right\})$}.

If
\max\left\{\frac{s}{d},\frac{\sigma}{d}\right\}<\frac{1}{p}<\min\left\{1,\frac{d-\sigma}{d}\right\}
, then

\|\mathcal{L}_{a}^{\frac{s}{2}}f\|_{L_{x}^{p}}\lesssim_{d,p,s}\|(-\Delta)^{\frac{s}{2}}f\|_{L_{x}^{p}}\quad\text{for $f\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\left\{{0}\right\})$}.

Especially, if
a>-\frac{(d-2)^{2}}{4}+\left(\frac{d-2}{d+2}\right)^{2}
, then

\|\mathcal{L}_{a}^{\frac{1}{2}}f\|_{L_{x}^{p}}\sim\|(-\Delta)^{\frac{1}{2}}f\|_{L_{x}^{p}}\quad\text{for all $\tfrac{2d}{d+2}\leq p\leq\tfrac{2d(d+2)}{d^{2}+4}$}.

We will also need the Littlewood–Paley theory adapted to
\mathcal{L}_{a}
. Let
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
be a smooth positive radial function satisfying

\phi(x)=1
if
|x|\leq 1
and
\phi(x)=0
if
|x|\geq\frac{11}{10}
. For
N\in 2^{\mathbb{Z}}
, we define

\phi_{N}(x):=\phi(x/N)\quad\text{and}\quad\psi_{N}(x)=\phi_{N}(x)-\phi_{N/2}(x).

We define the Littlewood-Paley projector as

\displaystyle f_{\leq N}

\displaystyle:=P^{a}_{\leq N}f:=\phi_{N}(\sqrt{\mathcal{L}_{a}})f,\quad f_{N}:=P^{a}_{N}f:=\psi_{N}(\sqrt{\mathcal{L}_{a}})f,

\displaystyle\quad\text{and}\quad f_{>N}:=P^{a}_{>N}f:=(I-P^{a}_{\leq N})f

The Littlewood-Paley projectors satisfy the following Bernstein estimates.

Lemma 2.2

(Bernstein inequalities,
[
21
]
)
.

Let
s\in\mathbb{R}
. For
q_{0}<q\leq r<q_{0}^{\prime}:=\frac{\sigma}{d}
and
f:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{C}
we have

\displaystyle\|P^{a}_{N}f\|_{L_{x}^{r}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{\frac{d}{q}-\frac{d}{r}}\|P^{a}_{N}f\|_{L_{x}^{q}},

\displaystyle\|P^{a}_{\geq N}f\|_{L_{x}^{r}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{-s}\|\mathcal{L}_{a}^{\frac{s}{2}}P^{a}_{\geq N}f\|_{L_{x}^{r}},

\displaystyle N^{s}\|P^{a}_{N}f\|_{L_{x}^{r}}

\displaystyle\sim\|\mathcal{L}^{\frac{s}{2}}_{a}P^{a}_{N}f\|_{L_{x}^{r}}.

We have the following global-in-time Strichartz estimates.

Lemma 2.3

(Strichartz estimates,
[
7
]
)
.

Fix
a>-\frac{(d-2)^{2}}{4}
. Then the solution
u
of
(i\partial_{t}-\mathcal{L}_{a})u=F
on an interval
t_{0}\in I

satisfying

\|u\|_{L_{t}^{q}L^{r}_{x}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\|u(t_{0})\|_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\|F\|_{L_{t}^{\tilde{q}^{\prime}}L^{\tilde{r}^{\prime}}_{x}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{d})},

where
2\leq\tilde{q},\tilde{r}\leq\infty
with

\frac{2}{q}+\frac{d}{r}=\frac{2}{\tilde{q}}+\frac{d}{\tilde{r}}=\frac{d}{2}
.

Remark 2.4
.

Bouclet-Mizutani
[
4
]
proved the double end-point case, i.e.
q=\tilde{q}=2
.

We also collect the following Kato’s local smoothing result for the linear Schrödinger group
e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}
, see
[
20
]
for the proof.

Lemma 2.5

(Local smoothing,
[
20
]
)
.

Let
a>-\left(\frac{d-2}{4}\right)^{2}
and
w=e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}w_{0}
, then we have

(2.5)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{|\nabla w(t,x)|^{2}}{R\langle R^{-1}x\rangle^{3}}+\frac{|w(t,x)|^{2}}{R|x|^{2}}dxdt\lesssim\|w_{0}\|_{2}\|\nabla w_{0}\|_{2}+R^{-1}\|w_{0}\|_{2}^{2},

(2.6)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{|x-z|\leq R}\frac{1}{R}|\nabla w(t,x)|^{2}dxdt\lesssim\|w_{0}\|_{2}\|\nabla w_{0}\|_{2}+R^{-1}\|w_{0}\|_{2}^{2}

uniformly for
z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

Finally, we have the following estimate of the linear flow
e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}f
on
compact domains. This estimate is used to control the error term in an approximate solution in the construction of minimal blowup solution(see (
7.49
)).

Lemma 2.6
.

Let
a>-\frac{(d-2)^{2}}{4}+\left(\frac{d-2}{d+2}\right)^{2}
and
\phi\in\dot{H}^{1}_{a}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, then we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}\phi\|_{L_{t,x}^{2}([\tau-T,\tau+T]\times\{|x-z|\leq R\})}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\frac{d^{2}+8d-4}{2(d+2)(3d-2)}}R^{\frac{2d(d-2)}{(3d-2)(d+2)}}\|\phi\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{\frac{2d}{3d-2}}\|e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}\phi\|_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{\frac{d-2}{3d-2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 8.61108pt+T^{\frac{d^{2}+8d-4}{8(d-1)(d+2)}}R^{\frac{3d^{2}-4d-4}{4(d-1)(d+2)}}\|\phi\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{\frac{3d-2}{4(d-1)}}\|e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}\phi\|_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{\frac{d-2}{4(d-1)}}.

uniformly in
\phi
and the parameters
R
,
T>0
,
z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\tau\in\mathbb{R}
and
R,T>0
.

Proof.

We write

e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}\phi=e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}(P_{\leq N}^{a}\phi+P_{>N}^{a}\phi).

First, we estimate the low frequency part. By the equivalence of Sobolev norms, Hölder’s inequality, Bernstein’s inequality and Strichartz estimate, we have

\displaystyle\hskip 12.91663pt\big\|\nabla e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}P_{\leq N}^{a}\phi\big\|_{L_{t,x}^{2}([\tau-T,\tau+T]\times\{|x-z|\leq R\})}

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\frac{d^{2}+8d-4}{4d(d+2)}}R^{\frac{d-2}{d+2}}\left\|\nabla e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}P_{\leq N}^{a}\phi\right\|_{L_{t}^{\frac{4d(d+2)}{(d-2)^{2}}}L_{x}^{\frac{2d(d+2)}{d^{2}+4}}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\frac{d^{2}+8d-4}{4d(d+2)}}R^{\frac{d-2}{d+2}}N^{\frac{d-2}{2d}}\|(\mathcal{L}_{a})^{\frac{d+2}{4d}}e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}P_{\leq N}^{a}\phi\|_{L_{t}^{\frac{4d(d+2)}{(d-2)^{2}}}L_{x}^{\frac{2d(d+2)}{d^{2}+4}}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\frac{d^{2}+8d-4}{4d(d+2)}}R^{\frac{d-2}{d+2}}N^{\frac{d-2}{2d}}\|e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}\phi\|_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{\frac{d-2}{2d}}\|(\mathcal{L}_{a})^{\frac{1}{2}}e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}\phi\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L_{x}^{2}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{\frac{d+2}{2d}}

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\frac{d^{2}+8d-4}{4d(d+2)}}R^{\frac{d-2}{d+2}}N^{\frac{d-2}{2d}}\|e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}\phi\|_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{\frac{d-2}{2d}}\|\phi\|_{\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{\frac{d+2}{2d}}.

For the high frequency, the local smoothing estimate
(
2.6
)
and Bernstein’s inequality imply

\displaystyle\|\nabla e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}P_{>N}^{a}\phi\|_{L_{t,x}^{2}([\tau-T,\tau+T]\times\{|x-z|\leq R\}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim R\|P_{>N}^{a}\phi\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\|\nabla P_{>N}^{a}\phi\|_{L_{x}^{2}}+\|P_{>N}^{a}\phi\|_{L_{x}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim(RN^{-1}+N^{-2})\|\phi\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{2}.

Then optimizing the choice of
N
, we have

\displaystyle\hskip 21.52771pt\|\nabla e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}\phi\|_{L_{t,x}^{2}([\tau-T,\tau+T]\times\{|x-z|\leq R\})}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\frac{d^{2}+8d-4}{2(d+2)(3d-2)}}R^{\frac{2d(d-2)}{(3d-2)(d+2)}}\|\phi\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{\frac{2d}{3d-2}}\|e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}\phi\|_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{\frac{d-2}{3d-2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 8.61108pt+T^{\frac{d^{2}+8d-4}{8(d-1)(d+2)}}R^{\frac{3d^{2}-4d-4}{4(d-1)(d+2)}}\|\phi\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{\frac{3d-2}{4(d-1)}}\|e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}\phi\|_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{\frac{d-2}{4(d-1)}}.

∎

In the rest of the paper, we shall use the notation:

S^{s}_{a}(I)=L^{q}_{t}H^{s,r}_{a}\cap C^{0}_{t}H^{s}_{a}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{d})\quad\text{and}\quad\dot{S}^{s}_{a}(I)=L^{q}_{t}\dot{H}^{s,r}_{a}\cap C_{t}^{0}\dot{H}^{s}_{a}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{d}).

where
s\in\{0,1\}
and
(q,r)
satisfies

\displaystyle\frac{2}{q}=d\bigg(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{r}\bigg),\quad 2\leqslant q,r\leqslant\infty.

2.1.
Local well-posedness and stability theory

In this subsection, we present the local well-posedness and stability theory for (
CNLSa
) in the space
H_{a}^{1}
. First, we give the local well-posedness result. When
a=0
, we refer to Tao-Visan-Zhang
[
38
]
for
d\geqslant 3
. When
a\neq 0
, we refer to Ardila-Murphy
[
2
]
for the three-dimensional case.

Before presenting the local well-posedness, we first give some definitions of the function space which will be used in the proof of local theory.

Definition 2.7

(Function Spaces)
.

We define the following spaces

\displaystyle\dot{X}_{a}^{0}(I)=L_{t}^{\gamma_{1}}L_{x}^{\rho_{1}}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{d})\cap L_{t}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}L_{x}^{\frac{2d(d+2)}{d^{2}+4}}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{d})\cap L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d}}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{d}),

where
\gamma_{1}=\frac{4(p_{1}+2)}{p_{1}(d-2)}
and
\rho_{1}=\frac{d(p_{1}+2)}{d+p_{1}}
and
p_{1}=\frac{d+3}{d-1}
.

\displaystyle\dot{X}_{a}^{1}(I)=\left\{u:\sqrt{\mathcal{L}_{a}}u\in\dot{X}_{a}^{0}(I)\right\},\quad X_{a}^{1}(I)=\dot{X}_{a}^{0}(I)\cap\dot{X}_{a}^{1}(I).

Now we are in position to give the local well-posedness result. The proof is very close to Tao-Visan-Zhang
[
38
]
. The one thing we need to be cautious is that in the inverse-square setting, we must ensure that the exponent is in the range of Lemma
2.1
. The
L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d}}
norm can be interpolated by
L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}
and
L_{t}^{\infty}L_{x}^{2}
. Then using Sobolev embedding, we can bound
L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}
by
L_{t}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}\dot{H}_{a}^{1,\frac{2d(d+2)}{d^{2}+4}}
, which is the endpoint of Lemma
2.1
.

Theorem 2.8

(Local well-posedness)
.

Given
a>-\left(\frac{d-2}{2}\right)^{2}+\left(\frac{d-2}{d+2}\right)^{2}
and
u_{0}\in H_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Then for every
T>0
, there exists
\eta=\eta(T)
such that if

(2.7)

\displaystyle\big\|e^{it\mathcal{L}_{a}}u_{0}\big\|_{X_{a}^{1}([-T,T])}\leqslant\eta,

then
(
CNLSa
)
admits a unique strong
H_{a}^{1}
-solution on time interval
[-T,T]
. Let
(T_{min},T_{max})
be the maximal lifespan of
u
. Then
u\in\dot{S}_{a}^{1}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
for every compact time interval
I\subset(T_{min},T_{max})
and the following properties hold

(1)

If
T_{max}<+\infty
, then

\displaystyle\big\|u\big\|_{{X}_{a}^{1}((0,T_{max}))}=+\infty.

Similarly, if
T_{min}>-\infty
, then

\displaystyle\big\|u\big\|_{{X}_{a}^{1}((T_{min},0))}=+\infty.

(2)

If
T_{max}=\infty
and
T_{min}=-\infty
with

\displaystyle\|u\|_{X_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R})}<\infty,

then
u
scatters in
H_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

To obtain global well-posedness in
H_{a}^{1}
, we will need a following results establishing scattering in
H_{a}^{1}
for sufficiently small initial data, along with a persistence of regularity result and a stability result. All of these are analogues of results in
[
24
, Section 6]
. As the proofs rely primarily on Strichartz estimates, which are readily available in the setting of the inverse-square potential, we omit them here.

Proposition 2.9

(Small data scattering)
.

Let
d\in\{3,4,5\}
,
a>-\left(\frac{d-2}{2}\right)^{2}+\left(\frac{d-2}{d+2}\right)^{2}
and
u_{0}\in H_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. There exists
\delta>0
such that if
\|u_{0}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}<\delta
, then the corresponding solution
u
of (
CNLSa
) is global and scatters, with

\|u\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leqslant C({M(u_{0})})\|\sqrt{\mathcal{L}_{a}}u_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Lemma 2.10

(Persistence of regularity)
.

Suppose that
u:\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
is a solution to (
CNLSa
) such that

S(u):=\|u\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}<\infty
.
Then for
t_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
we have

(2.8)

\displaystyle\|u\|_{\dot{S}_{a}^{0}(I)}

\displaystyle\leq C(S(u),M(u_{0}))\|u(t_{0})\|_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},

(2.9)

\displaystyle\|u\|_{\dot{S}_{a}^{1}(I)}

\displaystyle\leq C(S(u),M(u_{0}))\|u(t_{0})\|_{\dot{H}^{1}_{a}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Proof.

Indeed, first note that by Strichartz and Hölder

(2.10)

\begin{split}\|u\|_{S_{a}^{0}(I)}&\lesssim\|u(t_{0})\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|u\|^{\frac{4}{d-2}}_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}}\|u\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}_{x}}+\|u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L^{2}_{x}}^{\frac{2}{d-1}}\|u\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}}^{\frac{2}{d-1}}\|u\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}_{x}}\\
&\lesssim\|u(t_{0})\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+(\|u\|^{\frac{4}{d-2}}_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}}+[M(u)]^{\frac{1}{d-1}}\|u\|^{\frac{4}{d-2}}_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}})\|u\|_{S_{a}^{0}(I)}\end{split}

for any interval
t_{0}\in I
, where space-time norms are over
I\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Since
\|u\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}}<\infty
, a standard bootstrap argument implies estimate
(
2.8
)
.
Similarly, we can obtain that

\displaystyle\big\|\sqrt{\mathcal{L}_{a}}(|u|^{\frac{4}{d-1}}u)\big\|_{L_{t}^{\tfrac{d^{2}+d+2}{2(d-1)(d+2)}}L_{x}^{\tfrac{d^{3}+3d^{2}-12}{2d(d-1)(d+2)}}}\lesssim\|u\|_{L_{t}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}\dot{H}_{a}^{1,\frac{2d(d+2)}{d^{2}+4}}}\|u\|_{L_{t}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d}}L_{x}^{\frac{2d(d+2)}{d^{2}-d-2}}}^{\frac{4}{d-1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u\|_{L_{t}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}\dot{H}_{a}^{1,\frac{2d(d+2)}{d^{2}+4}}}\|u\|_{L_{t}^{2}L_{x}^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}}^{\frac{2}{d-1}}\|u\|_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}}^{\frac{2}{d-1}}

and

\displaystyle\big\|\sqrt{\mathcal{L}_{a}}(|u|^{\frac{4}{d-2}}u)\big\|_{L_{t}^{2}L_{x}^{\frac{2d}{d+2}}}\lesssim\|u\|_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}}^{\frac{4}{d-2}}\|u\|_{L_{t}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}\dot{H}_{a}^{1,\frac{2d(d+2)}{d^{2}+4}}}.

By the Strichartz estimates and Hölder’s inequality, we obtain
(
2.9
)
.
∎

Using the standard bootstrap argument as in
[
2
]
, we have the following stability result:

Lemma 2.11

(Stability)
.

Let
d\in\{3,4,5\}
. Fix
a>-\left(\frac{d-2}{2}\right)^{2}+\left(\frac{d-2}{d+2}\right)^{2}
. Let
I\subset\mathbb{R}
be a time interval containing
t_{0}
and let
\tilde{u}
satisfy

(i\partial_{t}-\mathcal{L}_{a})\tilde{u}=-|\tilde{u}|^{\frac{4}{d-2}}\tilde{u}+|\tilde{u}|^{\frac{4}{d-1}}\tilde{u}+e,\quad\tilde{u}(t_{0})=\tilde{u}_{0}

on
I\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
for some
e:I\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
. Assume the conditions

\|\tilde{u}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H^{1}_{a}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq E\quad\text{and}\quad\|\tilde{u}\|_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\|\tilde{u}\|_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-1}}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq L

for some
E
,
L>0
. Let
t_{0}\in I
and
u_{0}\in H_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
such that
\|u_{0}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\leq M

for some positive constant
M
.
Assume also the smallness conditions

\|u_{0}-\tilde{u}_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{1}_{a}}\leq{\varepsilon}\quad\text{and}\quad\|\sqrt{\mathcal{L}_{a}}e\|_{N(I)}\leq{\varepsilon},

for some
0<{\varepsilon}<{\varepsilon}_{0}={\varepsilon}_{0}(\mbox{A,L,M})>0
, where

N(I)=L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{d})+L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d+4}}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{d})+L_{t}^{2}L_{x}^{\frac{2d}{d+2}}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{d}).

Then there exists a unique global solution
u
to Cauchy problem (
CNLSa
) with initial data
u_{0}
at the time
t=t_{0}
satisfying

\|u-\tilde{u}\|_{\dot{S}_{a}^{1}(I)}\leq C(E,L,M){\varepsilon}\quad\text{and}\quad\|u\|_{\dot{S}_{a}^{1}(I)}\leq C(E,L,M).

2.2.
Linear profile decomposition

In this subsection, we give the linear profile decomposition associated to the propagator
e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}
. In fact, the result follows by combining the techniques of
[
20
,
40
]
, which described the bubble decomposition of the Schrödinger group
e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}f
where
f\in\dot{H}_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, with those of
[
24
,
28
]
, which developed the bubble decomposition of the Schrödinger group without potential
e^{it\Delta}f
with initial data
f\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Thus, we will focus on stating the main results and providing suitable references to the analogous results in the references just mentioned.

First, given a sequence
\left\{x_{n}\right\}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we define

(2.11)

\mathcal{L}^{n}_{a}:=-\Delta+\tfrac{a}{|x+x_{n}|^{2}}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{L}^{\infty}_{a}:=\begin{cases}-\Delta+\tfrac{a}{|x+x_{\infty}|^{2}}&\text{if $x_{n}\to x_{\infty}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}$},\\
-\Delta&\text{if $|x_{n}|\to\infty$}.\end{cases}

In particular,
\mathcal{L}_{a}[\phi(x-x_{n})]=[\mathcal{L}^{n}_{a}\phi](x-x_{n})
, and for any
x_{n}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
N_{n}>0
,

N^{\frac{d-2}{2}}_{n}e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}[\phi(N_{n}x-x_{n})]=N^{\frac{d-2}{2}}_{n}[e^{-iN^{2}_{n}t\mathcal{L}^{n}_{a}}\phi](N_{n}x-y_{n}).

To establish the linear profile decomposition associated to
e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}
, we need the following results related to the convergence of the operator
\mathcal{L}_{a}^{n}
to
\mathcal{L}_{a}^{\infty}
, which was initially proved in
[
20
,
23
]
:

Lemma 2.12
.

Fix
a>-\frac{(d-2)^{2}}{4}
.

•

If
t_{n}\to t_{\infty}\in\mathbb{R}
and
\left\{x_{n}\right\}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
satisfies
x_{n}\to x_{\infty}
or
|x_{n}|\to\infty
, then

(2.12)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\|\mathcal{L}^{n}_{a}\psi-\mathcal{L}^{\infty}_{a}\psi\|_{\dot{H}^{-1}_{x}}=0\quad\text{for all $\psi\in\dot{H}^{1}_{x}$},

(2.13)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\|(e^{-it_{n}\mathcal{L}^{n}_{a}}-e^{-it_{\infty}\mathcal{L}^{\infty}_{a}})\psi\|_{\dot{H}^{-1}_{x}}=0\quad\text{for all $\psi\in\dot{H}^{-1}_{x}$},

(2.14)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\|[\sqrt{\mathcal{L}^{n}_{a}}-\sqrt{\mathcal{L}^{\infty}_{a}}]\psi\|_{L^{2}_{x}}=0\quad\text{for all $\psi\in\dot{H}^{1}_{x}$}.

If
\frac{2}{q}+\frac{d}{r}=\frac{d}{2}
with
2<q\leq\infty
, then we have

(2.15)

\lim_{n\to\infty}\|(e^{-it\mathcal{L}^{n}_{a}}-e^{-it\mathcal{L}^{\infty}_{a}})\psi\|_{L^{q}_{t}L^{r}_{x}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}=0\quad\text{for all $\psi\in L^{2}_{x}$}.

Finally, if
x_{\infty}\neq 0
, then for any
t>0
,

(2.16)

\lim_{n\to\infty}\|(e^{-it\mathcal{L}^{n}_{a}}-e^{-it\mathcal{L}^{\infty}_{a}})\delta_{0}\|_{H^{-1}}=0.

•

Given
\psi\in\dot{H}^{1}_{x}
,
t_{n}\to\pm\infty
and any sequence

\left\{x_{n}\right\}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we have

(2.17)

\lim_{n\to\infty}\|e^{-it_{n}\mathcal{L}^{n}_{a}}\psi\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}_{x}}=0.

Moreover, if
\psi\in{H}^{1}_{x}
, then

(2.18)

\lim_{n\to\infty}\|e^{-it_{n}\mathcal{L}^{n}_{a}}\psi\|_{L^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}_{x}}=0.

•

Finally, fix
a>-\left(\frac{d-2}{4}\right)^{2}
. Then for any sequence
\{x_{n}\}
,

(2.19)

\lim_{n\to\infty}\|(e^{-it\mathcal{L}^{n}_{a}}-e^{-it\mathcal{L}^{\infty}_{a}})\psi\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}=0\quad\text{for all $\psi\in\dot{H}^{1}_{x}$}.

The linear profile decomposition is stated as follows:

Theorem 2.13

(Linear profile decomposition)
.

Let
d\geqslant 3
and
a>-\frac{(d-2)^{2}}{4}
.
Suppose that
\left\{f_{n}\right\}
is a bounded sequence in
H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Then, up to subsequence, there exist

J^{*}\in\left\{0,1,2,\ldots\right\}\cup\left\{\infty\right\}
, non-zero profiles

\{\phi^{j}\}^{J^{*}}_{j=1}\subset\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and parameters

\left\{\lambda^{j}_{n}\right\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subset(0,1],\quad\left\{t^{j}_{n}\right\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subset\mathbb{R}\quad\text{and}\quad\left\{x^{j}_{n}\right\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}

so that for each finite
1\leq J\leq J^{*}
, we have the decomposition

(2.20)

f_{n}=\sum^{J}_{j=1}\phi_{n}^{j}+W^{J}_{n},

where

(2.21)

\phi_{n}^{j}(x):=\begin{cases}[e^{-it^{j}_{n}\mathcal{L}^{n_{j}}_{a}}\phi^{j}](x-x^{j}_{n}),&\mbox{if $\lambda^{j}_{n}\equiv 1$},\\
(\lambda^{j}_{n})^{-\frac{d-2}{2}}[e^{-it^{j}_{n}\mathcal{L}^{n_{j}}_{a}}P^{a}_{\geq(\lambda^{j}_{n})^{\theta}}\phi^{j}]\left(\frac{x-x^{j}_{n}}{\lambda^{j}_{n}}\right),&\mbox{if $\lambda^{j}_{n}\rightarrow 0$},\end{cases}

for some
0<\theta<1

(
with
\mathcal{L}^{n_{j}}_{a}
as in (
2.11
) corresponding to sequence

\{\tfrac{x^{j}_{n}}{\lambda^{j}_{n}}\})
, satisfying

•

\lambda^{j}_{n}\equiv 1
or
\lambda^{j}_{n}\rightarrow 0
and
t^{j}_{n}\equiv 0
or
t^{j}_{n}\rightarrow\pm\infty
,

•

if
\lambda^{j}_{n}\equiv 1
then
\left\{\phi^{j}\right\}^{J^{*}}_{j=1}\subset L_{x}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

for each
j
. Furthermore, we have:

•

Smallness of the reminder:

(2.22)

\lim_{J\to J^{*}}\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\|e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}W^{J}_{n}\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}=0.

•

Weak convergence property:

(2.23)

e^{it^{j}_{n}\mathcal{L}_{a}}[(\lambda^{j}_{n})^{\frac{1}{2}}W^{J}_{n}(\lambda^{j}_{n}x+x^{j}_{n})]\rightharpoonup 0\quad\mbox{in}\,\,\dot{H}^{1}_{a},\quad\mbox{for all $1\leq j\leq J$.}

•

Asymptotic orthogonality: for all
1\leq j\neq k\leq J^{*}

(2.24)

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\left[\frac{\lambda^{j}_{n}}{\lambda^{k}_{n}}+\frac{\lambda^{k}_{n}}{\lambda^{j}_{n}}+\frac{|x^{j}_{n}-x^{k}_{n}|^{2}}{\lambda^{j}_{n}\lambda^{k}_{n}}+\frac{|t^{j}_{n}(\lambda^{j}_{n})^{2}-t^{k}_{n}(\lambda^{k}_{n})^{2}|}{\lambda^{j}_{n}\lambda^{k}_{n}}\right]=\infty.

•

Asymptotic Pythagorean expansions:

(2.25)

\displaystyle\sup_{J}\lim_{n\to\infty}\big[M(f_{n})-\sum^{J}_{j=1}M(\phi^{j}_{n})-M(W^{J}_{n})\big]=0,

(2.26)

\displaystyle\sup_{J}\lim_{n\to\infty}\big[E_{a}(f_{n})-\sum^{J}_{j=1}E_{a}(\phi^{j}_{n})-E_{a}(W^{J}_{n})\big]=0.

Remark 2.14
.

We notice that the parameters
x_{n}^{j}\equiv 0
if
\{f_{n}\}\subset\dot{H}_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
are radial functions.

In order to prove Theorem
2.13
,
we need the refined Strichartz estimate and inverse Strichartz estimate.

The refined Strichartz estimates for linear Schrödinger flow
e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}f
was first proved in
[
20
]
for
d=3
and then generalized to the higher dimensions by
[
40
]
.

Lemma 2.15

(Refined Strichartz)
.

Let
d\geqslant 3
and
a>-\frac{(d-2)^{2}}{4}+\left(\frac{d-2}{d+2}\right)^{2}
. For
f\in\dot{H}^{1}_{a}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
we have

\|e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}f\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\|f\|^{\frac{d-2}{d+2}}_{\dot{H}^{1}_{a}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\sup_{N\in 2^{\mathbb{Z}}}\|e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}f_{N}\|^{\frac{4}{d+2}}_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Using the similar argument as in
[
24
,
29
]
and Lemma
2.15
, we can prove the following inverse Strichartz inequality which implies that there is only one bubble concentration.

Proposition 2.16

(Inverse Strichartz inequality)
.

Let
a>-\left(\frac{d-2}{2}\right)^{2}+\left(\frac{d-2}{d+2}\right)^{2}
. Let
\left\{f_{n}\right\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
be a sequence such that

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\|f_{n}\|_{{H}^{1}_{a}}=A<\infty\quad\text{and}\quad\liminf_{n\to\infty}\|e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}f\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}={\varepsilon}>0.

Then, after passing to a subsequence in
n
, there exist
\phi\in\dot{H}^{1}_{a}
,

\left\{\lambda_{n}\right\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subset(0,\infty),\quad\left\{t_{n}\right\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subset\mathbb{R},\quad\left\{x_{n}\right\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}

such that the following statements hold:

(i)

\lambda_{n}\to\lambda_{\infty}\in[0,\infty)
, and if
\lambda_{\infty}>0
then

\phi\in{H}^{1}_{a}
.

(ii)

Weak convergence property:

(2.27)

\lambda^{\frac{d-2}{2}}_{n}(e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}f_{n})(\lambda_{n}x+x_{n})\rightharpoonup\phi(x)\quad\text{weakly in}\quad\begin{cases}H_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\quad\text{if $\lambda_{\infty}>0$}\\
\dot{H}_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\quad\text{if $\lambda_{\infty}=0$}.\end{cases}

(iii)

Decoupling of norms:

(2.28)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\left\{\|f_{n}\|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{1}_{a}}-\|f_{n}-\phi_{n}\|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{1}_{a}}\right\}\gtrsim_{{\varepsilon},A}1

(2.29)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\left\{\|f_{n}\|^{2}_{L_{x}^{2}}-\|f_{n}-\phi_{n}\|^{2}_{L_{x}^{2}}-\|\phi_{n}\|^{2}_{L_{x}^{2}}\right\}=0,

where

\phi_{n}(x):=\begin{cases}\lambda^{-\frac{d-2}{2}}_{n}e^{-it_{n}\mathcal{L}_{a}}\left[\phi\left(\frac{x-x_{n}}{\lambda_{n}}\right)\right]&\text{if $\lambda_{\infty}>0$},\\
\lambda^{-\frac{d-2}{2}}_{n}e^{-it_{n}\mathcal{L}_{a}}\left[(P^{a}_{\geq\lambda^{\theta}_{n}}\phi)\left(\frac{x-x_{n}}{\lambda_{n}}\right)\right]&\text{if $\lambda_{\infty}=0$},\end{cases}

with
0<\theta<1
.

(iv)

We may choose the parameters
\left\{\lambda_{n}\right\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
,
\left\{t_{n}\right\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
and
\left\{x_{n}\right\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
such that either

\frac{t_{n}}{\lambda^{2}_{n}}\to\pm\infty
or
t_{n}\equiv 0
and either
\frac{|x_{n}|}{\lambda_{n}}\to\infty

or
x_{n}\equiv 0
.

Arguing as in
[
20
, Proposition 3.7]
,
[
24
, Lemma 7.4]
and
[
28
, Lemma 3.6]
, we also have the following:

Lemma 2.17
.

Under the hypotheses of Proposition
2.16
, we have:

(i)

Passing to subsequence, we may assume that either
\lambda_{n}\equiv 1
or
\lambda_{n}\to 0
.

(ii)

(2.30)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\left\{\|f_{n}\|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}_{L_{x}^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}}-\|f_{n}-\phi_{n}\|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}_{L_{x}^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}}-\|\phi_{n}\|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}_{L_{x}^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}}\right\}=0,

(2.31)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\left\{\|f_{n}\|^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}_{L_{x}^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}}-\|f_{n}-\phi_{n}\|^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}_{L_{x}^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}}-\|\phi_{n}\|^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}_{L_{x}^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}}\right\}=0.

Using the standard argument as in
[
28
]
along with Proposition
2.16
and Lemma
2.17
, one can finish the proof of linear profile decomposition, i.e. Theorem
2.13
.

2.3.
Local virial argument

In this subsection, we give the computation of the local virial identity. Let
\phi
be a radial, smooth function which satisfy

\phi(x)=\begin{cases}|x|^{2},&\quad|x|\leq 1\\
0,&\quad|x|\geq 2,\end{cases}\quad\text{with}\quad|\partial^{\alpha}\phi(x)|\lesssim|x|^{2-|\alpha|}

for all multi-indices
\alpha
. For a given
R>1
, we define

(2.32)

w_{R}(x)=R^{2}\phi\left(\tfrac{x}{R}\right)

and introduce the localized virial quantity

(2.33)

\displaystyle V_{R}[u]=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}w_{R}|u|^{2}dx,

(2.34)

\displaystyle I_{R}[u]:=\frac{d}{dt}V_{R}[u]=2\IM\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\nabla w_{R}\cdot\nabla u\,\overline{u}\,dx.

By a direct calculation, we can deduce the following virial identity:

Lemma 2.18
.

Let
R\in[1,\infty)
. Assume that
u
solves (
CNLSa
). Then we have

(2.35)

\tfrac{d}{dt}I_{R}[u]=F_{R}[u],

where

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}I_{R}[u]

\displaystyle=\;4\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}w_{R}^{\prime\prime}(r)\big|\nabla u(t,x)\big|^{2}+\frac{ax}{|x|^{4}}\cdot\nabla w_{R}|u|^{2}\;dx-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\Delta^{2}w_{R})(x)|u(t,x)|^{2}\;dx

\displaystyle\hskip 8.61108pt-\frac{4}{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\Delta w_{R})|u(t,x)|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}\;dx+\frac{4}{d+1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\Delta w_{R})|u(t,x)|^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}\;dx

\displaystyle:=F_{R}^{c}[u]+\frac{4}{d+1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\Delta w_{R})|u(t,x)|^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}\;dx.

When
R=\infty
, we denote
F^{c}_{\infty}[u]=8G[u]
,
where

G[f]:=\|f\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{2}-\|f\|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}_{L_{x}^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}}.

Lemma 2.19
.

Fix
R\in[1,\infty]
,
\theta\in\mathbb{R}
and
\lambda>0
. Then we have

F^{c}_{R}[e^{i\theta}\lambda^{\frac{d-2}{2}}W_{a}(\lambda\cdot)]=0.

Proof.

If
R=\infty
, then by a change of variable and (
3.4
) we get

F^{c}_{\infty}[e^{i\theta}\lambda^{\frac{d-2}{2}}W_{a}(\lambda\cdot)]=8G[e^{i\theta}\lambda^{\frac{d-2}{2}}W_{a}(\lambda\cdot)]=8G[W_{a}]=0.

Now assume
R\in[1,\infty)
. Fix
\theta\in\mathbb{R}
and
\lambda>0
. Since

u(t,x)=e^{i\theta}\lambda^{\frac{d-2}{2}}W_{a}(\lambda x)

is a solution to (
NLSa
) and
I_{R}[e^{i\theta}\lambda^{\frac{d-2}{2}}W_{a}(\lambda\cdot)]=0
, Lemma
2.18
implies

F^{c}_{R}[e^{i\theta}\lambda^{\frac{d-2}{2}}W_{a}(\lambda\cdot)]=0.

∎

Combining Lemmas
2.18
and
2.19
yields the following, which we will use to incorporate the modulation analysis into the virial analysis.

Lemma 2.20
.

Consider
R\in[1,\infty]
,
\chi:I\to\mathbb{R}
,
\theta:I\to\mathbb{R}
and

\lambda:I\to\mathbb{R}
. If
u
is a solution to (
CNLSa
), then we have

\displaystyle\tfrac{d}{dt}I_{R}[u]=

\displaystyle F^{c}_{\infty}[u(t)]

(2.36)

\displaystyle+F_{R}[u(t)]-F^{c}_{\infty}[u(t)]

(2.37)

\displaystyle-\chi(t)\big\{F^{c}_{R}[e^{i\theta(t)}\lambda(t)^{\frac{d-2}{2}}W_{a}(\lambda(t)\cdot)]-F^{c}_{\infty}[e^{i\theta(t)}\lambda(t)^{\frac{d-2}{2}}W_{a}(\lambda(t)\cdot)]\big\}.

3.
Variational analysis

In this section, we give some basic properties for the ground states
W_{a}(x)
and
W_{0}(x)
satisfying the following elliptic equations

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{a}W_{a}=|W_{a}|^{\frac{4}{d-2}}W_{a},

\displaystyle-\Delta W_{0}=|W_{0}|^{\frac{4}{d-2}}W_{0}.

To simplify the notation, we assume that
d\geq 3
and
0>a>-\left(\frac{d-2}{2}\right)^{2}
in this section.

In the double-power setting, we still use the ground states
W_{a}(x)
and
W_{0}(x)
as the threshold for scattering.

Definition 3.1
.

We define
1\geqslant\beta>0
via
a=(\frac{d-2}{2})^{2}[\beta^{2}-1]
, or equivalently,
\sigma=\frac{d-2}{2}(1-\beta)
. We then define the
ground state soliton
by

(3.1)

W_{a}(x):=[d(d-2)\beta^{2}]^{\frac{d-2}{4}}\biggl[\frac{|x|^{\beta-1}}{1+|x|^{2\beta}}\biggr]^{\frac{d-2}{2}}.

By the direct calculation, we have

\mathcal{L}_{a}W_{a}=|W_{a}|^{\frac{4}{d-2}}W_{a}.

Then using the Euler’s Beta integral formula(cf. (1.1.20) in
[
1
]
), we can show that

(3.2)

\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}^{1}_{a}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|W_{a}(x)|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}\,dx=\tfrac{\pi d(d-2)}{4}\Bigl[\tfrac{2\sqrt{\pi}\beta^{d-1}}{\Gamma(\frac{d+1}{2})}\Bigr]^{\frac{2}{d}}.

Thus
W_{a}
is a ground state soliton in the sense of being a radial non-negative static solution to (
NLSa
).

Proposition 3.2

(Sharp Sobolev embedding)
.

(i)
We have the following sharp inequality

(3.3)

\|f\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq C_{GN}\|f\|_{\dot{H}^{1}_{a}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},

where

C_{GN}:=\|W_{a}\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}^{1}_{a}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{-1}.

Moreover, equality holds in (
3.3
) if and only if
f(x)=\alpha W_{a}(\lambda x)
for some
\alpha\in\mathbb{C}
and some
\lambda>0
.

(ii)
The inequality (
3.3
) is valid also when
a=0
; however, equality now holds if and only if
f(x)=\alpha W_{0}(\lambda x+y)
for some
\alpha\in\mathbb{C}
, some
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
and some
\lambda>0
.

The ground state
W_{a}
also satisfies the following Pohozaev identities:

(3.4)

\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{2}=\|W_{a}\|^{\tfrac{2d}{d-2}}_{L^{\tfrac{2d}{d-2}}}\mbox{ and }E_{a}^{c}(W_{a})=\tfrac{1}{d}\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{2}.

We will need the following two elementary lemmas.

Lemma 3.3
.

Let
f\in\dot{H}_{a}^{1}
such that
\|f\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}\leq\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}
. Then

\frac{\|f\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{2}}{\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{2}}\leq\frac{E_{a}^{c}(f)}{E_{a}^{c}(W_{a})}.

In particular,
E_{a}^{c}(f)
is positive.

Proof.

Let
\Phi(y)=\frac{1}{2}y-\frac{C_{GN}^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}}{\frac{2d}{d-2}}y^{\frac{2d}{d-2}/2}
. Then by Sobolev embedding

\Phi\big(\|f\|^{2}_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}\big)\leq\frac{1}{2}\|f\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{2}-\frac{1}{\frac{2d}{d-2}}\|f\|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}}=E_{a}^{c}(f).

Note that
\Phi
is concave on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
,
\Phi(0)=0
and
\Phi\big(\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{2}\big)=E_{a}^{c}(W_{a})
. Thus

\displaystyle\forall s\in(0,1),\quad\Phi\big(s\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{2}\big)\geq s\Phi(\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{2})=sE_{a}^{c}(W_{a}).

We take
s=\frac{\|f\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{2}}{\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{2}}
and hence complete the proof.
∎

Lemma 3.4
.

Suppose that
u_{0}\in H_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
satisfies

(3.5)

E_{a}(u_{0})\leq E_{a}^{c}(W_{a})\quad\text{and}\quad\|u_{0}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}<\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}.

Then the solution to (
CNLSa
) with
u|_{t=0}=u_{0}
satisfies

(3.6)

\|u\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}<\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}

throughout maximal lifespan. Similarly, if
\|u_{0}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}>\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}
, then
\|u\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}>\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}
throughout the maximal lifespan of
u
.

Proof.

Suppose that there exists
t_{0}
such that
\|u(t_{0})\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{2}=\|W_{a}\|^{2}_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}
. By Lemma
3.3
, we obtain

1=\frac{\|u(t_{0})\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{2}}{\|W_{a}\|^{2}_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}}\leq\frac{E_{a}^{c}(u(t_{0}))}{E_{a}^{c}(W_{a})}<\frac{E_{a}(u(t_{0}))}{E_{a}^{c}(W_{a})},

which contradicts to (
3.5
). A similar argument treats the remaining case. ∎

We also need the following comparison lemma which is crucial for us to prove Theorem
1.11
and
1.12
.

Lemma 3.5
.

We have the following comparsion estimate for the ground state
W_{0}
and
W_{a}
,

\displaystyle E_{a}^{c}(W_{a})<E_{0}^{c}(W_{0}),\quad\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}<\|W_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{1}}.

Proof.

In fact, by the sharp Sobolev inequality and
\|W_{0}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}<\|W_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{1}}
, we have

\displaystyle\|W_{0}\|_{\frac{2d}{d-2}}<C_{GN}\|W_{0}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}\leq C_{GN}\|W_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{1}}=\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{\frac{-2}{d}}\|W_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{1}}.

Since
\|W_{0}\|_{\frac{2d}{d-2}}/\|W_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{1}}=\|W_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{1}}^{\frac{-2}{d}}
, we finish the proof.
∎

Inspired by the work of Ibrahim-Masmoudi-Nakanishi
[
17
]
and Miao-Xu-Zhao
[
31
]
, we give the following variational characterization in order to prove Theorem
1.11
(i)(ii).
We first give some notation and definition associated with the variational analysis. For
\varphi\in H_{a}^{1}
, we denote the scaling quantity

\varphi^{\lambda}_{d,-2}
by

\displaystyle\varphi^{\lambda}_{d,-2}(x)=e^{d\lambda}\varphi(e^{2\lambda}x).

We denote the variation derivative of
E_{a}(\varphi)
by
K_{a}(\varphi)

\displaystyle K_{a}(\varphi)=\mathcal{T}E_{a}(\varphi)

\displaystyle:=\dfrac{d}{d\lambda}\Big|_{\lambda=0}E_{a}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{d,-2})

(3.7)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(2|\nabla\varphi|^{2}+2a\frac{|\varphi|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}-2|\varphi|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}+\frac{2d}{d+1}|\varphi|^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}\right)\;dx.

Then we denote the quadratic and nonlinear parts of
K_{a}
by
K_{a}^{Q}
and

K_{a}^{N}
, that is,

\displaystyle K_{a}(\varphi)=K_{a}^{Q}(\varphi)+K_{a}^{N}(\varphi),

where

\displaystyle K_{a}^{Q}(\varphi)=\displaystyle 2\;\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(|\nabla\varphi|^{2}+a\frac{|\varphi|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}\right)\;dx,

\displaystyle K_{a}^{N}(\varphi)=\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(-2|\varphi|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}+\frac{2d}{d+1}\varphi|^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}\right)\;dx.

Next, we define the threshold for Theorem
1.11
as the following constrained minimization problem associated to the energy
E_{a}(\varphi)
:

(3.8)

\displaystyle m_{a}=\inf\{E_{a}(\varphi)\;|\;0\not=\varphi\in H_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\;K_{a}(\varphi)=0\},

and two regions

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{a}^{+}

\displaystyle=\{f\in H_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})|E_{a}(f)<E_{a}^{c}(W_{a}),\ K_{a}(f)\geq 0\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{a}^{-}

\displaystyle=\{f\in H_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})|E_{a}(f)<E_{a}^{c}(W_{a}),\ K_{a}(f)<0\}.

In this section, we will prove that

m_{a}=E_{a}^{c}(W_{a})

and the scattering and blow-up region in Theorem
1.11
are actually the sets
\mathcal{K}_{a}^{+}
and
\mathcal{K}_{a}^{-}
respectively. Finally, we give the crucial convex lemma adpated to the proof of Theorem
1.11
.

We start with the following basic lemmas.

Lemma 3.6
.

For any
\varphi\in H_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, we have

(3.9)

\displaystyle\lim_{\lambda\rightarrow-\infty}K_{a}^{Q}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{d,-2})=0.

By the definition of
K_{a}^{Q}
, one can prove this lemma directly. Thus we omit the details.

The next lemma show the positivity of
K_{a}
for sequences which is near
0
in the energy
space.

Lemma 3.7
.

For any bounded sequence

\{\varphi_{n}\}\subset H_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\backslash\{0\}
with

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}K_{a}^{Q}(\varphi_{n})=0,

then for large
n
, we have

\displaystyle K_{a}(\varphi_{n})>0.

Proof.

Since
K_{a}^{Q}(\varphi_{n})\rightarrow 0
, we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\big\|\nabla\varphi_{n}\big\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\sim\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\big\|\varphi_{n}\big\|^{2}_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}=0.
Then using the Sobolev embedding, we have for large

n

\displaystyle\big\|\varphi_{n}\big\|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}_{x}}\lesssim\big\|\nabla\varphi_{n}\big\|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}_{L^{2}_{x}}=

\displaystyle o(\big\|\nabla\varphi_{n}\big\|^{2}_{L^{2}}),

\displaystyle\big\|\varphi_{n}\big\|^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}_{L^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}_{x}}\lesssim\big\|\varphi_{n}\big\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{2d+2}{d^{2}-1}}\big\|\nabla\varphi_{n}\big\|^{\frac{2d^{2}+2d}{d^{2}-1}}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=o(\big\|\nabla\varphi_{n}\big\|^{2}_{L^{2}}),

where we use the fact that
\varphi_{n}
is
L^{2}
bounded. Hence for large
n
, we have

\displaystyle K_{a}(\varphi_{n})=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(2|\nabla\varphi_{n}|^{2}+2a\frac{|\varphi|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}-2|\varphi_{n}|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}+\frac{2d}{d+1}|\varphi_{n}|^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}\right)\;dx\thickapprox\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\nabla\varphi_{n}|^{2}\;dx>0.

This complete the proof.
∎

By the definition of
K_{a}
, we denote two real numbers by

\displaystyle\bar{\mu}=\max\{\tfrac{2d+2}{d-1},0,\tfrac{2d}{d-2}\},\quad\underline{\mu}=\min\{\tfrac{2d+2}{d-1},0,\tfrac{2d}{d-2}\}.

Note that for
d\in\{3,4,5\}
,

\displaystyle\bar{\mu}=\max\{\tfrac{2d+2}{d-1},0,\tfrac{2d}{d-2}\}=\tfrac{2d}{d-2},\quad\underline{\mu}=\min\{\tfrac{2d+2}{d-1},0,\tfrac{2d}{d-2}\}=0.

Next, we show the behavior of the scaling derivative functional
K_{a}
.

Lemma 3.8
.

For any
\varphi\in H_{a}^{1}
, we have

\displaystyle\left(\bar{\mu}-\mathcal{L}\right)E_{a}(\varphi)=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{2}{d-1}\left(\big|\nabla\varphi\big|^{2}+a\frac{|\varphi|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}+\big|\varphi\big|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}\right)\;dx,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}\left(\bar{\mu}-\mathcal{L}\right)E_{a}(\varphi)=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\frac{8}{d-1}\big|\nabla\varphi\big|^{2}+\frac{8a}{d-1}\frac{|\varphi|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}+\frac{8d}{(d-1)(d-2)}\big|\varphi\big|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}\right)\;dx.

Proof.

By the definition of
\mathcal{T}
,
we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}\big\|\nabla\varphi\big\|^{2}_{L^{2}}=4\big\|\nabla\varphi\big\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+,4a\big\|\frac{\varphi}{x}\big\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\quad\mathcal{T}\big\|\varphi\big\|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}}=\frac{4d}{d-2}\big\|\varphi\big\|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}\big\|\varphi\big\|^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}_{L^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}}=\frac{4d}{d-1}\big\|\varphi\big\|^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}_{L^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}},

which implies that

\displaystyle\left(\bar{\mu}-\mathcal{T}\right)E_{a}(\varphi)=\frac{2d}{d-2}E_{a}(\varphi)-K_{a}(\varphi)=\frac{2}{d-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\big|\nabla\varphi\big|^{2}+a\frac{|\varphi|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}+\big|\varphi\big|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}\right)\;dx,

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}\left(\bar{\mu}-\mathcal{T}\right)E_{a}(\varphi)=\frac{2}{d-1}\mathcal{T}\big\|\nabla\varphi\big\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\frac{2}{d-1}\mathcal{T}\big\|\varphi\big\|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\frac{8}{d-1}\big|\nabla\varphi\big|^{2}+\frac{8a}{d-1}\frac{|\varphi|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}+\frac{8d}{(d-1)(d-2)}\big|\varphi\big|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}\right)\;dx.

This completes the proof.
∎

According to the above analysis, we will replace the functional

E_{a}
in (
3.8
) with a positive functional
H_{a}
, while
extending the minimizing region from “
K_{a}(\varphi)=0
” to “
K_{a}(\varphi)\leq 0
”. Let

\displaystyle H_{a}(\varphi):=\left(1-\frac{\mathcal{T}}{\bar{\mu}}\right)E_{a}(\varphi)=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\frac{1}{2d}\big|\nabla\varphi\big|^{2}+\frac{a}{2d}\frac{|\varphi|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}+\frac{1}{2d}\big|\varphi\big|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}\right)\;dx,

then for any
\varphi\in H_{a}^{1}\backslash\{0\}
, we have

\displaystyle H_{a}(\varphi)>0,\quad\mathcal{T}H_{a}(\varphi)\geq 0.

Now we can characterization the minimization problem (
3.8
) by
use of
H_{a}
.

Lemma 3.9
.

For the minimization
m_{a}
in (
3.8
), we
have

\displaystyle m_{a}=

\displaystyle\inf\{H(\varphi)\;|\;\varphi\in H_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\;\varphi\not=0,\;K_{a}(\varphi)\leq 0\}

(3.10)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\inf\{H(\varphi)\;|\;\varphi\in H_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\;\varphi\not=0,\;K_{a}(\varphi)<0\}.

Proof.

For any
\varphi\in H_{a}^{1}
,
\varphi\not=0
with
K_{a}(\varphi)=0
, we have

E_{a}(\varphi)=H_{a}(\varphi)
, this implies that

\displaystyle m_{a}=

\displaystyle\inf\{E_{a}(\varphi)\;|\;\varphi\in H_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\;\varphi\not=0,\;K_{a}(\varphi)=0\}

(3.11)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\inf\{H(\varphi)\;|\;\varphi\in H_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\;\varphi\not=0,\;K_{a}(\varphi)\leq 0\}.

On the other hand, for any
\varphi\in H_{a}^{1}
,
\varphi\not=0
with

K_{a}(\varphi)<0
, by Lemma
3.6
, Lemma
3.7

and the continuity of
K_{a}
in
\lambda
, we know that there
exists a
\lambda_{0}<0
such that

\displaystyle K_{a}(\varphi^{\lambda_{0}}_{d,-2})=0,

then by
\mathcal{T}H_{a}\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle E_{a}(\varphi^{\lambda_{0}}_{d,-2})=H_{a}(\varphi^{\lambda_{0}}_{d,-2})\leq H_{a}(\varphi^{0}_{d,-2})=H_{a}(\varphi).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\inf\{E_{a}(\varphi)\;|\;\varphi\in H_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\;\varphi\not=0,\;K_{a}(\varphi)=0\}

(3.12)

\displaystyle\leq\inf\{H_{a}(\varphi)\;|\;\varphi\in H_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\;\varphi\not=0,\;K_{a}(\varphi)<0\}.

By (
3.11
) and (
3.12
), we have

\displaystyle\inf\{H(\varphi)\;|\;\varphi\in H_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\;\varphi\not=0,\;K_{a}(\varphi)\leq 0\}

\displaystyle\leq m_{a}\leq\inf\{H_{a}(\varphi)\;|\;\varphi\in H_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\;\varphi\not=0,\;K_{a}(\varphi)<0\}.

In order to show (
3.10
), it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\inf\{H(\varphi)\;|\;\varphi\in H_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\;\varphi\not=0,\;K_{a}(\varphi)\leq 0\}

(3.13)

\displaystyle\geq\inf\{H(\varphi)\;|\;\varphi\in H_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\;\varphi\not=0,\;K_{a}(\varphi)<0\}.

For any
\varphi\in H_{a}^{1}
,
\varphi\not=0
with
K_{a}(\varphi)\leq 0
. By Lemma

3.8
, we know that

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}K_{a}(\varphi)=\bar{\mu}K_{a}(\varphi)-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\frac{8}{d-1}\big|\nabla\varphi\big|^{2}+\frac{8a}{d-1}\frac{|\varphi|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}+\frac{8d}{(d-1)(d-2)}\big|\varphi\big|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}\right)\;dx<0,

then for any
\lambda>0
we have

\displaystyle K_{a}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{d,-2})<0,

and as
\lambda\rightarrow 0

\displaystyle H_{a}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{d,-2})=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\frac{e^{4\lambda}}{2d}\big|\nabla\varphi\big|^{2}+\frac{e^{\frac{4d}{d-2}\lambda}}{2d}\big|\varphi\big|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}\right)\;dx\longrightarrow H_{a}(\varphi).

This shows (
3.13
), and completes the proof.
∎

Next we will use the (
\dot{H}_{a}^{1}
-invariant) scaling argument to remove the
L^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}
term (the lower regularity quantity than
\dot{H}_{a}^{1}
) in
K_{a}
,
that is, to replace the constrained
condition
K_{a}(\varphi)<0
with
K_{a}^{c}(\varphi)<0
, where

\displaystyle K_{a}^{c}(\varphi):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(2|\nabla\varphi|^{2}+2a\frac{|\varphi|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}-2|\varphi|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}\right)\;dx.

In fact, we have

Lemma 3.10
.

For the minimization
m_{a}
in (
3.8
), we
have

\displaystyle m_{a}=

\displaystyle\inf\{H_{a}(\varphi)\;|\;\varphi\in H_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\;\varphi\not=0,\;K_{a}^{c}(\varphi)<0\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\inf\{H_{a}(\varphi)\;|\;\varphi\in H_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\;\varphi\not=0,\;K_{a}^{c}(\varphi)\leq 0\}.

Proof.

Since

K_{a}^{c}(\varphi)\leq K_{a}(\varphi)
, it is obvious that

\displaystyle m_{a}=

\displaystyle\inf\{H_{a}(\varphi)\;|\;\varphi\in H_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\;\varphi\not=0,\;K_{a}(\varphi)<0\}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\inf\{H_{a}(\varphi)\;|\;\varphi\in H_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\;\varphi\not=0,\;K_{a}^{c}(\varphi)<0\}.

Hence in order to show the first equality, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\inf\{H_{a}(\varphi)\;|\;\varphi\in H_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\;\varphi\not=0,\;K_{a}(\varphi)<0\}

(3.14)

\displaystyle\leq\inf\{H_{a}(\varphi)\;|\;\varphi\in H_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\;\varphi\not=0,\;K_{a}^{c}(\varphi)<0\}.

To do so, for any
\varphi\in H_{a}^{1}
,
\varphi\not=0
with
K_{a}^{c}(\varphi)<0
, taking

\displaystyle\varphi^{\lambda}_{d-2,-2}(x)=e^{(d-2)\lambda}\varphi(e^{2\lambda}x),

we have
\varphi^{\lambda}_{(d-2),-2}\in H_{a}^{1}
and
\varphi^{\lambda}_{d-2,-2}\not=0
for any

\lambda>0
. In addition, we have

\displaystyle K_{a}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{d-2,-2})=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(2\big|\nabla\varphi\big|^{2}+2a\frac{|\varphi|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}-2\big|\varphi\big|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}+\frac{2d}{d+1}e^{-\frac{4}{d-1}\lambda}\big|\varphi\big|^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}\right)\;dx

\displaystyle\longrightarrow K_{a}^{c}(\varphi),

\displaystyle H_{a}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{d-2,-2})=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\frac{1}{2d}\big|\nabla\varphi\big|^{2}+\frac{1}{2d}\big|\varphi\big|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}\right)\;dx=

\displaystyle H_{a}(\varphi),

as
\lambda\rightarrow+\infty
. This gives
(
3.14
), and completes the proof of the
first equality.

For the second equality, it is obvious that

\displaystyle\inf\{H_{a}(\varphi)\;|\;\varphi\in H_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\;\varphi\not=0,\;K^{c}(\varphi)<0\}

\displaystyle\geq\inf\{H_{a}(\varphi)\;|\;\varphi\in H_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\;\varphi\not=0,\;K_{a}^{c}(\varphi)\leq 0\},

hence we only need to show that

\displaystyle\inf\{H_{a}(\varphi)\;|\;\varphi\in H_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\;\varphi\not=0,\;K_{a}^{c}(\varphi)<0\}

(3.15)

\displaystyle\leq\inf\{H_{a}(\varphi)\;|\;\varphi\in H_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\;\varphi\not=0,\;K_{a}^{c}(\varphi)\leq 0\}.

To do this, we use the (
L^{2}
-invariant) scaling argument. For any
\varphi\in H_{a}^{1}
,
\varphi\not=0
with
K_{a}^{c}(\varphi)\leq 0
, we have
\varphi^{\lambda}_{d,-2}\in H_{a}^{1}
,
\varphi^{\lambda}_{d,-2}\not=0
. In addition, by

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}K_{a}^{c}(\varphi)=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(8\big|\nabla\varphi\big|^{2}+8\frac{|u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}-\frac{8d}{d-2}\big|\varphi\big|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}\right)\;dx=4K^{c}(\varphi)-\frac{16}{d-2}\big\|\varphi\big\|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}}<0,

\displaystyle H_{a}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{d,-2})=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\frac{e^{4\lambda}}{2d}\big|\nabla\varphi\big|^{2}+\frac{e^{\frac{4d}{d-2}\lambda}}{2d}\big|\varphi\big|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}\right)\;dx,

we have
K_{a}^{c}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{d,-2})<0
for any
\lambda>0
, and

\displaystyle H_{a}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{d,-2})\rightarrow H_{a}(\varphi),\;\;\text{as}\;\;\lambda\rightarrow 0.

This implies (
3.15
) and completes the
proof.
∎

Now, we are in position to compute the minimization
m_{a}
of (
3.8
) by making use of the sharp Sobolev inequality in
(
3.3
)
.

Lemma 3.11
.

For the minimization
m_{a}
in (
3.8
), we
have

\displaystyle m_{a}=E_{a}^{c}(W_{a}).

Proof.

By Lemma
3.10
, we have

\displaystyle m_{a}=

\displaystyle\inf\left\{\frac{1}{2d}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(|\nabla\varphi|^{2}+a\frac{|\varphi|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}+|\varphi|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}\right)\;dx\;\Big|\;\varphi\in H_{a}^{1},\;\varphi\not=0,\;\big\|\varphi\big\|^{2}_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}\leq\big\|\varphi\big\|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}}\right\}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\inf\bigg\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{1}{2d}\left(|\nabla\varphi|^{2}+a\frac{|\varphi|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}+|\varphi|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}\right)+\frac{1}{2d}\left(|\nabla\varphi|^{2}+a\frac{|\varphi|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}-|\varphi|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}\right)\;dx\;\Big|\;

\displaystyle\hskip 21.52771pt\varphi\in H_{a}^{1},\;\varphi\not=0,\;\big\|\varphi\big\|^{2}_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}\leq\big\|\varphi\big\|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}}\bigg\}

where the equality holds if and only if the minimization is taken
by some
\varphi
with
\big\|\varphi\big\|^{2}_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}=\big\|\varphi\big\|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}}
. While

\displaystyle\inf\left\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{1}{d}|\nabla\varphi|^{2}+a\frac{|\varphi|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}\;dx\;\big|\;\varphi\in H_{a}^{1},\;\varphi\not=0,\;\big\|\varphi\big\|^{2}_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}\leq\big\|\varphi\big\|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}}\right\}

\displaystyle=\inf\left\{\frac{1}{d}\big\|\varphi\big\|^{2}_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}\left(\frac{\big\|\varphi\big\|^{2}_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}}{\big\|\varphi\big\|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}}}\right)^{\frac{d-2}{2}}\;\Big|\;\varphi\in H_{a}^{1},\;\varphi\not=0\right\}

\displaystyle=\inf\left\{\frac{1}{d}\left(\frac{\big\|\varphi\big\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}}{\big\|\varphi\big\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}}}\right)^{d}\;\Big|\;\varphi\in H_{a}^{1},\;\varphi\not=0\right\}

\displaystyle=\inf\left\{\frac{1}{d}\left(\frac{\big\|\varphi\big\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}}{\big\|\varphi\big\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}}}\right)^{d}\;\Big|\;\varphi\in\dot{H}_{a}^{1},\;\varphi\not=0\right\}=\frac{1}{d}\big(C_{GN}\big)^{-d},

where we use the density property
H_{a}^{1}\hookrightarrow\dot{H}_{a}^{1}
in the last second equality
and that
C_{GN}
is the sharp Sobolev constant in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, that is,

\displaystyle\big\|\varphi\big\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}}\leq C_{GN}\big\|\varphi\big\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}},\;\;\forall\;\varphi\in\dot{H}_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),

and the equality can be attained by the ground state
W_{a}
of the following
elliptic equation

\displaystyle-\Delta W_{a}+a\frac{|W_{a}|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}=|W_{a}|^{\frac{4}{d-2}}W_{a}.

This implies that
\frac{1}{d}\big(C_{GN}\big)^{-d}=E_{a}^{c}(W_{a})
. The proof
is finished.
∎

Combining Lemma
3.10
and
3.11
, we can deduce the characterization of
K_{a}^{+}/K_{a}^{-}
.

Theorem 3.12
.

We define

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{K}_{a}}^{+}

\displaystyle=\{f\in H_{a}^{1}|E_{a}(f)<E_{a}^{c}(W_{a}),\ \|f\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{2}\leq\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{2}\},
