\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{K}_{a}}^{-}

\displaystyle=\{f\in H_{a}^{1}|E_{a}(f)<E_{a}^{c}(W_{a}),\ \|f\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{2}>\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{2}\},

Then we have
\bar{\mathcal{K}_{a}}^{+}=\mathcal{K}_{a}^{+}
,
\bar{\mathcal{K}_{a}}^{-}=\mathcal{K}_{a}^{-}
.

Proof.

First we recall that

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{a}^{+}

\displaystyle=\{f\in H_{a}^{1}|E_{a}(f)<E_{a}^{c}(W_{a}),\ K_{a}(f)\geq 0\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{a}^{-}

\displaystyle=\{f\in H_{a}^{1}|E_{a}(f)<E_{a}^{c}(W_{a}),\ K_{a}(f)<0\}.

By the variational results of Killip-Miao-Visan-Zhang-Zheng
[
20
]
, we know that If

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{2}<\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{2},\ \ E_{a}^{c}(f)<E_{a}^{c}(W_{a}).

Then

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{2}-\|f\|_{\frac{2d}{d-2}}^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}\geq 0,\ \ E_{a}^{c}(f)<E_{a}^{c}(W_{a}).

This gives
\bar{\mathcal{K}_{a}}^{+}\subset\mathcal{K}_{a}^{+}
.

On the other hand, we clearly have
0\in\mathcal{K}_{a}^{+},\bar{\mathcal{K}_{a}}^{+}
.

Now let
f\in H_{a}^{1},f\neq 0
, we have
f_{d-2,-2}^{\lambda}\in H_{a}^{1}
and
f_{d-2,-2}^{\lambda}\neq 0
for any
\lambda>0
. In addition, we have

\displaystyle K_{a}(f_{d-2,-2}^{\lambda})

\displaystyle=2(\|f\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{2}-\|f\|_{\frac{2d}{d-2}}^{\frac{2d}{d-2}})+\frac{2d}{d+1}e^{-\frac{\lambda}{d-1}}\|f\|_{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}},

\displaystyle H_{a}(f_{d-2,-2}^{\lambda})

\displaystyle=H_{a}(f).

By Lemma
3.10
and direct calculation,

(3.16)

\displaystyle E_{a}^{c}(W_{a})=\inf\{H_{a}(f)|f\in H_{a}^{1},f\neq 0,K_{a}(f)\leq 0\},

(3.17)

\displaystyle E_{a}(f)=H_{a}(f)+\frac{1}{2d}K_{a}(f),

(3.18)

\displaystyle E_{a}(f_{d-2,-2}^{\lambda})\leq E_{a}(f)<E_{a}^{c}(W_{a}),\text{ \penalty\ for\penalty\ }\lambda>0,

and

K_{a}(f_{d-2,-2}^{\lambda})=K_{a}^{c}(f)+\frac{2d}{d+1}e^{-\frac{4\lambda}{d-2}}\|f\|_{\frac{2d}{d+1}}^{\frac{2d}{d+1}},

we can deduce
\mathcal{K}_{a}^{+}\subset\bar{\mathcal{K}_{a}}^{+}
.
In fact, if
K_{a}^{c}(f)<0
, there exists
\lambda_{0}\geq 0
such that
K_{a}(f^{\lambda_{0}}_{d-2,-2}))=0
(since
K_{a}(f_{d-2,-2}^{0})\geq 0
). Then, by the identity (
3.17
), we have

E_{a}(f_{d-2,-2}^{\lambda_{0}})=H_{a}(f)\geq E_{a}^{c}(W_{a}),

which is opposite to (
3.18
).
Then, the result
\mathcal{K}_{a}^{+}=\bar{\mathcal{K}_{a}}^{+}
follows, which implies
\mathcal{K}_{a}^{-}=\bar{\mathcal{K}_{a}}^{-}
.
∎

After the computation of the minimization
m_{a}
in
(
3.8
), we next give some variational estimates.

Lemma 3.13
.

For any
\varphi\in H_{a}^{1}
with
K_{a}(\varphi)\geq 0
, we
have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\frac{1}{2d}\big|\nabla\varphi\big|^{2}+\frac{a}{2d}\frac{|u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}+\frac{1}{2d}\big|\varphi\big|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}\right)dx

(3.19)

\displaystyle\leq E_{a}(\varphi)\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\frac{1}{2}\big|\nabla\varphi\big|^{2}+\frac{a}{2}\frac{|u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}+\frac{d-1}{2d+2}\big|\varphi\big|^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}\right)dx.

Proof.

On one hand, the right hand side of (
3.19
) is trivial. On the other hand,
by the definition of
E_{a}
and
K_{a}
, we have

\displaystyle E_{a}(\varphi)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\frac{1}{2d}\big|\nabla\varphi\big|^{2}+\frac{a}{2d}\frac{|u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}+\frac{1}{2d}\big|\varphi\big|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}\right)\;dx+\frac{1}{2d}K_{a}(\varphi),

which implies the left hand side of (
3.19
).
∎

At the last of this section, we give the uniform bounds on the
scaling derivative functional
K_{a}(\varphi)
with the energy

E_{a}(\varphi)
below the threshold
m_{a}
.

Lemma 3.14
.

For any
\varphi\in H_{a}^{1}
with
E_{a}(\varphi)<m_{a}
.

(1)

If
K_{a}(\varphi)<0
, then

(3.20)

\displaystyle K_{a}(\varphi)\leq-\bar{\mu}\big(m_{a}-E_{a}(\varphi)\big)=\frac{2d}{d-2}\big(m_{a}-E_{a}(\varphi)\big).

(2)

If
K_{a}(\varphi)\geq 0
, then

\displaystyle K_{a}(\varphi)

\displaystyle\geq\min\bigg(\bar{\mu}(m_{a}-E_{a}(\varphi)),\frac{2}{2d-3}\big\|\nabla\varphi\big\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\frac{2a}{2d-3}\|\frac{\varphi}{x}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 8.61108pt+\frac{2d}{(2d-3)(d+1)}\big\|\varphi\big\|^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}_{L^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}}\bigg)

(3.21)

\displaystyle=\min\bigg(\frac{2d}{d-2}(m_{a}-E_{a}(\varphi)),\frac{2}{2d-3}\big\|\nabla\varphi\big\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\frac{2a}{2d-3}\|\frac{\varphi}{x}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 8.61108pt+\frac{2d}{(2d-3)(d+1)}\big\|\varphi\big\|^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}_{L^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}}\bigg).

Proof.

By Lemma
3.8
, for any
\varphi\in H_{a}^{1}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}^{2}E(\varphi)=\bar{\mu}\mathcal{T}E_{a}(\varphi)-\frac{8}{d-1}\big\|\nabla\varphi\big\|^{2}_{L^{2}}-\frac{8a}{d-1}\big\|\frac{\varphi}{x}\big\|^{2}_{L^{2}}-\frac{8d}{(d-2)(d-1)}\big\|\varphi\big\|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}}.

Let
j(\lambda)=E_{a}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{d,-2})
, then we have

(3.22)

\displaystyle j^{\prime\prime}(\lambda)=\bar{\mu}j^{\prime}(\lambda)-\frac{8e^{4\lambda}}{d-1}-\frac{8ae^{4\lambda}}{d-1}\big\|\frac{\varphi}{x}\big\|^{2}_{L^{2}}-\big\|\nabla\varphi\big\|^{2}_{L^{2}}-\frac{8de^{\frac{4d}{d-2}\lambda}}{(d-2)(d-1)}\big\|\varphi\big\|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}}.

Case I:
If
K_{a}(\varphi)<0
, then by
(
3.9
), Lemma
3.7
and the continuity of
K_{a}
in

\lambda
, there exists a negative number

\lambda_{0}<0
such that
K_{a}(\varphi^{\lambda_{0}}_{d,-2})=0
, and

\displaystyle K_{a}(\varphi^{\lambda}_{d,-2})<0,\;\;\forall\;\;\lambda\in(\lambda_{0},0).

By (
3.8
), we obtain
j(\lambda_{0})=E_{a}(\varphi^{\lambda_{0}}_{d,-2})\geq m
. Now by integrating (
3.22
) over

[\lambda_{0},0]
, we have

\displaystyle\int^{0}_{\lambda_{0}}j^{\prime\prime}(\lambda)\;d\lambda\leq\bar{\mu}\int^{0}_{\lambda_{0}}j^{\prime}(\lambda)\;d\lambda,

which implies that

\displaystyle K_{a}(\varphi)=j^{\prime}(0)-j^{\prime}(\lambda_{0})\leq\bar{\mu}\left(j(0)-j(\lambda_{0})\right)\leq-\bar{\mu}\big(m_{a}-E_{a}(\varphi)\big),

which implies (
3.20
).

Case II:

K_{a}(\varphi)\geq 0
. We divide it
into two subcases:

When
2\bar{\mu}K_{a}(\varphi)\geq\frac{8d}{(d-2)(d-1)}\big\|\varphi\big\|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}}
.
Since

\displaystyle\hskip 21.52771pt\frac{8d}{(d-2)(d-1)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\big|\varphi\big|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}\;dx

\displaystyle=-\frac{4d}{(d-2)(d-1)}K_{a}(\varphi)+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\bigg(\frac{8d}{(d-2)(d-1)}\big|\nabla\varphi\big|^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{8ad}{(d-2)(d-1)}\frac{|\varphi|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}+\frac{8d^{2}}{(d-2)(d-1)(d+1)}\big|\varphi\big|^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}\bigg)\;dx,

then we have

\displaystyle 2\bar{\mu}K_{a}(\varphi)

\displaystyle\geq-\frac{4d}{(d-2)(d-1)}K_{a}(\varphi)+

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\frac{8d}{(d-2)(d-1)}\big|\nabla\varphi\big|^{2}+\frac{8ad}{(d-2)(d-1)}\frac{|\varphi|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}+\frac{8d^{2}}{(d-2)(d-1)(d+1)}\big|\varphi\big|^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}\right)\;dx,

which implies that

\displaystyle K_{a}(\varphi)\geq\frac{2d}{d-3}\big\|\nabla\varphi\big\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\frac{2d}{d-3}\big\|\frac{\varphi}{x}\big\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\frac{2d}{(2d-3)(d+1)}\big\|\varphi\big\|^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}_{L^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}}.

When
2\bar{\mu}K_{a}(\varphi)\leq\frac{8d}{(d-2)(d-1)}\big\|\varphi\big\|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}}
.
By (
3.22
), we have for
\lambda=0

\displaystyle 0<

\displaystyle 2\bar{\mu}j^{\prime}(\lambda)<\frac{8de^{\frac{4d}{d-2}\lambda}}{(d-2)(d-1)}\big\|\varphi\big\|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}},

(3.23)

\displaystyle j^{\prime\prime}(\lambda)=\bar{\mu}j^{\prime}(\lambda)-

\displaystyle\frac{8e^{4\lambda}}{d-1}\big\|\nabla\varphi\big\|^{2}_{L^{2}}-\frac{8ae^{4\lambda}}{d-1}\big\|\frac{\varphi}{x}\varphi\big\|^{2}_{L^{2}}-\frac{8de^{\frac{4d}{d-2}\lambda}}{(d-2)(d-1)}\big\|\varphi\big\|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}}\leq-\bar{\mu}j^{\prime}(\lambda).

By the continuity of
j^{\prime}
and
j^{\prime\prime}
in
\lambda
, we know that
j^{\prime}
is an accelerating decreasing function as
\lambda
increases until
j^{\prime}(\lambda_{0})=0
for some
finite number
\lambda_{0}>0
and (
3.23
) holds on
[0,\lambda_{0}]
.

By

K_{a}(\varphi^{\lambda_{0}}_{d,-2})=j^{\prime}(\lambda_{0})=0,

we know that

\displaystyle E_{a}(\varphi^{\lambda_{0}}_{d,-2})\geq m_{a}.

Now integrating (
3.23
) over
[0,\lambda_{0}]
, we obtain that

\displaystyle-K_{a}(\varphi)=j^{\prime}(\lambda_{0})-j^{\prime}(0)\leq-\bar{\mu}\big(j(\lambda_{0})-j(0)\big)\leq-\bar{\mu}(m_{a}-E_{a}(\varphi)).

This completes the proof.
∎

4.
Modulation analysis

In this section, we give the modulation analysis around the ground state
W_{a}(x)
, which is crucial in the proof of Theorem
1.12
. We assume that
d\in\{3,4,5\}
,
0>a>-\left(\frac{d-2}{2}\right)^{2}+\left(\frac{2(d-2)}{d+2}\right)^{2}
and
u:I\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{C}
is a solution to (
CNLSa
) with

(4.1)

E_{a}(u_{0})=E_{a}^{c}(W_{a}).

We write
\delta(t):=\delta(u(t))
, where

\delta(u):=\bigl|\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{2}-\|u\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{2}\bigr|.

Next,
we choose a small parameter
\delta_{0}
(to be determined below) and define

I_{0}=\{t\in I:\delta(u(t))<\delta_{0}\}.

Proposition 4.1
.

There exist
\delta_{0}>0
sufficiently small and functions
\theta:I_{0}\to\mathbb{R}
and
\mu:I_{0}\to\mathbb{R}
such that

(4.2)

u(t,x)=e^{i\theta(t)}[g(t)+\mu(t)^{\frac{d-2}{2}}W_{a}(\mu(t)x)]

for
t\in I_{0}
, we have

(4.3)

\displaystyle\|u(t)\|^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}_{L_{x}^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}}+\mu(t)^{-\frac{\beta(d-2)(d+1)}{d-1}}\lesssim\delta(t)^{2}\sim\|g(t)\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{2},

(4.4)

\displaystyle\left|\tfrac{\mu^{\prime}(t)}{\mu(t)}\right|\lesssim\mu^{2}(t)\delta(t).

We begin with the following lemma.

Lemma 4.2
.

Assume that
u\in\dot{H}_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
E_{a}(u)=E_{a}^{c}(W_{a})
. Then for any
\varepsilon>0
, there exists
\delta>0
such that

\delta(u(t))<\delta,\ \ \inf_{\theta\in\mathbb{S}^{1},\mu>0}\|u-e^{i\theta}\mu^{-\frac{d-2}{2}}W_{a}(\tfrac{x}{\mu})\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}<\varepsilon.

Proof.

We argue by contradiction. Suppose this lemma does not hold, then there must exist
\varepsilon_{0}>0
and a sequence of
\dot{H}_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
functions
\{u_{n}\}
such that

(4.5)

\text{ }E_{a}(u_{n})=E_{a}^{c}(W_{a}),\text{ }\delta(u_{n}(t))\to 0,

but

(4.6)

\inf_{\theta\in\mathbb{S}^{1},\mu>0}\|u_{n}-e^{i\theta}\mu^{-\frac{d-2}{2}}W_{a}(\tfrac{x}{\mu})\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}>\varepsilon_{0}.

Replacing
u_{n}
by
u_{n}\cdot\frac{\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}}{\|u_{n}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}}
, we may assume

(4.7)

\displaystyle\|f_{n}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}=\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}},\quad\|f_{n}\|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}_{\frac{2d}{d-2}}-\tfrac{(d-2)(d+1)}{(d-1)d}\|f_{n}\|^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}_{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}\to\|W_{a}\|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}_{\frac{2d}{d-2}},

(4.8)

\displaystyle\inf_{\theta\in\mathbb{S}^{1},\mu>0}\|u_{n}-e^{i\theta}\mu^{-\frac{d-2}{2}}W_{a}(\tfrac{x}{\mu})\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}>\varepsilon_{0}.

Applying Lemma
2.13
to
\{u_{n}\}
, we obtain

u_{n}=\sum_{j=1}^{J}\phi_{n}^{j}+r_{n}^{J},

for each
J\in\{1,\cdots,J^{*}\}
with the stated properties.
From the
\dot{H}_{a}^{1}
decoupling in Lemma
2.13
and (
4.7
) we have

(4.9)

\displaystyle\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{2}=\lim_{n\to\infty}\biggl(\sum_{j=1}^{J}\|\phi_{n}^{j}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{2}+\|r_{n}^{J}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{2}\biggr)=\sum_{j=1}^{J}\|\phi^{j}\|_{X^{j}}^{2}+\lim_{n\to\infty}\|r_{n}^{J}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{2}.

Here
\|\cdot\|_{X^{j}}=\|\cdot\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}
if
x_{n}^{j}\equiv 0
and
\|\cdot\|_{X^{j}}=\|\cdot\|_{\dot{H}^{1}}
if
\frac{|x_{n}^{j}|}{\lambda_{n}^{j}}\to\infty
. As (
4.9
) holds for any
J
, we take a limit and get

(4.10)

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{J^{*}}\|\phi^{j}\|_{X^{j}}^{2}\leq\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{2}.

On the other hand, using the decoupling in
L_{x}^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, (
4.7
) and the sharp Sobolev embedding, we have

\displaystyle\|W_{a}\|_{\frac{2d}{d-2}}^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}=\lim_{n\to\infty}\|u_{n}\|_{\frac{2d}{d-2}}^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}-\tfrac{(d-2)(d+1)}{(d-1)d}\|u_{n}\|^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}_{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{J^{*}}\|\phi^{j}\|_{\frac{2d}{d-2}}^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}\leq\sum_{j=1}^{J^{*}}\|\phi^{j}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}\cdot\frac{\|W_{a}\|_{\frac{2d}{d-2}}^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}}{\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}},

which implies

(4.11)

\displaystyle\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}\leq\sum_{j=1}^{J^{*}}\|\phi^{j}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}.

This together with (
4.10
) gives

\displaystyle\biggl(\sum_{j=1}^{J^{*}}\|\phi^{j}\|_{X^{j}}^{2}\biggr)^{\frac{d}{d-2}}\leq\sum_{j=1}^{J^{*}}\|\phi^{j}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}.

Note also for
a<0
,
\|\phi\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}<\|\phi\|_{\dot{H}^{1}}
, this obviously implies that

J^{*}=1,\quad x_{n}^{1}\equiv 0,\text{ and }\limsup_{n\to\infty}\|r_{n}^{1}\|_{\frac{2d}{d-2}}=0.

Therefore, (
4.10
) and (
4.11
) imply
\|\phi^{1}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}=\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}},\ \|\phi^{1}\|_{\frac{2d}{d-2}}=\|W_{a}\|_{\frac{2d}{d-2}}
. Moreover

u_{n}=(\lambda_{n})^{-\frac{d-2}{2}}\phi^{1}\bigl(\frac{x}{\lambda_{n}}\bigr)+r_{n}^{1},\mbox{ \it{and} }\|r_{n}^{1}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}\to 0

follow from (
4.9
). Hence
\phi^{1}=e^{i\theta_{0}}\mu_{0}^{-\frac{d-2}{2}}W_{a}(\frac{x}{\mu_{0}})
for some
\theta_{0},\mu_{0}
. This contradicts to the last inequality in (
4.7
).
∎

Proposition 4.3
.

We can choose sufficiently small
R>0
so that if
\delta_{0}
is sufficiently small, then

(4.12)

\mu_{0}(t)\geq R

for
t
in the lifespan of
u
.

Proof.

Suppose not, then there exists
\left\{t_{n}\right\}
so that

(4.13)

\delta(t_{n})\to 0\quad\text{and}\quad\mu_{0}(t_{n})\to 0\quad\text{as}\quad n\to\mathbb{N}.

From (
4.2
) we then deduce that

(4.14)

\displaystyle f_{n}(x):=e^{-i\theta_{0}(t_{n})}\mu_{0}(t_{n})^{-\frac{d-2}{2}}u(t_{n},\mu_{0}(t_{n})^{-1}x)\to W_{a}(x)\quad\text{in}\quad\dot{H}_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

By mass conservation and (
4.13
), we also get

\|f_{n}\|_{L^{2}}\leq\mu_{0}(t_{n})M(u_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\to 1,n\to\infty.

But then by (
4.2
) we obtain
f_{n}\rightharpoonup W_{a}
in
L^{2}(B_{1}(0))
as
n\to\infty
. By the proposition of weak convergence, we have

\|W_{a}\|_{L^{2}(B_{1}(0))}\leq\lim_{n\to\infty}{\|f_{n}\|_{L^{2}(B_{1}(0))}}\to 0

which is a contradiction.
∎

Arguing as in
[
41
, Lemma 4.2]
, we may also obtain the following lemma:

Lemma 4.4
.

If
\delta_{0}>0
is sufficiently small, then there exist
C^{1}
functions
\theta:I_{0}\to\mathbb{R}
and
\mu:I_{0}\to[0,\infty)
so that

(4.15)

\|u(t)-e^{i\theta(t)}\mu(t)^{\frac{d-2}{2}}W_{a}(\mu(t)\cdot)\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}\ll 1.

Writing

g(t):=g_{1}(t)+ig_{2}(t)=e^{-i\theta(t)}[u(t)-e^{i\theta(t)}\mu(t)^{\frac{d-2}{2}}W_{a}(\mu(t)\cdot)],

and
W_{1}^{a}:=\tfrac{d-2}{2}W_{a}+x\cdot\nabla W_{a}\in\dot{H}_{a}^{1}
, we have

(4.16)

\left\langle\sqrt{\mathcal{L}_{a}}g_{2}(t),\sqrt{\mathcal{L}_{a}}[\mu(t)^{\frac{d-2}{2}}W_{a}(\mu(t)\cdot)]\right\rangle=\left\langle\sqrt{\mathcal{L}_{a}}g_{1}(t),\sqrt{\mathcal{L}_{a}}[\mu(t)^{\frac{d-2}{2}}W_{1}^{a}(\mu(t)\cdot)]\right\rangle\equiv 0.

To analysis the situation when
\delta(t)
is small,we need to expand the energy around the ground state. In fact, we will prove that

(4.17)

E_{a}^{c}(u(t))-E_{a}^{c}(W_{a})=\mathcal{F}(\mu^{-\frac{d-2}{2}}(t)g(t,\mu^{-1}(t)x))+o(\|g\|^{2}_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}),

where
\mathcal{F}
is the quadratic form on
\dot{H}_{a}^{1}
defined by

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(h):=\frac{1}{2}\left\langle(E_{a}^{c})^{\prime\prime}(W_{a})[h],h\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\nabla h|^{2}dx+\frac{a}{|x|^{2}}|h|^{2}dx

\displaystyle\hskip 8.61108pt-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}W_{a}^{\frac{4}{d-2}}[\frac{d+2}{d-2}|h_{1}|^{2}+|h_{2}|^{2}]dx,

with
h=h_{1}+ih_{2}\in\dot{H}_{a}^{1}
.

To prove (
4.17
), first observe that using (
4.15
), we may write

\begin{split}&E_{a}^{c}(u(t))-E_{a}^{c}(W_{a})=E_{a}^{c}(e^{-i\theta(t)}u(t))-E_{a}^{c}(\mu^{\frac{d-2}{2}}(t)W_{a}(\mu(t)\cdot))\\
&=\left\langle(E_{a}^{c})^{\prime}(\mu^{\frac{d-2}{2}}(t)W_{a}(\mu(t)\cdot),g(t)\right\rangle+\frac{d-2}{2}\left\langle(E_{a}^{c})^{\prime\prime}(\mu^{\frac{d-2}{2}}(t)W_{a}(\mu(t)\cdot)[g(t)],g(t)\right\rangle+o(\|g\|^{2}_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}).\end{split}

As
(E_{a}^{c})^{\prime}(\mu^{\frac{d-2}{2}}(t)W_{a}(\mu(t)\cdot)=0
, we obtain (
4.17
).

On the other hand, The quadratic form
\mathcal{F}
can be written

\mathcal{F}(h)=\tfrac{1}{2}\left\langle L_{1}h_{1},h_{1}\right\rangle+\tfrac{1}{2}\left\langle L_{2}h_{2},h_{2}\right\rangle,\quad\text{where}\quad h=h_{1}+ih_{2}\in\dot{H}_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

and
L_{1}
and
L_{2}
are two bounded linear operators defined on
\dot{H}_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
by

\displaystyle L_{1}u:=\mathcal{L}_{a}u-\frac{d+2}{d-2}W_{a}^{\frac{4}{d-2}}u,\quad L_{2}v

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{L}_{a}v-W_{a}^{\frac{4}{d-2}}v.

Setting
H^{a}:=\text{span}\left\{W_{a},iW_{a},W_{a}+x\cdot\nabla W_{a}\right\}
(viewed as a subspace of
\dot{H}_{a}^{1}
), we have the following (see
[
41
, Proposition 3.1]
):

Lemma 4.5
.

There exists
C>0
such that for radial
h=h_{1}+ih_{2}\in(H^{a})^{\bot}
,

\mathcal{F}(h)\geq C\|h\|^{2}_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}.

begin proving the estimates appearing in Proposition
4.1
.

Lemma 4.6
.

Let
(\theta(t),\mu(t))
and
g(t)
be as in Lemma
4.4
. Then

(4.18)

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|u(t,x)|^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}dx\lesssim\delta^{2}(t)\sim\|g(t)\|^{2}_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}.

Proof.

By (
4.17
) and
E_{a}(u(t))=E_{a}^{c}(W_{a})
,we see that

(4.19)

0=\mathcal{F}(\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)g(t,\mu^{-1}(t)\cdot))+\tfrac{d-1}{2d+2}\|u(t)\|^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}_{L^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}}+o(\|g\|^{2}_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}).

We now decompose
g(t)
as follows

(4.20)

\displaystyle g(t)=\alpha(t)\mu(t)^{\frac{d-2}{2}}W_{a}(\mu(t)\cdot)+h(t),

(4.21)

\displaystyle\alpha(t):=\frac{(\mu^{-\frac{d-2}{2}}(t)g(t,\mu^{-1}(t)\cdot),W_{a})_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}}{(W_{a},W_{a})_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}}.

Note that
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}
is chosen to guarantee that

(4.22)

\displaystyle(\mu(t)^{\frac{d-2}{2}}W_{a}(\mu(t)\cdot),h(t))_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}=0.

Using (
4.15
) and (
4.20
) we first observe that

(4.23)

|\alpha(t)|\lesssim\|g\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}\ll 1.

Moreover, by definition of
h
(cf. (
4.22
)) and (
4.16
) we have
\mu^{-\frac{d-2}{2}}(t)h(t,\mu^{-1}(t)\cdot)\in(H^{a})^{\bot}
.
Thus, Lemma
4.5
implies

\mathcal{F}(\mu^{-\frac{d-2}{2}}(t)h(t,\mu^{-1}(t)\cdot))\gtrsim\|h\|^{2}_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}.

Combining this with (
4.19
) we deduce that

\|h\|^{2}_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}+\|u\|^{\frac{2(d+1)}{d-1}}_{L^{\frac{2(d+1)}{d-1}}}\lesssim\alpha^{2}+|\alpha\langle L_{1}W_{a},\mu^{-\frac{d-2}{2}}(t)h(t,\mu^{-1}(t)\cdot)\rangle|+o(\|g\|^{2}_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}).

Notice that
L_{2}(W_{a})=0
, so that
L_{1}W_{a}=\frac{4}{d-2}\mathcal{L}_{a}W_{a}
. Since (recalling (
4.22
))

\langle L_{1}W,\mu^{-\frac{d-2}{2}}(t)h(t,\mu^{-1}(t)\cdot)\rangle=-\tfrac{4}{d-2}(W_{a},\mu^{-\frac{d-2}{2}}(t)h(t,\mu^{-1}(t)\cdot))_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}=0,

the inequality above shows

\|h\|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{1}}+\|u\|^{\frac{2(d+1)}{d-1}}_{L^{\frac{2(d+1)}{d-1}}}\lesssim\alpha^{2}+o(\|h\|^{2}_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}).

In particular,

(4.24)

\|h(t)\|^{2}_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}\lesssim\alpha^{2}(t)\quad\text{and}\quad\|u(t)\|^{\frac{2(d+1)}{d-1}}_{L^{\frac{2(d+1)}{d-1}}}\lesssim\alpha^{2}(t).

On the other hand, by the orthogonality condition (
4.22
) we obtain

\|g(t)\|^{2}_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}=\alpha^{2}(t)\|W_{a}\|^{2}_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}+\|h(t)\|^{2}_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}},

which implies by (
4.23
) and (
4.24
) that
\|g\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}\sim|\alpha|
.

Finally, combining (
4.22
)) and (
4.24
) we obtain

\displaystyle\delta(t)

\displaystyle=\bigl|\|W_{a}\|^{2}_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}-\|[(1+\alpha)\mu(t)^{\frac{d-2}{2}}W_{a}(\mu(t)\cdot)+h]\|^{2}_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}\bigr|

\displaystyle=2|\alpha|\|W_{a}\|^{2}_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}+\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{2}),

which shows
\delta\sim|\alpha|
. Combining the estimates above, we have

\|u(t)\|^{\frac{2(d+1)}{d-1}}_{L^{\frac{2(d+1)}{d-1}}}\lesssim\alpha^{2}(t)\sim\delta^{2}(t)\sim\|g(t)\|^{2}_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}.

∎

Lemma 4.7
.

Under the conditions of Lemma
4.6
, if
\delta_{0}
is sufficiently small, then

(4.25)

\frac{1}{\mu(t)}\lesssim\delta(t)^{\frac{2d-2}{\beta(d-2)(d+1)}}\quad\text{for all $t\in I_{0}$}.

Proof.

By Proposition
4.3
, we have that if
\delta_{0}
is sufficiently small, then

\delta(t)<\delta_{0}\Rightarrow\mu(t)\geq R.

Recall that

(4.26)

\displaystyle W_{a}(x)=[d(d-2)\beta^{2}]^{\frac{d-2}{4}}\biggl(\frac{|x|^{\beta-1}}{1+|x|^{2\beta}}\biggr)^{\frac{d-2}{2}},\;\beta=\sqrt{1+(\tfrac{2}{d-2})^{2}a}.

We see that

(4.27)

\displaystyle\mu(t)^{\frac{d-2}{2}}W_{a}(\mu(t)x)\gtrsim(\mu(t))^{-\frac{\beta(d-2)}{2}}\mbox{ for all $|x|\leq 1$ and $t\in I_{0}$.}

On the other hand, by Hölder and Sobolev, we get

\displaystyle\|e^{-i\theta(t)}[u(t)-e^{i\theta(t)}\mu(t)^{\frac{d-2}{2}}W_{a}(\mu(t)\cdot)]\|_{L^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}(B(0,1))}

\displaystyle=\|g(t)\|_{L^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}(B(0,1))}\lesssim\|g(t)\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}}\lesssim\|g(t)\|_{\dot{H}^{1}}\lesssim\|g(t)\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}\lesssim\delta(t).

Thus, by (
4.18
) we deduce

\|\mu(t)^{\frac{d-2}{2}}W_{a}(\mu(t)\cdot)]\|_{L^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}(B(0,1))}\lesssim\delta(t)+\delta(t)^{\frac{d-1}{d+1}}\lesssim\delta(t)^{\frac{d-1}{d+1}}.

Now, combining (
4.27
) and the inequality above we get

\mu(t)^{-\frac{\beta(d-2)(d+1)}{d-1}}\lesssim\|\mu(t)^{\frac{d-2}{2}}W_{a}(\mu(t)\cdot)]\|^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}_{L^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}(B(0,1))}\lesssim\delta(t)^{2}.

∎

Lemma 4.8
.

Under the conditions of Lemma
4.6
, we have the following estimate

(4.28)

|\tfrac{\mu^{\prime}(t)}{\mu(t)}|\lesssim\mu^{2}(t)\delta(t)\quad\text{for all $t\in I_{0}$}.

Proof.

We define following notation

W^{a}_{[\mu(t)]}(x)=\mu(t)^{\frac{d-2}{2}}W_{a}(\mu(t)x),\quad W^{a}_{1,[\mu(t)]}(x)=\mu(t)^{\frac{d-2}{2}}W^{a}_{1}(\mu(t)x),

where
W_{1}^{a}=\frac{d-2}{2}W_{a}+x\cdot\nabla W_{a}
. In this notation, we have

g(t)=e^{-i\theta(t)}[u(t)-e^{i\theta(t)}W^{a}_{[\mu(t)]}].

By equation (
CNLSa
), we can write

(4.29)

\begin{split}i\partial_{t}g+\Delta g-\frac{a}{|x|^{2}}g-\theta^{\prime}g-\theta^{\prime}W^{a}_{[\mu(t)]}+i\tfrac{\mu^{\prime}(t)}{\mu(t)}W^{a}_{1,[\mu(t)]}\\
-|u|^{\frac{4}{d-1}}e^{-i\theta}u+[f(e^{-i\theta}u)-f(W^{a}_{[\mu(t)]})]=0,\end{split}

where
f(z)=|z|^{\frac{4}{d-2}}z
. Next we observe that for
\varphi
with
\mathcal{L}_{a}\varphi\in L^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}
, we have

\displaystyle|(f(e^{-i\theta}u)

\displaystyle-f(W^{a}_{[\mu(t)]}),\varphi)_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}|

(4.30)

\displaystyle=|(f(e^{-i\theta}u)-f(W^{a}_{[\mu(t)]}),\mathcal{L}_{a}\varphi)_{L^{2}}|

\displaystyle\leq\|e^{-i\theta}u-W^{a}_{[\mu(t)]}\|_{L^{{\frac{2d}{d-2}}}}[\|e^{-i\theta}u\|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}_{L^{{\frac{2d}{d-2}}}}+\|W_{a}\|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}_{L^{{\frac{2d}{d-2}}}}]\|\mathcal{L}_{a}\varphi\|_{L^{{\frac{2d}{d-2}}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta(t)\|\mathcal{L}_{a}\varphi\|_{L^{{\frac{2d}{d-2}}}}.

Moreover, by Hölder’s inequality,
L^{p}
interpolation, conservation of mass, and (
4.18
),

\displaystyle|(|u|^{\frac{4}{d-1}}u,\varphi)_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}|

\displaystyle=|(|u|^{\frac{4}{d-1}}u,\mathcal{L}_{a}\varphi)_{L^{2}}|

\displaystyle\leq\||u|^{\frac{4}{d-1}}u\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}}\|\mathcal{L}_{a}\varphi\|_{L^{{\frac{2d}{d-2}}}}

\displaystyle\leq\|u\|_{L^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}}\|u\|^{\frac{4}{d-1}}_{L^{\frac{4d^{2}+4d}{2d^{2}-d-1}}}\|\mathcal{L}_{a}\varphi\|_{L^{{\frac{2d}{d-2}}}}

\displaystyle\leq{\|u\|}_{L^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}}^{\frac{(d+1)^{2}}{d^{2}-d}}{\|u\|}_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{d}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta(t)^{\frac{d+1}{d-1}}\|\mathcal{L}_{a}\varphi\|_{L^{{\frac{2d}{d-2}}}}

(4.31)

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta(t)\|\mathcal{L}_{a}\varphi\|_{L^{{\frac{2d}{d-2}}}}.

Note also that

(4.32)

\displaystyle|(\mathcal{L}_{a}g,W^{a}_{[\mu(t)]})_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}|=|(g,\mathcal{L}_{a}W^{a}_{[\mu(t)]})_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}|\leq\mu^{2}(t)\|g\|_{\dot{H}^{1}}\|\mathcal{L}_{a}W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}\lesssim\mu^{2}(t)\delta(t),

(4.33)

\displaystyle|(\mathcal{L}_{a}g,W^{a}_{1,[\mu(t)]})_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}|\leq\mu^{2}(t)\|g\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}\|\mathcal{L}_{a}W_{1}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}\lesssim\mu^{2}(t)\delta(t).

We will now prove that

(4.34)

\displaystyle|\theta^{\prime}|\lesssim\mu^{2}(t)\delta(t)+|\tfrac{\mu^{\prime}(t)}{\mu(t)}|\delta(t).

In fact, by the orthogonality condition (
4.16
) we deduce that

\displaystyle|(i\partial_{t}g,W^{a}_{[\mu(t)]})_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}|

\displaystyle=|\IM(g,\partial_{t}W^{a}_{[\mu(t)]})_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}|

\displaystyle=\left|\tfrac{\mu^{\prime}(t)}{\mu(t)}\right||\IM(g,W^{a}_{[1,\mu(t)]})_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}|\lesssim\left|\tfrac{\mu^{\prime}(t)}{\mu(t)}\right|\delta(t).

Thus, taking the
\dot{H}_{a}^{1}
inner product of (
4.29
) with
W^{a}_{[\mu(t)]}
and using (
4.32
), (
4.31
), and (
4.30
) (with
\varphi=W^{a}_{[\mu(t)]}
), we obtain

|\theta^{\prime}|\lesssim|\theta^{\prime}|\delta(t)+\mu^{2}(t)\delta(t)+|\tfrac{\mu^{\prime}(t)}{\mu(t)}|\delta(t),

where we have used that
\|\mathcal{L}_{a}W^{a}_{[\mu(t)]}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}=\mu^{2}(t)\|\mathcal{L}_{a}W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}
. As
\delta(t)\ll 1
, the estimate above yields (
4.34
).

Next we claim that

(4.35)

\displaystyle\left|\tfrac{\mu^{\prime}(t)}{\mu(t)}\right|\lesssim|\theta^{\prime}|\delta(t)+\mu^{2}(t)\delta(t)+|\tfrac{\mu^{\prime}(t)}{\mu(t)}|\delta(t).

Indeed, taking the
\dot{H}_{a}^{1}
inner product of (
4.29
) with
i\,W^{a}_{1,[\mu(t)]}
, the estimates
(
4.31
) and (
4.30
) (with
\varphi=i\,W^{a}_{1,[\mu(t)]}
) and (
4.33
) yield

\left|\tfrac{\mu^{\prime}(t)}{\mu(t)}\right|\lesssim|(i\partial_{t}g,iW^{a}_{1,[\mu(t)]})_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}|+\mu^{2}(t)\delta(t)+|\theta^{\prime}|\delta(t).

In addition, by the orthogonality condition (
4.16
) we have

\displaystyle|(i

\displaystyle\partial_{t}g,iW^{a}_{1,[\mu(t)]})_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}|=|(g,\partial_{t}W^{a}_{1,[\mu(t)]})_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}|

\displaystyle\lesssim|\tfrac{\mu^{\prime}(t)}{\mu(t)}|\|g\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}\|\tfrac{d-2}{2}W_{1}^{a}+x\cdot\nabla W_{1}^{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}\lesssim|\tfrac{\mu^{\prime}(t)}{\mu(t)}|\delta(t),

which implies claim (
4.35
). Putting together (
4.34
) and (
4.35
) yields (
4.28
).
∎

Proposition
4.1
now follows from Lemmas
4.4
,
4.6
,
4.7
, and
4.8
.

5.
Blow-up for sub-threhold solution:the proof of Theorem
1.11
(ii)

In this section, we prove the
blow-up result of Theorem
1.11
(ii).

By Lemma
2.18
,
we have

\displaystyle\partial^{2}_{t}V_{R}(t)=

\displaystyle\;4\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}w_{R}^{\prime\prime}(r)\big|\nabla u(t,x)\big|^{2}+\frac{ax}{|x|^{4}}\cdot\nabla w_{R}|u|^{2}\;dx-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\Delta^{2}w_{R})(x)|u(t,x)|^{2}\;dx

\displaystyle-\frac{4}{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\Delta w_{R})|u(t,x)|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}\;dx+\frac{4}{d+1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\Delta w_{R})|u(t,x)|^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}\;dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;4\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(2|\nabla u(t)|^{2}+2\frac{a}{|x|^{2}}|u(t)|^{2}-2|u(t)|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}+\tfrac{2d}{d+1}|u(t)|^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}\right)\;dx

\displaystyle+\frac{c}{R^{2}}\int_{R\leq|x|\leq 3R}\big|u(t)\big|^{2}\;dx+c\int_{R\leq|x|\leq 3R}\left(\big|u(t)\big|^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}+\big|u(t)\big|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}\right)\;dx.

By the radial Sobolev inequality, we have

\displaystyle\big\|f\big\|_{L^{\infty}(|x|\geq R)}\leq

\displaystyle cR^{-\frac{d-1}{2}}\big\|f\big\|^{1/2}_{L^{2}(|x|\geq R)}\big\|\nabla f\big\|^{1/2}_{L^{2}(|x|\geq R)}.

Therefore, by mass conservation and Young’s inequality, we know that for any
{\varepsilon}>0
there exist sufficiently large
R
such that

\displaystyle\partial^{2}_{t}V_{R}(t)\leq

\displaystyle 4K_{a}(u(t))+{\varepsilon}\big\|u(t,x)\big\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{2}+{\varepsilon}^{2}

(5.1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{16d}{d-2}E_{a}(u)-\big(\frac{16}{d-2}-{\varepsilon}\big)\big\|u(t)\big\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{2}-\frac{8d}{(d+1)(d-2)}\big\|u(t)\big\|^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}_{L^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}}+{\varepsilon}^{2}.

By
K_{a}(u)<0
, mass and energy conservations, Lemma
3.14
and
the continuity argument, we know that for any
t\in I
, we have

\displaystyle K_{a}(u(t))\leq-\frac{2d}{d-2}\left(m_{a}-E_{a}(u(t))\right)<0.

By Lemma
3.9
, we have

\displaystyle m_{a}\leq H_{a}(u(t))<\frac{1}{d}\big\|u(t)\big\|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}},

where we have used the fact that
K_{a}(u(t))<0
in the second inequality. By the fact
m=\frac{1}{d}\left(C_{GN}\right)^{-d}
and the sharp Sobolev inequality, we have

\displaystyle\big\|u(t)\big\|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}\geq\left(C_{GN}\right)^{-\frac{2d}{d-2}}\big\|u(t)\big\|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}}>\left(dC_{GN}\right)^{\frac{d}{d-2}},

which implies that
\big\|u(t)\big\|^{2}_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}>dm_{a}
.

In addition, by
E_{a}(u_{0})<m_{a}
and energy conservation, there exists
\delta_{1}>0
such that

E_{a}(u(t))\leq(1-\delta_{1})m_{a}
. Thus, if we choose
{\varepsilon}
sufficiently small, we have

\displaystyle\partial^{2}_{t}V_{R}(t)\leq\frac{16}{d-2}(1-\delta_{1})m_{a}-d\big(\frac{16}{d-2}-{\varepsilon}\big)m_{a}+{\varepsilon}^{2}\leq-\frac{8d}{d-2}\delta_{1}m_{a},

which implies that
u
must blow up at finite time. ∎

6.
Blow-up for threshold solution:the proof of Theorem
1.12
(ii)

In this section we prove Theorem
1.12
(ii). Before giving the proof, we first give a notation which will be used in this section. Denote by
W_{[\lambda(t)]}^{a}:=\lambda(t)^{\frac{d-2}{2}}W_{a}(\lambda(t)\cdot)
. We proceed by proving several lemmas. We still use the local virial identity in subsection
2.3
.

Lemma 6.1
.

Suppose
u
is a solution to (
CNLSa
) satisfying

(6.1)

E_{a}(u)=E_{a}^{c}(W_{a})\quad\text{and}\quad\|u_{0}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}>\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}.

If
u
is forward-global, i.e.
[0,+\infty)\subset I
, then

(6.2)

\displaystyle|I_{R}[u(t)]|\lesssim R^{2}\delta(t)\quad\text{for all $t\geq 0$.}\quad

Proof.

First, we fix a
\delta_{1}\in(0,\delta_{0})
sufficiently small and prove the estimate in the following two cases
\delta(u(t))\geq\delta_{1}
or
\delta(u(t))<\delta_{1}
separately. In the first case, we use the Hölder and Hardy inequality along with Sobolev embedding to estimate

\displaystyle|I_{R}[u(t)]|

\displaystyle\lesssim R^{2}\|\nabla u(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim R^{2}\{\delta(t)+\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{2}\}\lesssim R^{2}\{1+\delta_{1}^{-1}\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{2}\}\delta(t).

In the second case, since
W_{a}
is real, thus we can deduce to

\displaystyle|I_{R}[u(t)]|

\displaystyle\leq\left|2\IM\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\nabla w_{R}(\bar{u}\nabla u-e^{-i\theta(t)}W^{a}_{[\lambda(t)]}\nabla[e^{i\theta(t)}W^{a}_{[\lambda(t)]}])dx\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim R^{2}[\|u(t)\|_{\dot{H}^{1}_{x}}+\|W^{a}_{[\lambda(t)]}\|_{\dot{H}^{1}}]\|u(t)-e^{i\theta(t)}W^{a}_{[\lambda(t)]}\|_{\dot{H}^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{W_{a}}R^{2}\|g(t)\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}\lesssim_{W_{a}}R^{2}\delta(t),

where we use (
4.3
) with
\lambda(t):=\mu(t)
for
t\in I_{0}
in the last inequality. ∎

In the following lemma, we use the same notation as in the proof of local virial identity, i.e. Section
2.3
.

Lemma 6.2
.

Under assumptions of Lemma
6.1
, if
u
is global in forward direction and radial, then there exists
R_{1}\geq 1
such that for
R\geq R_{1}
and
t\geq 0
, the following estimate holds

(6.3)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}V_{R}[u(t)]

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{20-2d}{d-2}\delta(t).

Proof.

We choose a parameter
R>1
which will be determined below. For
\delta_{1}\in(0,\delta_{0})
sufficiently small, we use the localized virial identity (see Section
2.3
) with
\chi(t)
satisfying

\chi(t)=\begin{cases}1&\quad\delta(t)<\delta_{1}\\
0&\quad\delta(t)\geq\delta_{1}.\end{cases}

Recall that
E_{a}^{c}(W_{a})=\frac{1}{d}\|W_{a}\|^{2}_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}
. By the kinetic energy condition (
6.1
), we have

{{F^{c}_{\infty}}}[u(t)]=-\frac{16}{d-2}\delta(t)-\frac{8d^{2}-8d}{d^{2}-d-2}\|u(t)\|^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}_{L^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}}.

Then using Lemma
2.20
, we get

(6.4)

\tfrac{d}{dt}I_{R}[u(t)]=F^{c}_{\infty}[u(t)]+\mathcal{E}(t)=-\frac{16}{d-2}\delta(t)-\frac{8d^{2}-8d}{d^{2}-d-2}\|u(t)\|^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}_{L^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}}+\mathcal{E}(t)

with

(6.5)

\mathcal{E}(t)=\begin{cases}F_{R}[u(t)]-F^{c}_{\infty}[u(t)]&\quad\text{if $\delta(t)\geq\delta_{1}$},\\
F_{R}[u(t)]-F^{c}_{\infty}[u(t)]-\mathcal{M}[t]&\quad\text{if $\delta(t)<\delta_{1}$},\end{cases}

where

(6.6)

\mathcal{M}(t):=F^{c}_{R}[e^{i\theta(t)}\lambda(t)^{\frac{d-2}{2}}W_{a}(\lambda(t)x)]-F^{c}_{\infty}[e^{i\theta(t)}\lambda(t)^{\frac{d-2}{2}}W_{a}(\lambda(t)x)]

and
\lambda(t):=\mu(t)
for
t\in I_{0}
.
Now by (
4.2
) we have
{u}_{[\theta(t),\lambda(t)]}=W_{a}+V_{a}
, where

{u}_{[\theta(t),\lambda(t)]}=e^{-i\theta(t)}\lambda(t)^{-\frac{d-2}{2}}u(t,\lambda(t)^{-1}\cdot)\quad\text{and}\quad\|V_{a}\|_{\dot{H}^{1}}\sim\|V_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}\sim\delta(t).

We now claim that

(6.7)

\displaystyle\lambda_{\text{inf}}:=\inf\left\{{\lambda(t):\ t\geq 0,\ \delta(t)\leq\delta_{1}}\right\}>0

for
\delta_{1}\in(0,\delta_{0})
sufficiently small. Indeed, the mass conservation implies that

\displaystyle M({u}_{0})

\displaystyle\gtrsim\int_{|x|\leq\tfrac{1}{\lambda(t)}}|u(x,t)|^{2}dx=\tfrac{1}{\lambda(t)^{2}}\int_{|x|\leq 1}|u_{[\theta(t),\lambda(t)]}|^{2}dx

\displaystyle\gtrsim\tfrac{1}{\lambda(t)^{2}}\left(\int_{|x|\leq 1}|W_{a}|^{2}dx-\int_{|x|\leq 1}|V_{a}|^{2}dx\right).

Since

\|V_{a}(t)\|_{L^{2}(|x|\leq 1)}\lesssim\|V_{a}(t)\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}(|x|\leq 1)}\lesssim\|V_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}\lesssim\delta(t),

it follows that

M({u}_{0})\gtrsim\tfrac{1}{\lambda(t)^{2}}\left(\int_{|x|\leq 1}|W_{a}|^{2}dx-C\delta^{2}(t)\right).

Choosing
\delta_{1}
sufficiently small yields (
6.7
).

Next we show that there exists
R_{*}
so that for
R\geq R_{*}
,

(6.8)

\displaystyle|\mathcal{E}(t)|\leq 2\delta(t)\quad

\displaystyle\text{ for $t\geq 0$ with $\delta(t)<\delta_{1}$}.

To simplify the notation, we set
\tilde{W}_{a}(t)=e^{i\theta(t)}\lambda(t)^{\frac{d-2}{2}}W_{a}(\lambda(t)x)
. Then

(6.9)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(t)

\displaystyle=-8\int_{|x|\geq R}[(|\nabla u|^{2}-(|\nabla\tilde{W}_{a}(t)|^{2})]\,dx+8\int_{|x|\geq R}[|u|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}-|\tilde{W}_{a}(t)|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}]\,dx

(6.10)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{R\leq|x|\leq 2R}(-\Delta\Delta w_{R})[|u|^{2}-|\tilde{W}_{a}(t)|^{2}]\,dx

(6.11)

\displaystyle\quad+4\int_{|x|>R}(\frac{a}{|x|^{4}}x\nabla w_{R}|u(t)|^{2}-\frac{a}{|x|^{4}}x\nabla w_{R}|W(t)|^{2})dx.

(6.12)

\displaystyle\quad-4\int_{|x|>R}(\frac{2a|u(t)|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}-\frac{2a|\tilde{W}_{a}(t)|^{2}}{|x|^{2}})dx.

(6.13)

\displaystyle\quad-\tfrac{4}{d}\int_{|x|\geq R}\Delta[w_{R}(x)](|u|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}-|\tilde{W}_{a}(t)|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}})\,dx

(6.14)

\displaystyle\quad+4\sum_{j,k}\RE\int_{|x|\geq R}[\overline{u_{j}}u_{k}-\overline{\partial_{j}\tilde{W}_{a}(t)}\partial_{k}\tilde{W}_{a}(t)]\partial_{jk}[w_{R}(x)]\,dx

(6.15)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{4}{d+1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Delta[w_{R}(x)]|u|^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}\,dx

for all
t\geq 0
such that
\delta(t)<\delta_{1}
.

As
|\Delta\Delta w_{R}|\lesssim 1/|x|^{2}
,
|\nabla w_{R}|\lesssim|x|
,
|\partial_{jk}[w_{R}]|\lesssim 1
and
|\Delta[w_{R}]|\lesssim 1
, we obtain that (
6.9
)–(
6.14
) can be estimated by terms of the form

\displaystyle\bigg\{\|u(t)\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{1}(|x|\geq R)}+\bigg\|\frac{u(t)}{x}\bigg\|_{L^{2}(|x|\geq R)}+\|\tilde{W}_{a}(t)\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{1}(|x|\geq R)}+\bigg\|\frac{\tilde{W}_{a}(t)}{x}\bigg\|_{L^{2}(|x|\geq R)}

(6.16)

\displaystyle+\|u(t)\|^{\frac{d+2}{d-2}}_{L_{x}^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}(|x|\geq R)}+\big\|\tilde{W}_{a}(t)\big\|^{\frac{d+2}{d-2}}_{L_{x}^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}(|x|\geq R)}\bigg\}\|g(t)\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}},

where
g(t)=e^{-i\theta(t)}[{u}(t)-\tilde{W}_{a}(t)]
. Moreover, since

\displaystyle\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{1}(|x|\geq R)}=O\big(R^{-\frac{\beta(d-2)}{2}}\big),\quad\|W_{a}\|_{L_{x}^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}(|x|\geq R)}=O\big(R^{-\frac{\beta(d-2)}{2}}\big),

\displaystyle\bigg\|\frac{W_{a}}{x}\bigg\|_{L_{x}^{2}(|x|\geq R)}=O\big(R^{-\frac{\beta(d-2)}{2}}\big),

we deduce that

\displaystyle\|u(t)\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{1}(|x|\geq R)}+\bigg\|\frac{u(t)}{x}\bigg\|_{L^{2}(|x|\geq R)}+\|\tilde{W}_{a}(t)\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{1}(|x|\geq R)}+\bigg\|\frac{\tilde{W}_{a}(t)}{x}\bigg\|_{L^{2}(|x|\geq R)}

\displaystyle+\|u(t)\|^{\frac{d+2}{d-2}}_{L_{x}^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}(|x|\geq R)}+\big\|\tilde{W}_{a}(t)\big\|^{\frac{d+2}{d-2}}_{L_{x}^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}(|x|\geq R)}

\displaystyle\leq\delta(t)+\delta(t)^{\frac{\beta(d+2)}{2}}+O\big((\lambda(t)R)^{-\frac{\beta(d+2)}{2}}\big)+O\big((\lambda(t)R)^{-\frac{\beta(d+2)}{2}}\big).

Thus, for
\delta_{1}\in(0,\delta_{0})
sufficiently small and
R
sufficiently large,

|\eqref{Decomp11}|+|\eqref{Decomp33}|+|\eqref{Decomp44}|+|\eqref{Decomp77}|+|\eqref{Decomp88}|+|\eqref{Decomp55}|\leq\delta(t)\quad\text{when $\delta(t)<\delta_{1}$.}\quad

By taking
\delta_{1}
smaller if necessary, Proposition
4.1
implies

\tfrac{4}{d+1}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Delta[w_{R}(x)]|u|^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}dx\right|\lesssim\delta(t)^{2}\lesssim{\delta}_{1}\delta(t)\leq\delta(t),

Combining the estimates above, we have (
6.8
).

Now, suppose that
\delta(t)\geq\delta_{1}
. We show that for large
R
, we have

(6.17)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(t)\leq\delta(t)+\frac{8d}{d+1}\|u(t)\|^{\frac{2d+2}{d-2}}_{L^{\frac{2d+2}{d-2}}}.

First, we recall the radial Sobolev embedding estimate:

\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(|x|\geq R)}\lesssim R^{-\frac{d-1}{2}}\|f\|^{1/2}_{L^{2}}\|\nabla f\|^{1/2}_{L^{2}}.

For
\delta(t)\geq\delta_{1}
, we may write

(6.18)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(t)=

\displaystyle\int_{|x|\geq R}(-\Delta\Delta w_{R})|u|^{2}+4\RE\overline{u_{j}}u_{k}\partial_{jk}[w_{R}]-8|\nabla u|^{2}dx

(6.19)

\displaystyle+\int_{|x|>R}(\frac{4a}{|x|^{4}}x\nabla\phi_{R}|u(t)|^{2}-\frac{8a|u(t)|^{2}}{|x|^{2}})dx.

(6.20)

\displaystyle-\tfrac{4}{d}\int_{|x|\geq R}\Delta[w_{R}(x)]|u|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}dx+8\int_{|x|\geq R}|u|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}dx

(6.21)

\displaystyle+\tfrac{4}{d+1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Delta[w_{R}(x)]|u|^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}dx.

Choosing
w_{R}
so that
4\partial^{2}_{r}w_{R}\leq 8
, we have

\int_{|x|\geq R}(4\RE\overline{u_{j}}u_{k}\partial_{jk}[w_{R}]-8|\nabla u|^{2})dx\leq 0.

Moreover, since
|\nabla w_{R}|\lesssim|x|
,
|\Delta\Delta w_{R}|\lesssim 1/|x|^{2}
, by (
6.18
)–(
6.21
) and radial Sobolev inequality, we deduce

\mathcal{E}(t)\leq CR^{-2}+CR^{-\frac{2d-2}{d-2}}\|u(t)\|^{\frac{2}{d-2}}_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}+\frac{4}{d+1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Delta[w_{R}(x)]|u|^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}dx.

Furthermore,

\displaystyle\frac{4}{d+1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Delta[w_{R}(x)]|u|^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}dx

\displaystyle=\frac{8d}{d+1}\int_{|x|\leq R}|u|^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}dx+\int_{|x|\geq R}\Delta[w_{R}(x)]|u|^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}dx

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8d}{d+1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|u|^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}dx+CR^{-2}\|u(t)\|^{\frac{2}{d-1}}_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}.

Combining estimates above we deduce

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(t)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8d}{d+1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|u|^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}dx+CR^{-2}+CR^{-\frac{2d-2}{d-2}}\|u(t)\|^{\frac{2}{d-2}}_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}+CR^{-2}\|u(t)\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8d}{d+1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|u|^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}dx+C(\delta_{1},\|\tilde{W}_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}})R^{-\frac{2}{d-1}}\delta(t)

for
\delta(t)\geq\delta_{1}
.
Thus (
6.17
) holds for
R
large. Combining (
6.8
) and (
6.17
) now yields (
6.3
).∎

Lemma 6.3
.

Given
{u}
as in Lemma
6.2
, there exists
C>1
and
c>0
such that

(6.22)

\displaystyle\int^{\infty}_{t}\delta(s)\,ds\leq Ce^{-ct}\quad\text{for all $t\geq 0$.}\quad

Proof.

Fix
R\geq R_{1}
(where
R_{1}
is as in Lemma
6.2
). Writing

(6.23)

\displaystyle V_{R}(t)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}w_{R}(x)|u(t,x)|^{2}\,dx,

we have that
\tfrac{d}{dt}V_{R}(t)=I_{R}[{u}]
. By Lemma
6.2
, we have

\tfrac{d^{2}}{dt^{2}}V_{R}(t)=\tfrac{d}{dt}I_{R}[u(t)]\leq-\tfrac{20-2d}{d-2}\delta(t).

Thus, since
\tfrac{d^{2}}{dt^{2}}V_{R}(t)<0
and
V_{R}(t)>0
for all
t\geq 0
, it follows that
I_{R}[u(t)]=\tfrac{d}{dt}V_{R}(t)>0
for all
t\geq 0
. Thus (
6.2
) implies

\tfrac{20-2d}{d-2}\int^{T}_{t}\delta(s)\,ds\leq-\int^{T}_{t}\tfrac{d}{ds}I_{R}[u(s)]\,ds=I_{R}[u(t)]-I_{R}[u(T)]\leq I_{R}[u(t)]\leq CR^{2}\delta(t).

Sending
T\to\infty
, (
6.22
) now follows from Gronwall’s inequality.
∎

As a direct consequence of Lemma
6.3
, we have the following result.

Corollary 6.4
.

Under the assumptions of Lemma
6.1
, there exists a sequence
t_{n}\to\infty
such that
\delta(t_{n})\to 0
.

Proof of Theorem
1.12
(ii).

Suppose that
u
is a solution to (
CNLSa
) and satisfy the condition in Theorem
1.12
(ii). Without loss of generality, we assume that
u
is forward global. By Corollary
6.4
, there exists increasing
t_{n}\to\infty
such that
\delta(t_{n})\to 0.
Moreover, by Proposition
4.1
we deduce that (with
\lambda(t)=\mu(t)
for
t\in I_{0}
)

(6.24)

\displaystyle e^{-i\theta(t_{n})}\lambda(t_{n})^{-\frac{d-2}{2}}u(t_{n},\lambda(t_{n})^{-1}x)\to W_{a}(x)\quad\text{in $\dot{H}_{a}^{1}$.}\quad

Passing to a subsequence, we may assume that

\lim_{n\to+\infty}\lambda(t_{n})=\lambda_{*}\in[0,\infty].

We show that
\lambda_{*}<\infty
. By contradiction, we assume that
\lambda_{*}=\infty
. From (
6.24
) and a change of variables, we get that for any
C>0
,

\|u(t_{n})\|_{{L}^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}(|x|\geq C)}\to 0\quad\text{as $n\to+\infty$.}\quad

Fix
R\geq R_{1}
(cf. Lemma
6.2
) and
{\varepsilon}>0
and recall (
6.23
). Using Hölder’s inequality, we deduce

\displaystyle V_{R}(t_{n})

\displaystyle=\int_{|x|\leq{\varepsilon}}w_{R}(x)|u(t_{n},x)|^{2}dx+\int_{{\varepsilon}\leq|x|\leq 2R}w_{R}(x)|u(t_{n},x)|^{2}dx

\displaystyle\lesssim{\varepsilon}^{2}\|u(t_{n})\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{2}+R^{2}\|u(t_{n})\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}(|x|\geq{\varepsilon})}^{2}.

Sending
n\to\infty
and then
{\varepsilon}\to 0
, we get that

\lim_{n\to\infty}V_{R}(t_{n})=0.\

However, since
\tfrac{d}{dt}V_{R}>0
for all
t\geq 0
, it follows that
V_{R}(t)<0
for
t\geq 0
, which is a contradiction. Thus
\lambda_{*}<\infty
.

As
\delta(t_{n})\to 0
as
n\to\infty
, there exists
N\in\mathbb{N}
so that
\delta(t_{n})<\delta_{0}
for
n\geq N
. Now observe that Proposition
4.1
yields

\frac{1}{\lambda(t_{n})}\leq C\delta(t_{n})^{\frac{2d-2}{\beta(d-2)(d+1)}}\quad\text{for all $n\geq N$.}\quad

Taking
n\to\infty
, we obtain a contradiction, so that
u
blows up forward in time.

We can also obtain that
u
blows up backward in time by reversing the time direction. Hence, we complete the proof. ∎

7.
Construction of minimal blowup solutions

The goal of this section is to show that if Theorem
1.11
(i) fails, then we may construct a blowup solution with mass-energy in the region
\mathcal{K}_{a}
that obeys certain compactness properties. In the next section, we will utilize a localized virial argument to preclude the possibility of such a solution, thus finishing the proof of Theorem
1.11
(i).

7.1.
Embedding nonlinear profiles

In this subsection,we devote to construct non-scattering solutions to (
CNLSa
) with profiles
\phi_{n}
living either at tiny length scales (i.e. in the regime
\lambda_{n}\to 0
) or far from the origin relative to their length scales (i.e. in the regime
|\tfrac{x_{n}}{\lambda_{n}}|\to\infty
1
1

1

As a consequence of radiality in dimension
3
,
x_{n}\equiv 0
. Thus, this regime cannot occur. For the sake of uniformity in our proof, we will still discuss this regime in
d=3
.
), or both. Compared to (
NLS
), the situation in (
CNLSa
) will be more complicate because the translation symmetry is broken by the potential and scaling symmetry is broken by the double-power nonlinearity.So we must consider several limiting regimes and use approximation by a suitable underlying model in each case. The essential idea is that if
\lambda_{n}\to 0
, the disturbance term
|u|^{\frac{4}{d-1}}u
will become negligible, while if
|\tfrac{x_{n}}{\lambda_{n}}|\to\infty
, the inverse-potential term will become negligible. To be more procisely:

•

If
\lambda_{n}\to 0
and
x_{n}\equiv 0
, we will approximate by using solutions to the focusing energy-critical NLS with inverse-square potential, i.e.
(
NLSa
)
. For the scattering result, see Theorem
1.3
.

•

If
\lambda_{n}\equiv 1
and
|x_{n}|\to\infty
, we will approximate using solutions to the combined NLS
(
CNLS
)
. For the scattering result of (
CNLS
), see Theorem
1.2
.

•

If
\lambda_{n}\to 0
and
|\tfrac{x_{n}}{\lambda_{n}}|\to\infty
, we will approximate by using solutions to the focusing energy-critical NLS without potential (
NLS
). For the scattering result of (
NLS
), see Theorem
1.1
.

Now we state the key result in this section:

Proposition 7.1

(Embedding nonlinear profiles)
.

Fix
a>-\frac{(d-2)^{2}}{4}+\left(\frac{d-2}{d+2}\right)^{2}
.

Let
\mathcal{L}_{a}^{n}
be as in (
2.11
) corresponding to sequence

\{\tfrac{x_{n}}{\lambda_{n}}\}
, and let
\{t_{n}\}
satisfy
t_{n}\equiv 0
or
t_{n}\to\pm\infty
.

•

If
\lambda_{n}\equiv 1
,
|x_{n}|\to\infty
, then let
\phi\in H_{a}^{1}
satisfy
E_{0}(\phi)<E_{0}(W_{0})
,
\|\phi\|_{\dot{H}^{1}}<\|W_{0}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}
and define

\phi_{n}(x):=[e^{-it_{n}\mathcal{L}^{n}_{a}}\phi](x-x_{n}).

•

If
\lambda_{n}\to 0
,
|\tfrac{x_{n}}{\lambda_{n}}|\to\infty
, then let
\phi\in\dot{H}^{1}
satisfy
E_{0}^{c}(\phi)<E_{0}^{c}(W_{0})
,
\|\phi\|_{\dot{H}^{1}}<\|W_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{1}}

\phi_{n}(x):=\lambda_{n}^{-\frac{d-2}{2}}[e^{-it_{n}\mathcal{L}^{n}_{a}}P^{a}_{\geq\lambda_{n}^{\theta}}\phi](\tfrac{x-x_{n}}{\lambda_{n}}).

•

If
\lambda_{n}\to 0
,
x_{n}\equiv 0
, then let
\phi\in\dot{H}_{a}^{1}
satisfy
E_{a}^{c}(\phi)<E_{a}^{c}(W_{a})
,
\|\phi\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}<\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}

\phi_{n}(x):=\lambda_{n}^{-\frac{d-2}{2}}[e^{-it_{n}\mathcal{L}^{n}_{a}}P^{a}_{\geq\lambda_{n}^{\theta}}\phi](\tfrac{x-x_{n}}{\lambda_{n}}).

Then for
n
sufficiently large, there exists a global solution
v_{n}
to (
CNLSa
) with

v_{n}(0)=\phi_{n}\quad\text{and}\quad\|v_{n}\|_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim 1,

with the implicit constant depending on
\|\phi\|_{H^{1}}
if
\lambda_{n}\equiv 1
or
\|\phi\|_{\dot{H}^{1}}
if
\lambda_{n}\to 0
.

Moreover, for any
{\varepsilon}>0
there exist
N=N({\varepsilon})\in\mathbb{N}
and a smooth compactly supported function

\chi_{{\varepsilon}}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
such that for
n\geq N
,

(7.1)

\displaystyle\Big\|v_{n}(t,x)-\lambda^{-\frac{d-2}{2}}_{n}\chi_{{\varepsilon}}(\tfrac{t}{\lambda^{2}_{n}}+t_{n},\tfrac{x-x_{n}}{\lambda_{n}})\Big\|_{X(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle<{\varepsilon},

where

X\in\{L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}},L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d}},L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d}}_{t}\dot{H}_{x}^{1,\frac{2(d+2)}{d}}\}.

Remark 7.2
.

In the scenario in which
\lambda_{n}\equiv 1
, the approximation in (
7.1
) may also be taken to hold in Strichartz spaces of
L^{2}
regularity.

Proof.

We will label the three cases in the proposition (
7.1
) as

•

Scenario I:
\lambda_{n}\to 0
and
x_{n}\equiv 0
. In this case,
\phi\in\dot{H}^{1}
;

•

Scenario II:
\lambda_{n}\equiv 1
and
|x_{n}|\to\infty
. In this case,
\phi\in H^{1}
;

•

Scenario III:
\lambda_{n}\to 0
and
|\tfrac{x_{n}}{\lambda_{n}}|\to\infty
. In this case,
\phi\in\dot{H}^{1}
.

for any
\mu,\theta\in(0,1)
, we initially define

\psi_{n}=\begin{cases}P_{>\lambda_{n}^{\theta}}^{a}\phi&\text{in Scenario I,}\\
P_{\leq|x_{n}|^{\mu}}^{a}\phi&\text{in Scenario II,}\\
P_{\lambda_{n}^{\theta}\leq\cdot<|\frac{x_{n}}{\lambda_{n}}|^{\mu}}^{a}\phi&\text{in Scenario III.}\end{cases}

We also set

H=\begin{cases}-\Delta&\text{in Scenarios II and III},\\
\mathcal{L}_{a}&\text{in Scenario I}.\end{cases}

In the rest of the paper, we denote
H^{\frac{1}{2}}
by
\sqrt{H}
. Here,
H^{\frac{1}{2}}
is defined by standard functional calculus of
H
.

Establishment of approximate solutions, part 1.
We first define functions
w_{n}
and
w
as follows:

If
t_{n}\equiv 0
, then we define
w_{n}
and
w
as the global solutions to an appropriate NLS model with initial data
\psi_{n}
and
\phi
, respectively. In particular, in Scenario I, we use the model (
NLSa
) (focusing energy-critical NLS with inverse-square potential). In Scenario II, we use the model (
CNLS
) (Combined NLS without potential). Finally, in Scenario III, we use the model (
NLS
) (energy-critical NLS without potential).

If instead
t_{n}\to\pm\infty
, then we define
w_{n}
and
w
to be the solutions to the appropriate model (determined according to the three scenarios as above) satisfying

(7.2)

\|w_{n}-e^{-itH}\psi_{n}\|_{\dot{H}^{1}}\to 0\quad\text{and}\quad\|w-e^{-itH}\phi\|_{\dot{H}^{1}}\to 0

as
t\to\pm\infty
. Note that in either case (i.e.
t_{n}\equiv 0
or
t_{n}\to\pm\infty
),
w
has scattering states
w_{\pm}
as
t\to\pm\infty
in
\dot{H}^{1}
.

The solutions
w_{n}
and
w
we just established above obey

(7.3)

\|\sqrt{H}w_{n}\|_{S^{0}(\mathbb{R})}+\|\sqrt{H}w\|_{S^{0}(\mathbb{R})}\leq C(\|\phi\|_{\dot{H}^{1}})

uniformly in
n
. It also have the persistence of regularity at
L^{2}
level,that is,

(7.4)

\begin{cases}\|w_{n}\|_{S_{a}^{0}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim C(\|\phi\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}})\lambda_{n}^{-\theta}&\text{in Scenario I},\\
\|w_{n}\|_{S^{0}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim C(\|\phi\|_{H^{1}})&\text{in Scenario II},\\
\|w_{n}\|_{S^{0}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim C(\|\phi\|_{\dot{H}^{1}})\lambda_{n}^{-\theta}&\text{in Scenario III},\end{cases}

uniformly in
n
. In Scenarios II and III, we may futher use persistence of regularity to derive the following bounds

(7.5)

\||\nabla|^{s}w_{n}\|_{\dot{S}^{0}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim\bigl|\tfrac{x_{n}}{\lambda_{n}}\bigr|^{s\mu}

for any
0<s<1
.

Finally,by stability theory of equations (
NLSa
), (
CNLS
) and (
NLS
), we may also derive that in each case

(7.6)

\lim_{n\to\infty}\|\sqrt{H}[w_{n}-w]\|_{L_{t}^{q}L_{x}^{r}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}=0\quad\text{for all admissible}\quad(q,r).

Estabilishment of approximate solutions, part 2.
Next we define approximate solutions to (
CNLSa
) on
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
:

For each
n
, let
\chi_{n}
be a
C^{\infty}
function satisfying

\chi_{n}(x)=\begin{cases}0&|x_{n}+\lambda_{n}x|<\tfrac{1}{4}|x_{n}|\\
1&|x_{n}+\lambda_{n}x|>\tfrac{1}{2}|x_{n}|,\end{cases}\quad\text{with}\quad|\partial^{k}\chi_{n}(x)|\lesssim\bigl(\tfrac{\lambda_{n}}{|x_{n}|}\bigr)^{|k|}

uniformly in
x
.Especially,
\chi_{n}(x)\to 1
as
n\to\infty
for each
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. It’s worth noticing that in Scenario I, we have
x_{n}\equiv 0
, so that
\chi_{n}(x)\equiv 1
and the derivatives of
\chi_{n}
vanish identically.

For
T\geq 1
, In Scenario I,we define

\tilde{v}_{n,T}(t,x)=\lambda^{-\frac{d-2}{2}}_{n}[\chi_{n}w_{n}](\lambda^{-2}_{n}t,\lambda^{-1}_{n}(x-x_{n}))

In Scenario II and III,we will futher define

\tilde{v}_{n,T}(t,x)=\begin{cases}\lambda^{-\frac{d-2}{2}}_{n}[\chi_{n}w_{n}](\lambda^{-2}_{n}t,\lambda^{-1}_{n}(x-x_{n})),&|t|\leq\lambda^{2}_{n}T,\\
e^{-i(t-\lambda^{2}_{n}T)\mathcal{L}_{a}}\tilde{v}_{n,T}(\lambda^{2}_{n}T,x),&t>\lambda^{2}_{n}T,\\
e^{-i(t+\lambda^{2}_{n}T)\mathcal{L}_{a}}\tilde{v}_{n,T}(-\lambda^{2}_{n}T,x),&t<-\lambda^{2}_{n}T.\end{cases}

We emphasize that
\tilde{v}_{n,T}
are intended to be approximate solutions to (
CNLSa
), So we have the following error terms:

e_{n,T}:=(i\partial_{t}-\mathcal{L}_{a})\tilde{v}_{n,T}-|\tilde{v}_{n,T}|^{\frac{4}{d-2}}\tilde{v}_{n,T}+|\tilde{v}_{n,T}|^{\frac{4}{d-1}}\tilde{v}_{n,T}.

Conditions for stability lemma.
In order to apply the stability lemma (
2.11
),We need to establish the following estimates:

(7.7)

\displaystyle\limsup_{T\to\infty}\limsup_{n\to\infty}\bigl\{\|\tilde{v}_{n,T}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{1}_{x}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\|\tilde{v}_{n,T}\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}\bigr\}\lesssim 1,

(7.8)

\displaystyle\limsup_{T\to\infty}\limsup_{n\to\infty}\|\tilde{v}_{n,T}\|_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-1}}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim 1,

(7.9)

\displaystyle\limsup_{T\to\infty}\limsup_{n\to\infty}\|\tilde{v}_{n,T}(\lambda^{2}_{n}t_{n})-\phi_{n}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}=0,

(7.10)

\displaystyle\limsup_{T\to\infty}\limsup_{n\to\infty}\|\nabla e_{n,T}\|_{N(\mathbb{R})}=0,

where space-time norms are over
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

Proof of (
7.7
) and (
7.8
) (space-time uniform bounds).
First, by definition of
\tilde{v}_{n,T}
, Strichartz estimate and (
7.4
)

\displaystyle\|\tilde{v}_{n,T}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L_{x}^{2}}\lesssim\lambda_{n}\|\chi_{n}\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}\|w_{n}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L_{x}^{2}}\lesssim\lambda_{n}^{1-\theta}.

Similarly, noting that
\chi_{n}\equiv 1
in Scenario I and

\|\nabla\chi_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\tfrac{\lambda_{n}}{|x_{n}|}\to 0\quad\text{as}\quad n\to\infty

and

\|\nabla\chi_{n}\|_{L^{d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\tfrac{|x_{n}|}{\lambda_{n}}\tfrac{\lambda_{n}}{|x_{n}|}\lesssim 1.

in the remaining scenarios and using equivalence of Sobolev spaces, we may estimate

\displaystyle\|\nabla

\displaystyle\tilde{v}_{n,T}\|_{L_{t}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}L_{x}^{\frac{2d(d+2)}{d^{2}+4}}\cap L_{t}^{\infty}L_{x}^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla[\chi_{n}w_{n}]\|_{L_{t}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}L_{x}^{\frac{2d(d+2)}{d^{2}+4}}\cap L_{t}^{\infty}L_{x}^{2}}+\|[\chi_{n}w_{n}](\pm\lambda_{n}^{2}T)\|_{\dot{H}^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|w_{n}\|_{L_{t}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}\dot{H}^{1,\frac{2d(d+2)}{d^{2}+4}}\cap L_{t}^{\infty}\dot{H}^{1}}+\|\nabla\chi_{n}\|_{L^{d}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|w_{n}\|_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}}+\|\nabla\chi_{n}\|_{L^{d}}\|w_{n}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L_{x}^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 8.61108pt+\|\chi_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla w_{n}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L_{x}^{2}}\lesssim 1.

Thus, using Sobolev embedding as well, we derive (
7.7
). Finally, one can deduce
(
7.8
)
from
(
7.7
)
, persistence of the regularity(Lemma (
2.10
)) and
L^{p}
interpolation.

Proof of (
7.9
) (agreement of initial data).

First, if
t_{n}\equiv 0
, We change variables to obtain that

\|\nabla[\tilde{v}_{n,T}(0)-\phi_{n}]\|_{L^{2}}=\begin{cases}0&\text{in Scenario I}\\
\bigl\|\nabla[\chi_{n}P^{a}_{\leq|x_{n}|^{\mu}}\phi-\phi]\bigr\|_{L^{2}}&\text{in Scenario II}\\
\bigl\|\nabla[\chi_{n}P^{a}_{\lambda_{n}^{\theta}\leq\cdot<|\tfrac{x_{n}}{\lambda_{n}}|^{\mu}}\phi-P_{>\lambda_{n}^{\theta}}^{a}\phi]\bigr\|_{L^{2}}&\text{in Scenario III}.\end{cases}

We will only treat the most complex Scenario III in detail and omit details for the simpler Scenario II. In Scenario III we
rewrite

(7.11)

\displaystyle\chi_{n}P_{\lambda_{n}^{\theta}\leq\cdot<|\frac{x_{n}}{\lambda_{n}}|^{\mu}}^{a}\phi-P^{a}_{>\lambda_{n}^{\theta}}\phi

\displaystyle=(\chi_{n}-1)P_{>\lambda_{n}^{\theta}}^{a}\phi

(7.12)

\displaystyle\quad-\chi_{n}P_{>|\frac{x_{n}}{\lambda_{n}}|^{\mu}}^{a}\phi

For (
7.11
), we apply the Leibniz rule and write

\nabla\eqref{1jda}=\nabla\chi_{n}\cdot\phi+(1-\chi_{n})\nabla\phi-\nabla\chi_{n}\cdot P_{\leq\lambda_{n}^{\theta}}^{a}\phi-(1-\chi_{n})\nabla P_{\leq\lambda_{n}^{\theta}}^{a}\phi.

For the first two terms, we have the following estimate:

\displaystyle\|\nabla\chi_{n}\phi+(1-\chi_{n})\nabla\phi\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla\chi_{n}\|_{L^{d}}\|\phi\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}(\text{supp}(\nabla\chi_{n}))}+\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^{2}(\text{supp}(1-\chi_{n}))}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\phi\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}(\text{supp}(\nabla\chi_{n}))}+\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^{2}(\text{supp}(1-\chi_{n}))}=o(1)

as
n\to\infty
by Lebesgue’s dominated convergence theorem. For the last two terms, we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla

\displaystyle\chi_{n}\cdot P_{\leq\lambda_{n}^{\theta}}^{a}\phi+(1-\chi_{n})\nabla P_{\leq\lambda_{n}^{\theta}}^{a}\phi\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla\chi_{n}\|_{L^{d}}\|P_{\leq\lambda_{n}^{\theta}}^{a}\phi\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}}+\|\nabla P_{\leq\lambda_{n}^{\theta}}^{a}\phi\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|P_{\leq\lambda_{n}^{\theta}}^{a}\phi\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}}+\|\sqrt{\mathcal{L}_{a}}P_{\leq\lambda_{n}^{\theta}}^{a}\phi\|_{L^{2}}=o(1)

as
n\to\infty
by a standard density argument and using the fact that
\lambda_{n}\to 0
. Finally we also apply the Leibniz rule to (
7.12
) and then estimating as we just did for the last two terms shows that

\|\nabla[\chi_{n}P_{>|\frac{x_{n}}{\lambda_{n}}|^{\mu}}^{a}\phi]\|_{L^{2}}\to 0\quad\text{as}\quad n\to\infty,

as well. Thus we have estabilished

\lim_{n\to\infty}\|\nabla[\tilde{v}_{n,T}(0)-\phi_{n}]\|_{L^{2}}=0.

in the case
t_{n}\equiv 0
.

We next establish
\dot{H}^{1}
convergence in the case
t_{n}\to+\infty
(the case
t_{n}\to-\infty
is handled similarly,so we omit it). As we did before, we first change variables to see that

\displaystyle\|\tilde{v}_{n,T}

\displaystyle(\lambda_{n}^{2}t_{n})-\phi_{n}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\|\sqrt{\mathcal{L}_{a}}\bigl[w_{n}(t_{n})-e^{-it_{n}\mathcal{L}_{a}}P^{a}_{>\lambda_{n}^{\theta}}\phi\bigr]\|_{L^{2}}&\text{in Scenario I,}\\
\|\sqrt{\mathcal{L}_{a}^{n}}\bigl[(\chi_{n}w_{n})(T)-e^{-iT\mathcal{L}_{a}^{n}}P_{>\lambda_{n}^{\theta}}^{a}\phi\bigr]\|_{L^{2}}&\text{in Scenario III},\\
\|\sqrt{\mathcal{L}_{a}^{n}}\bigl[(\chi_{n}w_{n})(T)-e^{-iT\mathcal{L}_{a}^{n}}\phi\bigr]\|_{L^{2}}&\text{in Scenario II}.\end{cases}

In Scenario I, we have
P_{>\lambda_{n}^{\theta}}^{a}\phi=\psi_{n}
, thus by (
7.2
) we have

\lim_{n\to\infty}\|\tilde{v}_{n,T}(\lambda_{n}^{2}t_{n})-\phi_{n}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}=0.

Again,we will only treat the most complex Scenario III in detail and omit details for the simpler Scenario II. Notice that by using the equivalence of Sobolev spaces and directly calculation, we have the following estimate:

(7.13)

\displaystyle\|\tilde{v}_{n,T}(\lambda_{n}^{2}t_{n})-\phi_{n}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla[\chi_{n}(w_{n}(T)-w(T))]\|_{L^{2}}

(7.14)

\displaystyle\quad+\|\nabla[w(T)(\chi_{n}-1)]\|_{L^{2}}

(7.15)

\displaystyle\quad+\|\sqrt{\mathcal{L}_{a}^{n}}[w(T)-e^{-iT\mathcal{L}_{a}^{n}}\phi]\|_{L^{2}}

(7.16)

\displaystyle\quad+\|P_{\leq\lambda_{n}^{\theta}}^{a}\phi\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}.

We will treat (
7.13
)-(
7.16
) seperately.
For (
7.13
), we use Hölder’s inequality and (
7.6
) to obtain

(
7.13
)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla\chi_{n}\|_{L^{d}}\|w_{n}(T)-w(T)\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}}+\|\chi_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla[w_{n}(T)-w(T)]\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\to 0\quad\text{as}\quad n\to\infty.

Using the same argument as above we can obtain the following estimare For (
7.14
):

\displaystyle\|\nabla[w(T)(\chi_{n}-1)]\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla w(T)\|_{L^{2}(\text{supp}(\chi_{n}-1))}+\|w(T)\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}(\text{supp}(\nabla\chi_{n}))}

\displaystyle\to 0\quad\text{as}\quad n\to\infty.

To estimate (
7.15
), we ned to decompose (
7.15
) further and first write

(7.17)

(
7.15
)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|(\sqrt{\mathcal{L}_{a}^{n}}-\sqrt{H})w(T)\|_{L^{2}}+\|[\sqrt{\mathcal{L}_{a}^{n}}-\sqrt{H}]\phi\|_{L^{2}}

(7.18)

\displaystyle\quad+\|(e^{-iT\mathcal{L}_{a}^{n}}-e^{-iTH})\sqrt{H}\phi\|_{L^{2}}

(7.19)

\displaystyle\quad+\|\sqrt{H}(w(T)-e^{-iTH}\phi)\|_{L^{2}}.

For (
7.17
),it’s easy to check that these terms converge to zero as
n\to\infty
by using (
2.14
). The term in (
7.18
) converges to zero as
n\to\infty
due to (
2.15
), while the term in (
7.19
) converges to zero as
T\to\infty
due to (
7.2
).

Finally for (
7.16
),one can use a stardard density argument and the fact that
\lambda_{n}\to 0
to check that (
7.16
) converges to zero as as
T\to\infty
. So we completes the proof of (
7.8
).

Proof of (
7.10
) (control of error terms).
We will also prove each scenario separately.

Proof of (
7.10
)
in Scenario I
. In Scenario I, we have

e_{n}=\lambda_{n}^{-\frac{(d-2)(d+3)}{2(d-1)}}(|w_{n}|^{\frac{4}{d-1}}w_{n})(\lambda_{n}^{-2}t,\lambda_{n}^{-1}x),

By a change of variables, (
7.4
), and (
),we can deduce that

\displaystyle\|\nabla e_{n}\|_{L_{t}^{2}L_{x}^{\frac{2d}{d+2}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda_{n}^{\frac{2}{d-1}}\|w_{n}\|_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+1)}{d-1}}}^{\frac{4}{d-1}}\|\nabla w_{n}\|_{L_{t}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}L_{x}^{\frac{2d(d+2)}{d^{2}+4}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda_{n}^{\frac{2}{d-1}}\|w_{n}\|_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}}^{\frac{2}{d-1}}\|w_{n}\|_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d}}}^{\frac{2}{d-1}}\|\nabla w_{n}\|_{L_{t}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}L_{x}^{\frac{2d(d+2)}{d^{2}+4}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda_{n}^{\frac{2}{d-1}(1-\theta)}\to 0\quad\text{as}\quad n\to\infty.

Thus (
7.10
) holds in Scenario I.

Proof of (
7.10
) in Scenario II.
By the definition of
\tilde{v}_{n,T}
and use the fact that
\lambda_{n}\equiv 1
, we will
\tilde{v}_{n,T}
treat in two regions
|t|\leq T
and
|t|>T
separately.

First we recall that
w_{n}
is a solution to (
CNLS
), so in the region
|t|\leq T
we deduce the following estimate:

(7.20)

\displaystyle e_{n,T}(t,x)

\displaystyle=[(\chi_{n}-\chi^{\frac{d+3}{d-1}}_{n})|w_{n}|^{\frac{4}{d-1}}w_{n}](t,x-x_{n})

(7.21)

\displaystyle\quad-[(\chi_{n}-\chi^{\frac{d+2}{d-2}}_{n})|w_{n}|^{\frac{4}{d-2}}w_{n}](t,x-x_{n})

(7.22)

\displaystyle\quad+2[\nabla\chi_{n}\cdot\nabla w_{n}](t,x-x_{n})+[\Delta\chi_{n}w_{n}](t,x-x_{n})

(7.23)

\displaystyle\quad-\tfrac{a}{|x|^{2}}[\chi_{n}w_{n}](t,x-x_{n}).

In the region
|t|>T
, we instead have

(7.24)

\displaystyle e_{n,T}=|\tilde{v}_{n,T}|^{\frac{4}{d-2}}\tilde{v}_{n,T}-|\tilde{v}_{n,T}|^{\frac{4}{d-1}}\tilde{v}_{n,T}.

As before,we will estimate (
7.20
)–(
7.23
) on
[-T,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
seperately.

For (
7.20
), we apply a change of variables and Hölder’s inequality to deduce that

\displaystyle\|\nabla

\displaystyle\eqref{11new}\|_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d+4}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|(\chi_{n}-\chi^{\frac{d+3}{d-1}}_{n})|w_{n}|^{\frac{4}{d-1}}\nabla w_{n}\|_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d+4}}}+\|\nabla\chi_{n}(1-\tfrac{d+3}{d-1}\cdot\chi^{\frac{4}{d-1}}_{n})w_{n}^{\frac{d+3}{d-1}}\|_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d+4}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigl[\|\nabla w_{n}\|_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}}\|w_{n}\|_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d}}}^{\frac{2}{d-1}}+\|\nabla\chi_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|w_{n}\|_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d}}}\|w_{n}\|_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}}^{\frac{2}{d-1}}\bigr]

\displaystyle\quad\times\bigl[\|w_{n}-w\|_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}}^{\frac{2}{d-1}}+\|w\|_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}([-T,T]\times\{|x+x_{n}|\leq\frac{|x_{n}|}{4}\})}^{\frac{2}{d-1}}\bigr]

\displaystyle\quad\to 0\quad\text{as}\quad n\to\infty,

where we have used the Lebesgue’s dominated convergence theorem and (
7.6
).
The terms (
7.21
), (
7.22
), and (
7.23
) may be handled exactly as the same argument in
[
2
]
, so we omit the details.

For the term (
7.24
), first by Sobolev embedding and Strichartz estimate, it’s easy to show that

(7.25)

\limsup_{T\to\infty}\limsup_{n\to\infty}\|e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}^{n}}[\chi_{n}w_{n}(T)]\|_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}((0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}\to 0.

So we can deduce the estimates of terms in (
7.24
) as follows:

\displaystyle\|\nabla

\displaystyle(|\tilde{v}_{n,T}|^{\frac{4}{d-1}}\tilde{v}_{n,T})\|_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d+4}}(\left\{t>\lambda^{2}_{n}T\right\}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla\tilde{v}_{n,T}\|_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d}}(\left\{t>\lambda^{2}_{n}T\right\}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|\tilde{v}_{n,T}\|_{L^{{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}}_{t,x}(\left\{t>\lambda^{2}_{n}T\right\}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{\frac{2}{d-1}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\|\tilde{v}_{n,T}\|_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d}}(\left\{t>\lambda^{2}_{n}T\right\}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{\frac{2}{d-1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|e^{it\mathcal{L}^{n}_{a}}[\chi_{n}w_{n}(T)]\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}((0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{\frac{2}{d-1}}\to 0\mbox{ (by \eqref{esstential-LimitAx})}

as
n\to\infty
and
T\to\infty
. Similarly,

\displaystyle\hskip 12.91663pt\|\nabla(\tilde{v}_{n,T}|^{\frac{4}{d-2}}\tilde{v}_{n,T})\|_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d+4}}(\left\{t>\lambda^{2}_{n}T\right\}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla\tilde{v}_{n,T}\|_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d}}(\left\{t>\lambda^{2}_{n}T\right\}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|\tilde{v}_{n,T}\|^{\frac{4}{d-2}}_{L^{{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}}_{t,x}(\left\{t>\lambda^{2}_{n}T\right\}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|e^{-it\mathcal{L}^{n}_{a}}(\chi_{n}\omega_{n}(T))\|^{\frac{4}{d-2}}_{L^{{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}}_{t,x}((0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}\to 0\mbox{ (by \eqref{esstential-LimitAx})}

as
n\to\infty
and
T\to\infty
. So (
7.10
) holds in Scenario II.

Proof of (
7.10
) in Scenario III.
Once again, we will treat
e_{n,T}
in the regions
|t|\leq\lambda_{n}^{2}T
and
|t|>\lambda_{n}^{2}T
separately.

Recalling that
w_{n}
is a solution to (
NLS
), we find that in the region
|t|\leq\lambda_{n}^{2}T
, we have

(7.26)

\displaystyle e_{n,T}(t,x)

\displaystyle=-\lambda^{-\frac{(d-2)(d+3)}{2(d-1)}}_{n}[\chi^{\frac{d+3}{d-1}}_{n}|w_{n}|^{\frac{4}{d-1}}w_{n}](\lambda^{-2}_{n}t,\lambda^{-1}_{n}(x-x_{n}))

(7.27)

\displaystyle\quad-\lambda^{-\frac{d+2}{2}}_{n}[(\chi_{n}-\chi^{\frac{d+2}{d-2}}_{n})|w_{n}|^{\frac{4}{d-2}}w_{n}](\lambda^{-2}_{n}t,\lambda^{-1}_{n}(x-x_{n}))

(7.28)

\displaystyle\quad+2\lambda^{-\frac{d+2}{2}}_{n}[\nabla\chi_{n}\cdot\nabla w_{n}](\lambda^{-2}_{n}t,\lambda^{-1}_{n}(x-x_{n}))

(7.29)

\displaystyle\quad+\lambda^{-\frac{d+2}{2}}_{n}[\Delta\chi_{n}w_{n}](\lambda^{-2}_{n}t,\lambda^{-1}_{n}(x-x_{n}))

(7.30)

\displaystyle\quad-\lambda^{-\frac{d-2}{2}}_{n}\tfrac{a}{|x|^{2}}[\chi_{n}w_{n}](\lambda^{-2}_{n}t,\lambda^{-1}_{n}(x-x_{n})).

In the region
t>\lambda_{n}^{2}T
, we again have

(7.31)

e_{n,T}=|\tilde{v}_{n,T}|^{\frac{4}{d-2}}\tilde{v}_{n,T}-|\tilde{v}_{n,T}|^{\frac{4}{d-1}}\tilde{v}_{n,T}.

By the almost same argument in
[
2
]
, We can deduce that (
7.27
)–(
7.30
) tend to 0 as
n\to\infty
and
T\to\infty
.So we only treat
(
)
in details.

In fact,by changing variables and Hölder’s inequality, (
7.5
), and (
7.4
), we obtain

\displaystyle\|\nabla\eqref{e11}\|_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d+4}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda_{n}^{\frac{2}{d-1}}\|\chi_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\nabla w_{n}\|_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d}}}\|w_{n}\|^{\frac{2}{d-1}}_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d}}}\|w_{n}\|^{\frac{2}{d-1}}_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}}

\displaystyle\quad+\lambda^{\frac{d+1}{d-1}}_{n}\|\chi_{n}\|^{\frac{4}{d-1}}_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\nabla\chi_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|w_{n}\|^{\frac{2}{d-1}}_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}}\|w_{n}\|^{\frac{d+1}{d-1}}_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda^{\tfrac{(d+1)(1-\theta)}{d-1}}_{n}\to 0\quad\text{as $n\to\infty$.}

Finally we treat (
7.31
).By (
7.25
), we can handle the estimate of the energy-critical term with exactly the same argument in Scenario II.For the energy-subcritical pertubation term, We just use Strichartz estimate and the persistence of
L^{2}
regularity (
7.4
) to deduce that

\displaystyle\|\nabla|\tilde{v}_{n,T}|^{\frac{4}{d-1}}\tilde{v}_{n,T}\|_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d+4}}(\left\{t>\lambda^{2}_{n}T\right\}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla\tilde{v}_{n,T}\|_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d}}(\left\{t>\lambda^{2}_{n}T\right\}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|\tilde{v}_{n,T}\|^{\frac{2}{d-1}}_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d}}(\left\{t>\lambda^{2}_{n}T\right\}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|\tilde{v}_{n,T}\|^{\frac{2}{d-1}}_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}(\left\{t>\lambda^{2}_{n}T\right\}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\tilde{v}_{n,T}\|^{\frac{2}{d-1}}_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d}}(\left\{t>\lambda^{2}_{n}T\right\}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\lambda^{\frac{2}{d-1}}_{n}\|w_{n}\|^{\frac{2}{d-1}}_{{L}^{\infty}_{t}{L}^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\lambda^{\frac{2(1-\theta)}{d-1}}_{n}\to 0\quad\text{as $n\to\infty$.}

Thus (
7.10
) holds in Scenario III.

Estabilishment of true solutions.
With (
7.7
)–(
7.10
) in place, we may apply the stability lemma (Lemma
2.11
) to deduce the existence of a global solution
v_{n}
to (
CNLSa
) with
v_{n}(0)=\phi_{n}
,

\|v_{n}\|_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim 1\quad\text{uniformly in}\quad n,

and

\limsup_{T\to\infty}\limsup_{n\to\infty}\|v_{n}(t-\lambda_{n}^{2}t_{n})-\tilde{v}_{n,T}(t)\|_{\dot{S}_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}=0.

Approximation by compactly supported functions.
Once again, One can verify (
7.1
) by almost the same argument in
[
2
]
. Thus we omit the proof. ∎

7.2.
Existence of minimal blow-up solutions

For each
E>0
and
C>0
, we define

\displaystyle L_{C}(E):=\sup\bigg\{\|u\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}:\mbox{\emph{u} solves \eqref{NLS} on }I\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\mbox{ and }

\displaystyle M(u)\leqslant C,\quad E_{a}(u(t))\leq E,\quad\|u(t_{0})\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}<\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\mbox{ for some }t_{0}\in I\bigg\}.

By the local theory, we suppose that Theorem
2.8
fails, then there exists
C_{*}>0
such that
E_{C_{*}}<E_{a}(W_{a})
. Using Lemma
2.11
, this implies that
L_{C_{*}}(E_{C_{*}})=\infty
. Thus there exists a sequence of solutions
u_{n}
such that
E_{a}(u_{n})\rightarrow E_{C_{*}}
and
\|u_{n}\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}\rightarrow\infty
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. We will prove the existence of a solution
u_{C_{0}}\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
such that
E_{a}(u_{C_{*}})=E_{C_{*}},\text{sup}_{t\in I}\|u_{C_{*}}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}<\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}},0\in I=(T_{min},T_{max})
with

(7.32)

\|u_{C_{*}}\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}([0,T_{max})\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}=\|u_{C_{*}}\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}((T_{min},0]\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}=\infty,

and such that

(7.33)

\left\{u_{C_{*}}(t):t\in I\right\}\quad\text{is precompact in }\quad\dot{H}_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

Theorem 7.3

(Existence of minimal blow-up solutions)
.

Suppose Theorem
1.11
fails. Then there exists a solution
u_{C_{*}}
to (
CNLSa
) with maximal lifespan
I=(T_{min},T_{max})
such that
0\in I
,

M(u_{C_{*}})\leq C_{*},E_{a}(u_{C_{*}})=E_{C_{*}},\text{sup}_{t\in I}\|u_{C_{*}}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}<\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}
.Morever,(
7.32
) and (
7.33
) hold.

Arguing as in
[
24
, Theorem 9.6]
, to establish Theorem
7.3
, it will suffice to establish the following Palais–Smale condition.

Proposition 7.4
.

Let
\left\{u_{n}\right\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subset H_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
be a sequence of solutions to (
CNLSa
) such that

M(u_{n})\leq C_{*},\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}E_{a}(u_{n})=E_{C_{*}}
, and suppose
t_{n}\in\mathbb{R}
satisfy

(7.34)

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\|u_{n}\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}([t_{n},\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}=\|u_{n}\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}((-\infty,t_{n}]\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}=\infty.

Then we have that
\left\{u_{n}\right\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
converges along a subsequence in
\dot{H}_{x}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

Proof.

By time-translation invariance, we may assume that
t_{n}\equiv 0
. Using Lemma
3.4
and writing
u_{n,0}=u_{n}(0)
, we have

\|u_{n,0}\|^{2}_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}<\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{2}

and

\|u_{n,0}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\lesssim M(u_{n})\lesssim C_{*}.

Applying Theorem
2.13
, we may write

(7.35)

u_{n}(0)=\sum^{J}_{j=1}\phi^{j}_{n}+W^{J}_{n}

for each
J\leq J^{\ast}
, with the various sequences satisfying (
2.22
)–(
2.26
). We have
M(u_{n})\leq C_{*}
,
E_{a}(u_{n})\rightarrow E_{C_{*}}
and
By (
2.25
) and (
2.26
), we also have

(7.36)

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\sum^{J}_{j=1}M(\phi_{n}^{j})+M(W^{J}_{n})\leq C_{*},

(7.37)

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\sum^{J}_{j=1}E_{a}(\phi_{n}^{j})+E_{a}(W^{J}_{n})\leq E_{C_{*}},

for each finite
J\leq J^{\ast}
, with all energies in (
7.37
) nonnegative. Moreover, by sharp Sobolev embedding and the nontriviality of
\phi_{n}^{j}
we have that
\liminf\limits_{n\to\infty}E_{a}(\phi_{n}^{j})>0
.

We shall prove that there at most exists one nonzero
\phi_{n}^{j}
.

Scenario I.
If

(7.38)

\sup_{j}\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}E_{a}(\phi_{n}^{j})=E_{C_{*}}.

By (
7.37
), positivity of energy yields
J^{\ast}=1
. In this case, we have that

(7.39)

u_{n}(0)=\phi^{1}_{n}+W_{n}^{1},\quad\text{with}\quad\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\|W_{n}^{1}\|^{2}_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}=0.

Now suppose that
\frac{|x^{1}_{n}|}{\lambda^{1}_{n}}\to\infty
. Then Proposition
7.1
yields a global solution
v_{n}
with
v_{n}(0)=\phi^{1}_{n}
such that

\|v_{n}\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim 1,\hskip 8.61108pt\|v_{n}\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-1}}_{t,x}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim 1.

As
W_{n}^{1}=u_{n}(0)-v_{n}(0)
, it follows that
\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}\|u_{n}(0)-v_{n}(0)\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}=0
. Thus, Lemma
2.11
yields that for
n
large
u_{n}
is a global solution with finite scattering norm, contradicting (
7.34
). It follows that
x^{1}_{n}\equiv 0
.

Next, suppose that
\lambda^{1}_{n}\to 0
as
n\to\infty
. In this case, Proposition
7.1
yields a global solution
v_{n}

with
v_{n}(0)=\phi^{1}_{n}
and
\|v_{n}\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim 1
. Then Lemma
2.11
implies that
\|u_{n}\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})},\|v_{n}\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-1}}_{t,x}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim 1
for
n
large enough, again contradicting (
7.34
). It follows that
\lambda_{n}^{1}\equiv 1
.

Finally, suppose that
t^{1}_{n}\to\infty
as
n\to\infty
. By Sobolev embedding, Strichartz estimate and (
7.39
), we obtain that

(7.40)

\begin{split}\|&e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}u_{n}(0)\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}([0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
&\leq\|e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}\phi^{1}_{n}\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}([0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\|e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}W_{n}^{1}\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}([0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
&\lesssim\|e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}\phi^{1}\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}([t^{1}_{n},\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\|W_{n}^{1}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{1}}\rightarrow 0,\end{split}

as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Writing
\tilde{u}_{n}=e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}u_{n}(0)
and
e_{n}=|\tilde{u}_{n}|^{\frac{4}{d-2}}\tilde{u}_{n}-|\tilde{u}_{n}|^{\frac{4}{d-1}}\tilde{u}_{n}
, By (
7.40
), Hölder’s inequality, and
Strichartz estimate,we obtain

\|\nabla e_{n}\|_{N(\mathbb{R})}\to 0\quad\text{as $n\to\infty$}.

Then by stability lemma (Lemma
2.11
),
\|u_{n}\|_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}}<\infty
uniformly with
n
, which contradicts (
7.34
). An analogous argument handles the case
t^{1}_{n}\rightarrow-\infty
as
n\rightarrow\infty
.

Thus, in Scenario I, we obtain that
x^{1}_{n}\equiv 0
,
t^{1}_{n}\equiv 0
and
\lambda^{1}_{n}\equiv 1
. This yields the desired conclusion of Proposition
7.4
, and hence it remains to show that the only remaining scenario results in a contradiction.

Scenario II.
If (
7.38
) fails for all
j
, then there exists
\delta>0
such that

(7.41)

\sup_{j}\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}M(\phi_{n}^{j})\leq C_{*}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\sup_{j}\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}E_{a}(\phi_{n}^{j})\leq E_{C_{*}}-\delta.

We then define nonlinear profiles
\psi_{n}^{j}
associated to each
\phi_{n}^{j}
as follows:

•

If
\frac{|x^{j}_{n}|}{\lambda^{j}_{n}}\to\infty
for some
j
, then we are in position to apply Proposition
7.1
, and hence we have a global solution
\psi_{n}^{j}
of (
CNLSa
) with data
\psi_{n}^{j}(0)=\phi_{n}^{j}
.

•

If
x^{j}_{n}\equiv 0
and
\lambda^{j}_{n}\rightarrow 0
, we define
\psi_{n}^{j}
to be the global solution of (
CNLSa
) with the initial data
\psi_{n}^{j}(0)=\phi_{n}^{j}
guaranteed by Proposition
7.1
.

•

If
x^{j}_{n}\equiv 0
,
\lambda^{j}_{n}\equiv 1
and
t^{n}_{j}\equiv 0
, we take
\psi^{j}
to be the global solution of
(
CNLSa
) with the initial data
\psi^{j}(0)=\phi^{j}
.

•

If
x^{j}_{n}\equiv 0
,
\lambda^{j}_{n}\equiv 1
and
t^{n}_{j}\rightarrow\pm\infty
, we take
\psi^{j}
to be the global solution of (
CNLSa
) that scatters to
e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}\phi^{j}
in
H^{1}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
as
t\rightarrow\pm\infty
. In either case, we define the global solution to (
CNLSa
),

\psi^{j}_{n}(t,x):=\psi^{j}(t+t^{j}_{n},x).

By construction, we have that for each
j
,

(7.42)

\|\psi_{n}^{j}(0)-\phi_{n}^{j}\|_{H^{1}_{a}}\rightarrow 0,\quad\text{as $n\rightarrow\infty$}.

Moreover, notice that by (
7.41
) and the definition of
E_{C_{*}}
, we may obtain

(7.43)

\|\psi^{j}_{n}\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}}\lesssim_{\delta}1,\quad\text{for $n$ large and $1\leq j\leq J$}.

Case 1
:
d=4,5
.
By (
7.43
) and
Lemma
2.10
, we have the following estimate:

(7.44)

\displaystyle\|\psi^{j}_{n}\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}}\lesssim_{\delta}[E_{a}(\psi^{j}_{n})]^{\frac{1}{2}},\quad\|\psi^{j}_{n}\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t}\dot{H}_{a}^{1,\frac{2d(d+2)}{d^{2}+4}}}\lesssim_{\delta}[E_{a}(\psi^{j}_{n})]^{\frac{1}{2}},

(7.45)

\displaystyle\|\nabla\psi^{j}_{n}\|_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d}}}\sim\|\sqrt{\mathcal{L}_{a}}\psi^{j}_{n}\|_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d}}}\lesssim_{\delta}[E_{a}(\psi^{j}_{n})]^{\frac{1}{2}},\|\psi^{j}_{n}\|_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d}}}\lesssim_{\delta}[M(\psi^{j}_{n})]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

We define the approximate solutions

u^{J}_{n}(t):=\sum^{J}_{j=1}\psi^{j}_{n}(t)+e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}W^{J}_{n},

with the goal of applying Lemma
2.11
to contradict (
7.34
). In particular, we define the errors
e_{n}^{J}
via

(i\partial_{t}-\mathcal{L}_{a}){u}^{J}_{n}=-|{u}^{J}_{n}|^{\frac{4}{d-2}}{u}^{J}_{n}+|{u}^{J}_{n}|^{\frac{4}{d-1}}{u}^{J}_{n}+e^{J}_{n}.

From (
7.42
) we see that

(7.46)

\begin{split}\lim_{n\to\infty}\|u_{n}^{J}(0)-u_{n}(0)\|_{\dot{H}^{1}_{a}}=0,\quad\text{for any $J$}.\end{split}

As before, it’s sufficient to establish the following estimates:

(7.47)

\displaystyle\sup_{J}\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\|{u}^{J}_{n}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H^{1}_{a}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim_{\delta}1,

\displaystyle\sup_{J}\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\big[\|{u}^{J}_{n}\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}}+\|u_{n}^{J}\|_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d}}}+\|u_{n}^{J}\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t}\dot{H}_{a}^{1,\frac{2d(d+2)}{d^{2}+4}}}

(7.48)

\displaystyle\hskip 60.27759pt+\|u_{n}^{J}\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d}}_{t}\dot{H}_{a}^{1,\frac{2(d+2)}{d}}}\big]\lesssim_{\delta}1,

(7.49)

\displaystyle\lim_{J\to J^{\ast}}\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\|\nabla e^{J}_{n}\|_{N(\mathbb{R})}=0,

where here and below all space-time norms are taken over
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Indeed, using (
7.46
), (
7.47
), (
7.48
), and (
7.49
), Lemma
2.11
implies that
\|u_{n}\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}}\lesssim_{{\varepsilon},\delta}1
for
n
large, contradicting (
7.34
).

We therefore turn to establishing the estimates (
7.47
)-(
7.49
). We will use the following lemma.

Lemma 7.5

(Asymptotic decoupling)
.

If
j\neq k
we have

\lim_{n\to\infty}[\|\psi^{j}_{n}\psi^{k}_{n}\|_{L^{\frac{d+2}{d-2}}_{t,x}}+\|\psi^{j}_{n}\nabla\psi^{k}_{n}\|_{L^{\frac{d+2}{d-1}}_{t,x}}+\|\nabla\psi^{j}_{n}\nabla\psi^{k}_{n}\|_{L^{\frac{d+2}{d}}_{t,x}}+\|\psi^{j}_{n}\psi^{k}_{n}\|_{L^{\frac{d+2}{d}}_{t,x}}]=0.

Proof.

The proof is based on Proposition
7.1
, (
7.44
) and (
7.45
), which is similar to the proof in
[
40
]
. Hence, we omit the details. ∎

As (
7.47
) readily follows from Strichartz (
7.46
), (
7.48
), and (
7.49
), it will suffice to establish (
7.48
) and (
7.49
).

Proof of (
7.48
)
We only prove the estimate of the
L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}
-norm, and the estimates of other terms can be checked by the same argument. In fact, by (
7.44
), equivalence of Sobolev spaces and Strichartz estimate, we deduce that

\begin{split}\|u^{J}_{n}\|^{2}_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}}&\lesssim\sum^{J}_{j=1}\|\psi^{j}_{n}\|^{2}_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{x}}+\sum_{j\neq k}\|\psi^{j}_{n}\psi^{k}_{n}\|_{L^{\frac{(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}}+\|e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}W^{J}_{n}\|^{2}_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}}\\
&\lesssim\sum^{J}_{j=1}E_{a}(\psi^{j}_{n})+\sum^{J}_{j\neq k}o(1)+E_{a}(W^{J}_{n})\lesssim 1+o(1)\cdot J^{2}\end{split}

as
n\to\infty
. Thus (
7.48
) holds.

Proof of (
7.49
)
Since
\psi^{j}_{n}
is a solution of (
CNLSa
), we can write

(7.50)

\displaystyle e^{J}_{n}

\displaystyle=\sum^{J}_{j=1}F(\psi^{j}_{n})-F\big(\sum^{J}_{j=1}\psi^{j}_{n}\big)

(7.51)

\displaystyle+\sum^{J}_{j=1}F(u^{J}_{n}-e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}W^{J}_{n})-F(u^{J}_{n}),

where
F(z)=F_{2}(z)-F_{1}(z)
with
F_{1}(z):=|z|^{\frac{4}{d-2}}z
and
F_{2}(z):=|z|^{\frac{4}{d-1}}z
.By Hölder’s inequality we have

(7.52)

\displaystyle\|\nabla\big[\sum^{J}_{j=1}F_{1}(\psi^{j}_{n})-F_{1}(\sum^{J}_{j=1}\psi^{j}_{n})\big]\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d+4}}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j\neq k}\|\psi^{j}_{n}\|^{\frac{6-d}{d-2}}_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}}\|\psi^{j}_{n}\nabla\psi^{k}_{n}\|_{L^{\frac{d+2}{d-1}}_{t,x}},

(7.53)

\displaystyle\|\nabla\big[\sum^{J}_{j=1}F_{2}(\psi^{j}_{n})-F_{2}(\sum^{J}_{j=1}\psi^{j}_{n})\big]\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d+4}}_{t,x}}
