\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j\neq k}\|\psi^{j}_{n}\|^{\frac{5-d}{d-2}}_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}}\|\psi^{j}_{n}\nabla\psi^{k}_{n}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{\frac{d+2}{d}}_{t,x}}\|\psi^{j}_{n}\nabla\psi^{k}_{n}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{\frac{d+2}{d-1}}_{t,x}}.

Thus, by orthogonality, (
7.44
), (
7.45
), (
7.52
) and (
7.53
) we get

(7.54)

\lim_{J\to J^{\ast}}\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\|\nabla\eqref{Fdefi11}\|_{N(\mathbb{R})}=0.

We next estimate (
7.51
). First, by interpolation we get

\displaystyle\|\nabla[F_{1}(u^{J}_{n}-e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}W^{J}_{n})-F_{1}(u^{J}_{n})]\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d+4}}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}W^{J}_{n}\|^{\frac{4}{d-2}}_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}}\|\nabla e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}W^{J}_{n}\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d}}_{t,x}}+\|e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}W^{J}_{n}\|^{\frac{4}{d-2}}_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}}\|\nabla u^{J}_{n}\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d}}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle+\|e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}W^{J}_{n}\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}}\|u^{J}_{n}\|^{\frac{6-d}{d-2}}_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}}\|\nabla u^{J}_{n}\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d}}_{t,x}}+\|u^{J}_{n}\|^{\frac{6-d}{d-2}}_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}}\|u^{J}_{n}\nabla e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}W^{J}_{n}\|_{L^{\frac{d+2}{d-1}}_{t,x}}.

Combining (
2.22
), (
7.37
) and (
7.44
) we see that

\begin{split}\lim_{J\to J^{\ast}}\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\|\nabla[F_{1}(u^{J}_{n}-e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}W^{J}_{n})-F_{1}(u^{J}_{n})]\|_{N(\mathbb{R})}\\
\lesssim\lim_{J\to J^{\ast}}\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\|u^{J}_{n}\nabla e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}W^{J}_{n}\|_{L^{\frac{d+2}{d-1}}_{t,x}}.\end{split}

Similarly,

\displaystyle\|\nabla[F_{2}(u^{J}_{n}-e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}W^{J}_{n})-F_{2}(u^{J}_{n})]\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d+4}}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}W^{J}_{n}\|^{\frac{5-d}{d-2}}_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}}\|e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}W^{J}_{n}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}}\|e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}W^{J}_{n}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d}}_{t,x}}\|\nabla e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}W^{J}_{n}\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d}}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\quad+\|e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}W^{J}_{n}\|^{\frac{5-d}{d-2}}_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}}\|e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}W^{J}_{n}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}}\|e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}W^{J}_{n}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d}}_{t,x}}\|\nabla u^{J}_{n}\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d}}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\quad+\|u^{J}_{n}\|^{\frac{5-d}{d-2}}_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}}\|e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}W^{J}_{n}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}}\|e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}W^{J}_{n}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d}}_{t,x}}\|\nabla u^{J}_{n}\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d}}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\quad+\|u^{J}_{n}\|^{\frac{5-d}{d-2}}_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}}\|u^{J}_{n}\nabla e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}W^{J}_{n}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{\frac{d+2}{d}}_{t,x}}\|u^{J}_{n}\nabla e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}W^{J}_{n}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{\frac{d+2}{d-1}}_{t,x}}.

As Strichartz together with (
2.22
), (
7.37
) and (
7.44
) implies

(7.55)

\begin{split}\lim_{J\to J^{\ast}}\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\|\nabla[F_{2}(u^{J}_{n}-e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}W^{J}_{n})-F_{2}(u^{J}_{n})]\|_{N^{1}(\mathbb{R})}\\
\lesssim\lim_{J\to J^{\ast}}\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\|u^{J}_{n}\nabla e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}W^{J}_{n}\|_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{d+2}{d-1}}},\end{split}

it remains to show

(7.56)

\lim_{J\to J^{\ast}}\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\|u^{J}_{n}\nabla e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}W^{J}_{n}\|_{L^{\frac{d+2}{d-1}}_{t,x}}=0.

Applying Hölder we deduce

(7.57)

\begin{split}\|u^{J}_{n}\nabla e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}W^{J}_{n}\|_{L^{\frac{d+2}{d-1}}_{t,x}}\leq\|\big(\sum^{J}_{j=1}\psi^{j}_{n}\big)\nabla e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}W^{J}_{n}\|_{L^{\frac{d+2}{d-1}}_{t,x}}\\
+\|e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}W^{J}_{n}\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}}\|\nabla e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}W^{J}_{n}\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d}}_{t,x}}.\end{split}

Thus, using Strichartz inequality, (
2.22
) and (
7.37
) we see that

\lim_{J\to J^{\ast}}\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\|u^{J}_{n}\nabla e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}W^{J}_{n}\|_{L^{\frac{d+2}{d-1}}_{t,x}}\leq\lim_{J\to J^{\ast}}\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\|\big(\sum^{J}_{j=1}\psi^{j}_{n}\big)\nabla e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}W^{J}_{n}\|_{L^{\frac{d+2}{d-1}}_{t,x}}.

On the other hand, it follows from (
7.44
) that

\begin{split}\|\sum^{J}_{j=J^{\prime}}\psi^{j}_{n}\|^{2}_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}}&\lesssim\sum^{J}_{j=J^{\prime}}\|\psi^{j}_{n}\|^{2}_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}}+\sum_{j\neq k}\|\psi^{j}_{n}\psi^{k}_{n}\|_{L^{\frac{d+2}{d-2}}_{t,x}}\lesssim\sum^{J}_{j=J^{\prime}}E_{a}(\psi^{j}_{n})+\sum^{J}_{j\neq k}o(1)\end{split}

as
n\to\infty
. Thus, applying (
7.37
), Hölder’s inequality and Strichartz estimate, we deduce that there
exists
J^{\prime}=J(\eta)
such that

\begin{split}\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\|\big(\sum^{J}_{j=J^{\prime}}\psi^{j}_{n}\big)\nabla e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}W^{J}_{n}\|_{L^{\frac{d+2}{d-1}}_{t,x}}&\lesssim\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\big\|\sum^{J}_{j=J^{\prime}}\psi^{j}_{n}\big\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}}\big\|\nabla e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}W^{J}_{n}\big\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d}}_{t,x}}\\
&\lesssim\eta\quad\text{uniformly in $J\geq J^{\prime}$}\end{split}

for any
\eta>0
. Especially, in order to establish (
7.56
), it suffices to show

(7.58)

\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\|\psi^{j}_{n}\nabla e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}W^{J}_{n}\|_{L^{\frac{d+2}{d-1}}_{t,x}}=0\quad\text{for all $1\leq j\leq J^{\prime}$.}

To this end, we just observe that for any
\delta>0
there exists
\varphi^{j}_{\delta}\in C^{\infty}_{\delta}

with support in
[-T,T]\times\left\{|x|\leq R\right\}
such that (see (
7.1
))

\|\psi^{j}_{n}-(\lambda^{j}_{n})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\varphi^{j}_{\delta}\big(\tfrac{t}{(\lambda_{n}^{j})^{2}}+t^{j}_{n},\tfrac{x-x^{j}_{n}}{\lambda^{j}_{n}}\big)\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}}\leq\delta.

Writing

\tilde{W}^{J}_{n}(t,x):=(\lambda^{j}_{n})^{\frac{d-2}{2}}[e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}W^{J}_{n}]((\lambda^{j}_{n})^{2}(t-t^{j}_{n}),\lambda^{j}_{n}x+x^{j}_{n})

and applying the equivalence of Sobolev norms and Hölder’s inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\psi^{j}_{n}\nabla e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}W^{J}_{n}\|_{L^{\frac{d+2}{d-1}}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\delta\|\nabla e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}W^{J}_{n}\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d}}_{t,x}}+\|\varphi^{j}_{\delta}\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+1)(d+2)}{d^{2}-2d-2}}_{t,x}}\|\nabla\tilde{W}^{J}_{n}\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+1)}{d}}_{t,x}([-T,T]\times\left\{|x|\leq R\right\})}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\delta+C(\delta,T,R)\|\nabla\tilde{W}^{J}_{n}\|^{\frac{d+2}{2(d+1)}}_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d}}_{t,x}}\|\nabla\tilde{W}^{J}_{n}\|^{\frac{d}{2(d+1)}}_{L^{2}_{t,x}}.

Thus (
7.58
) finally follows from Lemma
2.6
and (
2.22
), which completes the proof of (
7.49
) in
d=4,5
.

As described above, with (
7.48
) and (
7.49
), we show that Scenario II cannot occur and hence finish the proof of Proposition
7.4
in dimensions
d=4,5
.

Case 2
:
d=3
.
We note that the following Sobolev norm equivalence

\displaystyle\|\nabla\psi_{n}^{j}\|_{L_{x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d}}}\sim\|\sqrt{\mathcal{L}_{a}}\psi_{n}^{j}\|_{L_{x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d}}},

fails,
so we cannot simply carry out the proof above to deal with the case of
d=3
. Hence we need to choose the admissible pairs carefully. We refer to Ardila-Murphy
[
2
]
for the detailed proof of
d=3
.
1
1

1

In Ardila-Murphy
[
2
]
, they deal with the defocusig-focusing NLS:

i\partial_{t}u+\mathcal{L}_{a}=-|u|^{4}u+|u|^{2}u
. But the proof can be paralleled to our case without any additional modification.

∎

8.
Preclusion of compact solutions: The proof of Theorem
1.11
(i)

In this section, we use the localized virial argument to preclude the possibility of a solution
u_{c}
as in Theorem
7.3
, thus completing the proof of Theorem
1.11
. Without loss of generality, we only consider
u_{c}(t)
on
t\in[0,T_{max})
.

We begin with the finite-time blow-up case.

Proposition 8.1
.

There are no solutions to
(
CNLSa
)
of the form given in Theorem
7.3
with
T_{max}<\infty
.

Proof.

By contradiction, we suppose that the solution of
(
CNLSa
)
exists in the sense of Proposition
7.3
. Then, we can choose
\{t_{n}\}
such that
t_{n}\rightarrow T_{max}
. Using the compactness property,
u(t_{n})\rightarrow h
in
\dot{H}^{1}
. Note that
\|u(t_{n})\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim C
. Moreover, by the mass conservation and the uniqueness of the weak limit, we have
\|u(t_{n})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leqslant C
. From the local theory,
u
can be extended to the neighbourhood of
T_{max}
, which is a contradiction.
∎

Next, we preclude the infinite time blow-up case.

Proposition 8.2
.

Suppose
u_{C_{*}}
is a solution as in Theorem
7.3
. Then for every
{\varepsilon}>0
there exists
R=R({\varepsilon})>1
such that

(8.1)

\sup_{t\in[0,\infty)}\int_{|x|>R}|\nabla u_{C_{*}}(t,x)|^{2}+\tfrac{a}{|x|^{2}}|u_{C_{*}}(t,x)|^{2}+|u_{C_{*}}(t,x)|^{\frac{2(d+1)}{d-1}}+|u_{C_{*}}(t,x)|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}dx\leq{\varepsilon}.

Proof of Theorem
1.11
.

We suppose Theorem
1.11
fails and take a solution
u_{C_{*}}
as in Theorem
7.3
. By Lemma
3.3
,
E_{a}(u_{C_{*}})>0
. We recall the virial identity(Lemma
2.35
) with
u=u_{C_{*}}

(8.2)

\displaystyle I_{R}[u_{C_{*}}]=

\displaystyle 2\IM\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\nabla w_{R}\cdot\nabla u_{C_{*}}\,\overline{u_{C_{*}}}\,dx

(8.3)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}I_{R}[u_{C_{*}}]=

\displaystyle 8\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\nabla u_{C_{*}}|^{2}dx+\tfrac{a}{\left|x\right|^{2}}|u_{C_{*}}|^{2}dx-|u_{C_{*}}|^{\tfrac{2d}{d-2}}dx+\tfrac{d}{d+1}|u_{C_{*}}|^{\frac{2(d+1)}{d-1}}

(8.4)

\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}\left(\int_{|x|\sim R}|\nabla u_{C_{*}}|^{2}+\tfrac{a}{|x|^{2}}|u_{C_{*}}|^{2}+|u_{C_{*}}|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}+|u_{c}|^{\frac{2(d+1)}{d-1}}dx\right)

By sharp Sobolev embedding(Lemma
3.3
) and Lemma
3.4
,
E_{a}(u_{C_{*}})>0

(8.5)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left|\nabla u_{C_{*}}\right|^{2}+\frac{a}{\left|x\right|^{2}}\left|u_{C_{*}}\right|^{2}-\left|u_{C_{*}}\right|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}dx\gtrsim\|u\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{2}\gtrsim\eta_{0}>0.

Combining with Proposition
8.2
,there exists
R_{0}>0
so that

\displaystyle I_{R_{0}}[u_{C_{*}}]\gtrsim\eta_{0}.

On the other hand,

\left|I_{R_{0}}[u_{C_{*}}]\right|\lesssim R_{0}\|u_{C_{*}}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{1}_{x}}\lesssim R_{0}.

Thus, the Fundamental Theorem of Calculus implies

\eta_{0}T\lesssim\left|\int^{T}_{0}\partial_{t}I_{R_{0}}[u_{C_{*}}]dt\right|\lesssim R_{0}\quad\text{for any}\quad T>0,

which yields a contradiction for
T
sufficiently large. Thus we finish the proof.
∎

9.
Compactness for non-scattering threshold solutions

In this section, we will prove that the failure of Theorem
1.12
(i) implies the existence of a non-scattering solution
u
to (
CNLSa
) at the energy threshold such that the orbit of
u
is pre-compact in
\dot{H}_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
modulo scaling symmetry.

Proposition 9.1
.

Assume that Theorem
1.12
(i) fails. Then there exists a solution
{u}:[0,T^{*})\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{C}
of (
CNLSa
) with

(9.1)

\displaystyle E_{a}(u_{0})=E_{a}^{c}(W_{a})\quad\text{and}\quad\|u_{0}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}<\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}

(9.2)

\displaystyle\|{u}\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}([0,T^{*})\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}=\infty.

Moreover, there exists
\lambda:[0,T^{*})\to(0,\infty)
such that

(9.3)

\left\{\lambda(t)^{-\frac{d-2}{2}}u(t,\tfrac{x}{\lambda(t)}):t\in[0,T^{*})\right\}\quad\text{is pre-compact in $\dot{H}_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})$}.

Proof.

The proof follows that of
[
3
]
and we will only give the sketch of the proof. If Theorem
1.12
(i) fails, then there exists a solution
{u}:[0,T^{*})\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{C}

satisfying (
9.1
) and (
9.2
). In particular, we have by Lemma
3.4
,

(9.4)

\displaystyle\|u(t)\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}<\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}\quad\text{for all $t\in[0,T^{*})$.}\quad

It is sufficient to prove that for any sequence
\left\{\tau_{n}\right\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subset[0,T^{*})
, there exists
\lambda_{n}
such that

(9.5)

\displaystyle\lambda_{n}^{-\frac{d-2}{2}}u\left(\tau_{n},\tfrac{x}{\lambda_{n}}\right)\mbox{ converges strongly in $\dot{H}_{a}^{1}$ (up to a subsequence).}

By continuity of
u
it suffices to consider
\tau_{n}\to T^{*}
. Using (
9.4
), we apply the profile decomposition (Theorem
2.13
) to
\left\{u(\tau_{n})\right\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
and write

(9.6)

u_{n}:=u(\tau_{n})=\sum^{J}_{j=1}\phi^{j}_{n}+R_{n}^{J}

with
J\leq J^{*}
and all of the properties stated in Theorem
2.13
.

Similar to the previous section, we shall prove that there exists at most one nonzero
\phi_{n}^{j}
.

Scenario I
.
J^{*}\geq 2
.

By Theorem
2.13
and (
9.4
) we have

(9.7)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\big(\sum_{j=1}^{J}M(\phi_{n}^{j})+M(R_{n}^{J})\big)=\lim_{n\to\infty}M(u_{n})=M(u_{0}),

(9.8)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\big(\sum_{j=1}^{J}E_{a}(\phi_{n}^{j})+E_{a}(R_{n}^{J})\big)=\lim_{n\to\infty}E_{a}(u_{n})=E_{a}^{c}(W),

(9.9)

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\big(\sum_{j=1}^{J}\|\phi_{n}^{j}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{2}+\|R_{n}^{J}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{2}\big)=\limsup_{n\to\infty}\|u(\tau_{n})\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{2}\leq\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{2}

for any
0\leq J\leq J^{*}
. By Lemma
3.3
and (
9.9
) we deduce that for
n
large,

(9.10)

\displaystyle\|\phi_{n}^{j}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{2}\leq\frac{\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{2}}{E_{a}^{c}(W_{a})}E_{a}(\phi_{n}^{j})\quad\text{and}\quad\|R_{n}^{J}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{2}\leq\frac{\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{2}}{E_{a}^{c}(W)}E_{a}(R_{n}^{J}).

In particular,
\liminf\limits_{n\to\infty}E_{a}(\phi_{n}^{j})>0
. Thus there exists
\delta>0
so that

(9.11)

\displaystyle E_{a}(\phi_{n}^{j})

\displaystyle\leq E_{a}^{c}(W_{a})-\delta,

for sufficiently large
n
and
1\leq j\leq J
, so that each
\phi_{n}^{j}
satisfies (
1.17
) in Theorem
1.11
(i).

We then define nonlinear profiles
\psi_{n}^{j}
associated to each
\phi_{n}^{j}
as follows:

•

If
\frac{|x^{j}_{n}|}{\lambda^{j}_{n}}\to\infty
for some
j
, then we are in position to apply Proposition
7.1
, and hence we have a global solution
\psi_{n}^{j}
of (
CNLSa
) with data
\psi_{n}^{j}(0)=\phi_{n}^{j}
.

•

If
x^{j}_{n}\equiv 0
and
\lambda^{j}_{n}\rightarrow 0
, we define
\psi_{n}^{j}
to be the global solution of (
CNLSa
) with the initial data
\psi_{n}^{j}(0)=\phi_{n}^{j}
guaranteed by Proposition
7.1
.

•

If
x^{j}_{n}\equiv 0
,
\lambda^{j}_{n}\equiv 1
and
t^{j}_{n}\equiv 0
, we take
\psi^{j}
to be the global solution of
(
CNLSa
) with the initial data
\psi^{j}(0)=\phi^{j}
.

•

If
x^{j}_{n}\equiv 0
,
\lambda^{j}_{n}\equiv 1
and
t^{n}_{j}\rightarrow\pm\infty
, we take
\psi^{j}
to be the global solution of (
CNLSa
) that scatters to
e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}\phi^{j}
in
H^{1}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
as
t\rightarrow\pm\infty
. In either case, we define the global solution to (
CNLSa
),

\psi^{j}_{n}(t,x):=\psi^{j}(t+t^{j}_{n},x).

We define the approximate solutions

u^{J}_{n}(t):=\sum^{J}_{j=1}\psi^{j}_{n}(t)+e^{-it\mathcal{L}_{a}}W^{J}_{n},

Following the same strategy in proving Proposition
7.4
, one can show that
u_{n}^{J}(t)
is the approximate solution of
u_{n}
for large
n
and
J\to J^{*}
. Thus we deduce that
\|u_{n}\|_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}<\infty
which is contradict to (
9.2
), so Scenario I cannot occur.

Scenario II.

J^{*}=1
. (
9.6
) simplifies to

(9.12)

\displaystyle u_{n}=u(\tau_{n})=\phi_{n}+W^{1}_{n}.

We now observe that

(9.13)

\displaystyle\|W^{1}_{n}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}\to 0\quad\text{as $n\to+\infty$.}\quad

Indeed, otherwise, by (
9.10
) we see that there exists
c>0
so that
E_{a}(W^{1}_{n})\geq c
. Thus,
\phi_{n}
obeys the sub-threshold condition (
9.11
), and the same arguments used above show
u\in L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
, contradicting (
9.2
).

First, we preclude the possibility that
t_{n}\rightarrow\pm\infty
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Without loss of generality, suppose
\tau_{n}\rightarrow\infty
. If
\lambda_{n}\equiv 1
, then by monotone convergence we have

\|e^{it\mathcal{L}_{a}}\phi_{n}\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}([0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}=\|e^{it\mathcal{L}_{a}}\phi\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}([\tau_{n},\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}\rightarrow 0

as
n\rightarrow\infty
. On the other hand, if
\lambda_{n}\to+\infty
a change of variables, Bernstein’s inequality, Strichartz estimate, and monotone convergence yield

\|e^{it\mathcal{L}_{a}}\phi_{n}\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}([0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}=\|e^{it\mathcal{L}_{a}}P_{\geq(\lambda_{n})^{-\theta}}\phi\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}([\tau_{n},\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}\rightarrow 0,

as
n\rightarrow\infty
. In either case, we may apply Lemma
2.11
with
\tilde{u}:=e^{it\mathcal{L}_{a}}\phi_{n}
to obtain that
u\in L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}([0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
, contradicting (
9.2
). Thus
t_{n}\equiv 0
.

Next we show that
x_{n}\equiv 0
.
In fact, by Lemma
3.4
and Proposition
7.1
one can show that
u
has an approximate solution which has is
L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}
-bounded for sufficiently large
n
. It contradicts to (
9.2
). Thus
x_{n}\equiv 0
.
Finally, by (
9.12
) and (
9.13
) we get

\|\lambda^{-\frac{d-2}{2}}_{n}u(\tau_{n},\tfrac{x}{\lambda_{n}})-\phi\|^{2}_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}\to 0\quad\text{as $n\to\infty$.}\quad

Note that in the case
\lambda_{n}\to+\infty
, we have used the fact that

\|P^{a}_{\geq(\lambda_{n})^{-\theta}}\phi-\phi\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}\to 0
as
n\to\infty
. This complete the proof of (
9.5
).
∎

10.
Preclusion of compact solutions: The proof of Theorem
1.12
(i)

In this section we complete the proof of Theorem
1.12
(i). It’s enough to prove that there are no solutions of (
CNLSa
) as in Proposition
9.1
.

10.1.
Finite-time blow-up

In this subsection we preclude the possibility of finite-time blow-up.

Theorem 10.1
.

There are no solutions of (
CNLSa
) as in Proposition
9.1
with
T^{*}<\infty
.

Proof.

Suppose that
u:[0,T^{*})\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{C}
is such a solution. Now we claim that

(10.1)

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to T^{*}}\lambda(t)=\infty.

By contradiction, we suppose that
\lim_{t\to T^{*}}\lambda(t)<\infty
. Then there exists a sequence
\{t_{n}\}
that converges to
T^{*}
such that
\lambda(t_{n})\to\lambda_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
. Indeed, we have
\lambda_{0}>0
. Thus, by (
9.3
), we obtain that there exists nonzero
\psi\in\dot{H}_{a}^{1}
such that

(10.2)

\displaystyle\|u(t_{n})-\psi\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}\to 0\quad\text{as $n\to\infty$.}\quad

Moreover, we have
\|\psi\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\leq M(u_{0})
by using the mass conservation and the uniqueness of the weak limit. Following the argument in
[
18
]
, (
10.2
) and Lemma
2.11
, we can show that

\|u\|_{S^{0}([T^{*}-{\varepsilon},T^{*}+{\varepsilon}]\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}<\infty

for some
0<{\varepsilon}\ll 1
, which contradicts (
9.2
) and hence proves (
10.1
).

For a fixed
R>0
and
t\in[0,T^{*})
, we define

M_{R}(t)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|u(t,x)|^{2}\xi\left(\tfrac{x}{R}\right)\,dx.\quad\text{}\quad

where
\xi(x)=1
if
|x|\leq 1
and
\xi(x)=0
if
|x|\geq 2
. Since

M^{\prime}_{R}(t)=\tfrac{2}{R}\IM\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\overline{u}\nabla u\cdot(\nabla\xi)\left(\tfrac{x}{R}\right)dx,

it follows from (
3.6
) that
M^{\prime}_{R}(t)\lesssim_{W_{a}}1
. Thus

(10.3)

\displaystyle M_{R}(t_{1})=M_{R}(t_{2})+\int^{t_{2}}_{t_{1}}M^{\prime}_{R}(t)dt\lesssim_{W_{a}}M_{R}(t_{2})+|t_{1}-t_{2}|.

On the other hand, for
\mu\in(0,1)
and
t\in[0,T^{*})
,

\displaystyle\int_{|x|\leq R}|u(t,x)|^{2}dx

\displaystyle\leq\int_{|x|\leq\mu R}|u(t,x)|^{2}dx+\int_{\mu R\leq|x|\leq R}|u(t,x)|^{2}dx

\displaystyle\lesssim\mu^{2}R^{2}\|u(t)\|^{2}_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}}+R^{2}\left(\int_{|x|\geq\eta R}|u(t,x)|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}dx\right)^{\frac{d-2}{d}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mu^{2}R^{2}\|\nabla W_{a}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+R^{2}\left(\int_{|x|\geq\eta R}|u(t,x)|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}dx\right)^{\frac{d-2}{d}}.

By (
9.3
) and (
10.1
) we see that for all
R_{0}>0
,

\int_{|x|>R_{0}}|u(t,x)|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}\,dx\to 0\quad\text{as $t\to T^{*}$.}\quad

Thus we deduce that for any
R>0
,

\limsup_{t\nearrow T^{*}}\int_{|x|\leq R}|u(t,x)|^{2}dx=0.

In particular,
M_{R}(t_{2})\to 0
as
t_{2}\to T^{*}
, so that (
10.3
) implies

M_{R}(t_{1})\lesssim_{W_{a}}|T^{{*}}-t_{1}|.

Letting
R\to\infty
and using conservation of mass, we get
M(u_{0})\lesssim_{W}|T^{{*}}-t_{1}|
. Letting
t_{1}\to T^{*}
we obtain
{u}_{0}=0
, which contradicts
E_{a}({u}_{0})=E^{c}(W_{a})>0
.∎

Combining the above result with Theorem
1.11
, we can deduce the following corollary.

Corollary 10.2
.

If
u
is a maximal-lifespan solution to (
CNLSa
) satisfying

E_{a}({u}_{0})\leq E_{a}^{c}(W_{a})\mbox{ and }\|u_{0}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}\leq\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}},

then
u
is global in time.

10.2.
Infinite time blow-up

In this subsection, we preclude the possibility of infinite time blow-up.

Theorem 10.3
.

There are no solutions to (
CNLSa
) as in Proposition
9.1
with
T^{*}=\infty
.

We prove Theorem
10.3
by contradiction. We suppose that
u:[0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{C}
is a solution to (
CNLSa
) as in Proposition
9.1
with
T^{*}=\infty
. In particular,
u
obeys

(10.4)

\displaystyle E_{a}^{c}(u_{0})=E_{a}^{c}(W_{a}),\quad\|u_{0}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}<\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}},\quad\text{and}\quad\|u\|_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}([0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}=\infty.

Moreover, there exists compactness parameter
\lambda_{0}:[0,\infty)\to(0,\infty)
such that the set

(10.5)

\left\{\lambda_{0}(t)^{-\frac{d-2}{2}}u(t,\tfrac{x}{\lambda_{0}(t)}):t\in[0,+\infty)\right\}\quad\text{is pre-compact in $\dot{H}_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})$}.

We first show that the parameter
\lambda_{0}(t)
is equivalent to the modulation parameter
\mu(t)
when
\delta(t)
is sufficently small.

Lemma 10.4
.

If
\delta_{0}
is sufficiently small, then there exists
C>0
so that

(10.6)

\displaystyle\tfrac{\lambda_{0}(t)}{\mu(t)}+\tfrac{\mu(t)}{\lambda_{0}(t)}\leq C\quad\text{for $t\in I_{0}$},

where the parameter
\mu(t)
is given in Proposition
4.1
.

Proof.

By (
4.2
) and (
4.3
), the following holds true

\int_{\frac{1}{\mu(t)}\leq|x|\leq\frac{2}{\mu(t)}}|\nabla u(t,x)|^{2}dx\geq\tfrac{1}{2}\int_{1\leq|x|\leq 2}|\nabla W(x)|^{2}dx-C\delta^{2}(t)\geq c

for
\delta_{0}\ll 1
,
t\in I_{0}
and some positive constant
c
. By a change of variable, we deduce

(10.7)

\displaystyle\int_{\frac{\lambda_{0}(t)}{\mu(t)}\leq|x|\leq\frac{2\lambda_{0}(t)}{\mu(t)}}\tfrac{1}{\lambda_{0}(t)^{d}}\left|\nabla u\left(t,\tfrac{x}{\lambda_{0}(t)}\right)\right|^{2}dx\geq c\quad\text{for all}\quad t\in I_{0}.

Thus (
10.6
) follows from (
10.5
).
∎

As a consequence of the above lemma, we deduce that

(10.8)

\left\{\lambda(t)^{-\frac{d-2}{2}}u(t,\tfrac{x}{\lambda(t)}):t\in[0,\infty)\right\}\quad\text{is pre-compact in $\dot{H}_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})$},

where

\lambda(t)=\begin{cases}\lambda_{0}(t)&\quad t\in[0,\infty)\setminus I_{0},\\
\mu(t)&\quad t\in I_{0}.\end{cases}

We note that
\lambda(t)\geqslant 1
for
t\geqslant 0
.

Next we will prove some essential lemmas which are the key to proving Theorem
10.3
.

Lemma 10.5
.

For any sequence
\left\{t_{n}\right\}\subset[0,\infty)
, the following holds

(10.9)

\lambda(t_{n})\to\infty\quad\text{if and only if}\quad\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|u(t_{n},x)|^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}dx\to 0.

Proof.

Suppose
\lambda(t_{n})\to\infty
and
{\varepsilon}>0
. Using (
10.8
), there exists
\rho_{{\varepsilon}}>0
so that

\int_{{|x|\geq\tfrac{\rho_{{\varepsilon}}}{\lambda(t_{n})}}}\bigl[|\nabla u(t_{n},x)|^{2}+|u(t_{n},x)|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}\bigr]dx\ll{\varepsilon}\quad\text{for all $n\in\mathbb{N}$.}\quad

we obtain by using interpolation and the mass conservation
M(u(t_{n}))=M(u_{0})
,

(10.10)

\displaystyle\int_{{|x|\geq\tfrac{\rho_{{\varepsilon}}}{\lambda(t_{n})}}}|u(t_{n},x)|^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}dx<{\varepsilon}\quad\text{for all $n\in\mathbb{N}$.}\quad

Next, since
\tfrac{1}{\lambda(t_{n})}\to 0
as
n\to\infty
, we deduce from holder inequality that

(10.11)

\displaystyle\int_{{|x|\leq\tfrac{\rho_{{\varepsilon}}}{\lambda(t_{n})}}}|u(t_{n},x)|^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}dx\to 0\quad\text{as $n\to\infty$.}\quad

Combining (
10.10
) and (
10.11
) we have

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|u(t_{n},x)|^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}dx\to 0,\quad n\to+\infty.

On the other hand, we assume that

(10.12)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|u(t_{n},x)|^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}dx\to 0,

but
\left\{\lambda(t_{n})\right\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
converges. From (
10.8
), we see that there exists
\phi\in\dot{H}_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
so that

(10.13)

u(t_{n})\to\phi\quad\text{in $\dot{H}_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})$}.

Combining (
10.12
) and (
10.13
) we have
E_{a}^{c}(W_{a})=E_{a}^{c}(\phi)
, so that
\phi\neq 0
. Moreover, since
M(u(t_{n}))=M(u_{0})
, we deduce that
\phi\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

However, by the sharp Sobolev inequality and (
10.13
) we get

\|u(t_{n})-\phi\|_{L^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}}\lesssim\|u(t_{n})-\phi\|^{\frac{1}{d+1}}_{L^{2}}\|u(t_{n})-\phi\|^{\frac{d}{d+1}}_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}\to 0\quad\text{as $n\to\infty$.}\quad

Thus, by (
10.12
) we have that
\|\phi\|_{L^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}}=0
, which is contradict to
\phi\neq 0
. ∎

Lemma 10.6
.

If
t_{n}\to\infty
, then

(10.14)

\lambda(t_{n})\to\infty\quad\text{if and only if}\quad\delta(t_{n})\to 0.

Proof.

If
\delta(t_{n})\to 0
, then Proposition
4.1
and
Lemma
10.5
imply
\lambda(t_{n})\to\infty
.

Next, by contradiction we assume that
\lambda(t_{n})\to\infty
but

(10.15)

\delta(u(t_{n}))\geq c>0.

Notice that

\left\{\lambda(t)^{-\frac{d-2}{2}}u(t,\tfrac{x}{\lambda(t)}):t\in[0,\infty)\right\}\quad\text{is pre-compact in $\dot{H}_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})$},

there exists
v_{0}\in\dot{H}_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
such that

(10.16)

\lambda(t_{n})^{-\frac{d-2}{2}}u(t_{n},\tfrac{x}{\lambda(t_{n})})\to v_{0}\quad\text{in}\quad\dot{H}_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

In particular, from (
10.4
), (
10.9
) and (
10.15
) we have

(10.17)

\displaystyle E_{a}^{c}(v_{0})=E_{a}^{c}(W_{a})\quad\text{and}\quad\|v_{0}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}<\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}.

Now let
v
be the solution of the energy-critical NLS (
NLSa
) with
v|_{t=0}=v_{0}
. By Theorem
,
v
is global and its scattering size is finite

\|v\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}([0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}<\infty\quad\text{or}\quad\|v\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}((-\infty,0]\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}<\infty.

In either case, we will use stability theory to deduce that

u\in L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}([0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
, contradicting (
10.4
).

The argument is similar to the proof of Proposition
7.1
. For simpleness, we suppose that
\|v\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}([0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}<+\infty
. For the case of
\|v\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}((-\infty,0]\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}<+\infty
, it can be handled similarly.

Let
w_{n}
be the solution to the energy-critical NLS with inverse-square potential (
NLSa
) with initial data
w_{n}(0)=P^{a}_{\geq(\lambda_{n})^{-\theta}}v_{0}
for some
0<\theta\ll 1
, where

\lambda_{n}:=\lambda(t_{n})
. Since
\lambda_{n}\to\infty
as
n\to\infty
,

(10.18)

\displaystyle\|P^{a}_{\geq(\lambda_{n})^{-\theta}}v_{0}-v_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{1}_{x}}\rightarrow 0\quad\text{as $n\rightarrow\infty$}.

By (
10.18
) and Lemma
2.11
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\|w_{n}\|_{S_{a}^{1}([0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim 1\quad\text{for $n$ large.}\quad

Using Benstein’s estimate, we obtain

\|P^{a}_{\geq(\lambda_{n})^{-\theta}}v_{0}\|_{{L}^{2}}\lesssim\lambda^{\theta}_{n}\|v_{0}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}},

which implies

(10.19)

\displaystyle\|w_{n}\|_{S_{a}^{0}([0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq C(\|v_{0}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}})\lambda^{\theta}_{n}

by persistence of regularity.
Arguing as in Proposition
7.1
one can show that

\tilde{u}_{n}(t,x)=\lambda^{\frac{d-2}{2}}_{n}w_{n}(\lambda^{2}_{n}t,\lambda_{n}x)

are approximate solutions to equation (
CNLSa
) on
[0,\infty)
. Indeed, following the same argument as in (
), we obtain that

\lim_{n\to\infty}\|\sqrt{\mathcal{L}_{a}}\bigl(|\tilde{u}_{n}|^{\frac{4}{d-1}}\tilde{u}_{n}\bigr)\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d+4}}_{t,x}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}=0.

Then combining (
10.16
) and (
10.18
) we get

\|\tilde{u}_{n}(0)-u(t_{n})\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}\rightarrow 0\quad\text{as $n\rightarrow\infty$}.

Applying Lemma
2.11
, we deduce that for
n
large,

\|u(t_{n}+t)\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}([0,+\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}=\|u\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}([t_{n},+\infty))}\lesssim 1,

which is contradict to (
10.4
).∎

Lemma 10.7
.

There exists
c>0
so that

(10.20)

{{F^{c}_{\infty}}}[u(t)]=8\|\nabla u(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+8a\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{|u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}dx-8\|u(t)\|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}}\geq c\delta(t)\quad\text{for all $t\geq 0$.}\quad

Proof.

We recall that
E_{a}^{c}(W_{a})=\frac{1}{d}\|W_{a}\|^{2}_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}
. By using
E_{a}(u)=E_{a}^{c}(W_{a})
we have

(10.21)

{{F^{c}_{\infty}}}[u(t)]=\tfrac{16}{d-2}\delta(t)-\tfrac{8d^{2}-8d}{d^{2}-d-2}\|u(t)\|^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}_{L^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}}.

We claim that
{{F^{c}_{\infty}}}[u(t)]>0
for all
t\geq 0
. Indeed, by using sharp Sobolev inequality (
3.3
) and (
3.6
)
we deduce

\displaystyle\|u\|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}}

\displaystyle\leq C^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}_{GN}\|u\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}=\frac{1}{\|W_{a}\|^{\frac{4}{d-2}}_{\dot{H}^{1}_{a}}}\|u\|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}_{\dot{H}^{1}_{a}}

(10.22)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\|u\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}}{\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}}\right)^{\frac{4}{d-2}}\|u\|^{2}_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}<\|u\|^{2}_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}.

Suppose that (
10.20
) were false. Then there exists
\left\{t_{n}\right\}
such that

(10.23)

{{F^{c}_{\infty}}}[u(t_{n})]\leq\tfrac{1}{n}\delta(t_{n}).

We first observe that
\left\{\delta(t_{n})\right\}
is bounded (cf. Lemma
3.4
). Now, we show that

\delta(t_{n})\to 0\quad\text{as $n\to\infty$}.

In fact
{{F^{c}_{\infty}}}[u(t_{n})]\to 0
as
n\to\infty
by (
10.23
), so that (
10.22
) implies

\displaystyle\|u(t_{n})\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}\rightarrow\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}},

i.e.
\delta(t_{n})\to 0
as
n\to\infty
. By Proposition
4.1
we have

\|u(t_{n})\|^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}_{L^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}}\lesssim\delta(t_{n})^{2}\leq\tfrac{d^{2}-d-2}{8d^{2}-8d}\delta(t_{n})\quad\text{for $n$ large}.

Thus, by using (
10.21
) and (
10.23
) we get

\tfrac{18-d}{d-2}\delta(t_{n})\leq\tfrac{1}{n}\delta(t_{n})\quad\text{for $n$ large},

which is a contradiction because
\delta(t)>0
for all
t\geq 0
.
∎

Lemma 10.8
.

For
\delta_{1}\in(0,\delta_{0})
sufficiently small, there exists a constant
C=C(\delta_{1})>0
such that for any interval
[t_{1},t_{2}]\subset[0,\infty)
,

(10.24)

\int^{t_{2}}_{t_{1}}\delta(t)\,dt\leq C\sup_{t\in[t_{1},t_{2}]}\tfrac{1}{\lambda(t)^{2}}\left\{\delta(t_{1})+\delta(t_{2})\right\}.

Proof.

Let
\delta_{1}\in(0,\delta_{0})
and
R>1
be two parameters to be determined below. We use the localized virial identities with
\chi(t)
satisfying

\chi(t)=\begin{cases}1&\quad\delta(t)<\delta_{1}\\
0&\quad\delta(t)\geq\delta_{1}.\end{cases}

From Lemmas
2.20
and Lemma
10.7
we deduce

(10.25)

\tfrac{d}{dt}I_{R}[u(t)]=F^{c}_{\infty}[u(t)]+\mathcal{E}(t)\geq c\delta(t)+\mathcal{E}(t)

with

(10.26)

\mathcal{E}(t)=\begin{cases}F_{R}[u(t)]-F^{c}_{\infty}[u(t)]&\quad\text{if $\delta(t)\geq\delta_{1}$},\\
F_{R}[u(t)]-F^{c}_{\infty}[u(t)]-\mathcal{M}[t]&\quad\text{if $\delta(t)<\delta_{1}$},\end{cases}

where

(10.27)

\mathcal{M}(t)=F^{c}_{R}[e^{i\theta(t)}\lambda(t)^{\frac{d-2}{2}}W_{a}(\lambda(t)x)]-F^{c}_{\infty}[e^{i\theta(t)}\lambda(t)^{\frac{d-2}{2}}W_{a}(\lambda(t)x)].

On the one hand, it has been proven that

(10.28)

\displaystyle|I_{R}[u(t)]|\lesssim R^{2}\delta(t).

for any
R>1
.

On the other hand, we shall prove that for any
{\varepsilon}>0
, there exists
\rho_{{\varepsilon}}=\rho({\varepsilon})>0
so that if

(10.29)

R:=\rho_{{\varepsilon}}\sup_{t\in[t_{1},t_{2}]}\tfrac{1}{\lambda(t)},

then

(10.30)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(t)\geq-{\varepsilon}\geq-\tfrac{{\varepsilon}}{\delta_{1}}\delta(t)\quad

\displaystyle\text{uniformly for $t\in[t_{1},t_{2}]$ such that
$\delta(t)\geq\delta_{1}$},

(10.31)

\displaystyle|\mathcal{E}(t)|\leq({\varepsilon}+\delta_{1})\delta(t)\quad

\displaystyle\text{uniformly for $t\in[t_{1},t_{2}]$ such that $\delta(t)<\delta_{1}$}.

In fact,for
\delta(t)<\delta_{1}
,

(10.32)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(t)=F_{R}[u(t)]-F^{c}_{\infty}[{u}(t)]

\displaystyle=\int_{|x|\geq R}(-\Delta\Delta w_{R})|u|^{2}+4\RE\overline{u_{j}}u_{k}\partial_{jk}[w_{R}]-8|\nabla u|^{2}dx

(10.33)

\displaystyle+\int_{|x|>R}(\frac{4a}{|x|^{4}}x\nabla\phi_{R}|u(t)|^{2}-\frac{8a|u(t)|^{2}}{|x|^{2}})dx.

(10.34)

\displaystyle-\frac{4}{d}\int_{|x|\geq R}\Delta[w_{R}(x)]|u|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}dx+8\int_{|x|\geq R}|u|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}dx

(10.35)

\displaystyle+\frac{4}{d+1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Delta[w_{R}(x)]|u|^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}dx.

By compactness (
10.8
) and mass conservation, we show that

(10.36)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\int_{|x|>\tfrac{\rho_{{\varepsilon}}}{\lambda(t)}}\big[|\nabla u|^{2}+|u|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}+|u|^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}+\tfrac{|u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}\big](t,x)dx\ll{\varepsilon}

for large enough
\rho_{\varepsilon}>0
. Given
T>0
, we now choose

R:=\sup_{t\in[t_{1},t_{2}]}\frac{\rho_{{\varepsilon}}}{\lambda(t)},\quad\text{so that}\quad\left\{|x|\geq R\right\}\subset\left\{|x|\geq\tfrac{\rho_{{\varepsilon}}}{\lambda(t)}\right\}\quad\text{for all}\quad t\in[t_{1},t_{2}].

It follows that

(10.37)

|\eqref{F11}|+|\eqref{F22}|+|\eqref{F33}|<{\varepsilon}\quad\text{for all $t\in[0,T]$.}\quad

Next, we use (
10.36
) and
|\Delta[w_{R}](x)|\lesssim 1
to get

\eqref{F44}=2d\int_{|x|\leq R}|u|^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}dx+\int_{|x|\geq R}\Delta[w_{R}(x)]|u|^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}dx\geq-{\varepsilon}

So (
10.30
) holds.
For
\delta<\delta_{1}

For
\delta(t)<\delta_{1}
. We recall the notation
\tilde{W}_{a}(t)=e^{i\theta(t)}\lambda(t)^{\frac{d-2}{2}}W_{a}(\lambda(t)x)
. Then

(10.38)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(t)

\displaystyle=-8\int_{|x|\geq R}[(|\nabla u|^{2}-(|\nabla\tilde{W}_{a}(t)|^{2})]\,dx+8\int_{|x|\geq R}[|u|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}-|\tilde{W}_{a}(t)|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}]\,dx

(10.39)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{R\leq|x|\leq 2R}(-\Delta\Delta w_{R})[|u|^{2}-|\tilde{W}_{a}(t)|^{2}]\,dx

(10.40)

\displaystyle\quad+4\int_{|x|>R}(\frac{a}{|x|^{4}}x\nabla w_{R}|u(t)|^{2}-\frac{a}{|x|^{4}}x\nabla w_{R}|\tilde{W}_{a}(t)|^{2})dx.

(10.41)

\displaystyle\quad-4\int_{|x|>R}(\frac{2a|u(t)|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}-\frac{2a|\tilde{W}_{a}(t)|^{2}}{|x|^{2}})dx.

(10.42)

\displaystyle\quad-\tfrac{4}{d}\int_{|x|\geq R}\Delta[w_{R}(x)](|u|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}-|\tilde{W}_{a}(t)|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}})\,dx

(10.43)

\displaystyle\quad+4\sum_{j,k}\RE\int_{|x|\geq R}[\overline{u_{j}}u_{k}-\overline{\partial_{j}W(t)}\partial_{k}\tilde{W}_{a}(t)]\partial_{jk}[w_{R}(x)]\,dx

(10.44)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{4}{d+1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Delta[w_{R}(x)]|u|^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}\,dx

for all
t\geq 0
such that
\delta(t)<\delta_{1}
.

As
|\Delta\Delta w_{R}|\lesssim 1/|x|^{2}
,
|\nabla w_{R}|\lesssim|x|
,
|\partial_{jk}[w_{R}]|\lesssim 1
and
|\Delta[w_{R}]|\lesssim 1
, (
10.38
)–(
10.43
) can be bounded by the following terms

\displaystyle\bigg\{\|u(t)\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{1}(|x|\geq R)}+\bigg\|\frac{u(t)}{x}\bigg\|_{L^{2}(|x|\geq R)}+\|\tilde{W}_{a}(t)\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{1}(|x|\geq R)}+\bigg\|\frac{\tilde{W}_{a}(t)}{x}\bigg\|_{L^{2}(|x|\geq R)}

(10.45)

\displaystyle+\|u(t)\|^{\frac{d+2}{d-2}}_{L_{x}^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}(|x|\geq R)}+\big\|\tilde{W}_{a}(t)\big\|^{\frac{d+2}{d-2}}_{L_{x}^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}(|x|\geq R)}\bigg\}\|g(t)\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}},

where
g(t)=e^{-i\theta(t)}[{u}(t)-\tilde{W}_{a}(t)]
. Moreover, since

\displaystyle\|W_{a}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{1}(|x|\geq R)}=O(R^{-\frac{\beta(d-2)}{2}}),\quad\|W_{a}\|_{L_{x}^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}(|x|\geq R)}=O(R^{-\frac{\beta(d-2)}{2}}),

\displaystyle\bigg\|\frac{W_{a}}{x}\bigg\|_{L_{x}^{2}(|x|\geq R)}=O(R^{-\frac{\beta(d-2)}{2}}),

we have that

\displaystyle\|u(t)\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{1}(|x|\geq R)}+\bigg\|\frac{u(t)}{x}\bigg\|_{L^{2}(|x|\geq R)}+\|\tilde{W}_{a}(t)\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{1}(|x|\geq R)}+\bigg\|\frac{\tilde{W}_{a}(t)}{x}\bigg\|_{L^{2}(|x|\geq R)}

\displaystyle+\|u(t)\|^{\frac{d+2}{d-2}}_{L_{x}^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}(|x|\geq R)}+\big\|\tilde{W}_{a}(t)\big\|^{\frac{d+2}{d-2}}_{L_{x}^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}(|x|\geq R)}

\displaystyle\leq\delta(t)+\delta(t)^{\frac{\beta(d+2)}{2}}+O((\lambda(t)R)^{-\frac{\beta(d+2)}{2}})+O\big(((\lambda(t)R)^{-\frac{\beta(d+2)}{2}}\big).

Thus, for
\delta_{1}\in(0,\delta_{0})
sufficiently small and
R
sufficiently large,

|\eqref{Decomp111}|+|\eqref{Decomp222}|+|\eqref{Decomp333}|+|\eqref{Decomp444}|+|\eqref{Decomp555}|+|\eqref{Decomp666}|\leq\delta(t)\quad\text{when $\delta(t)<\delta_{1}$.}\quad

Taking
\delta_{1}
small if necessary, Proposition
4.1
implies

\frac{4}{d+1}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Delta[w_{R}(x)]|u|^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}dx\right|\lesssim\delta(t)^{2}\lesssim{\delta}_{1}\delta(t)\leq\delta(t),

Putting together the estimates above yields (
10.31
).

Integrating inequality (
10.25
) on
[t_{1},t_{2}]
and using the estimates (
10.28
), (
10.30
) and (
10.31
), we deduce

\int^{t_{2}}_{t_{1}}\delta(t)\,dt\lesssim\rho^{2}_{{\varepsilon}}\sup_{t\in[t_{1},t_{2}]}\tfrac{1}{\lambda(t)^{2}}(\delta(t_{1})+\delta(t_{2}))+(\tfrac{{\varepsilon}}{\delta_{1}}+{\varepsilon}+\delta_{1})\int^{t_{2}}_{t_{1}}\delta(t)\,dt.

For
\delta_{1}\in(0,\delta_{0})
small, and choosing
{\varepsilon}={\varepsilon}(\delta_{1})
sufficiently small yields (
10.24
).
∎

As a direct consequence of the lemma, we can show the existence of a sequence
t_{n}\to\infty
so that
\delta(t_{n})\to 0
:

Proposition 10.9
.

There exists a sequence
t_{n}\to\infty
such that

\lim_{n\to\infty}\delta(t_{n})=0.

Proof.

First we recall that

(10.46)

\displaystyle\lambda(t)\gtrsim 1\quad\text{for all $t\geq 0$.}\quad

By (
10.24
),we have

\int_{0}^{T}\delta(t)\lesssim 1\quad\text{for all $T>0$.}\quad

So we can find a such sequence
{t_{n}}
.
∎

We next show that we may use
\delta
to control the variation of the spatial scale.

Proposition 10.10
.

Let
[t_{1},t_{2}]\subset(0,\infty)
with
t_{1}+\tfrac{1}{\lambda(t_{1})^{2}}<t_{2}
. Then

(10.47)

\left|\tfrac{1}{\lambda(t_{2})^{2}}-\tfrac{1}{\lambda(t_{1})^{2}}\right|\leq\widetilde{C}\int^{t_{2}}_{t_{1}}\delta(t)\,dt.

Proof.

Step 1.
First we show that there exists a constant
C_{1}
such that

(10.48)

\tfrac{\lambda(s)}{\lambda(t)}+\tfrac{\lambda(t)}{\lambda(s)}\leq C_{1}\quad\text{for all $t$, $s\geq 0$ such that $|t-s|\leq\tfrac{1}{\lambda(s)^{2}}$}.

By contradiction, we suppose that there exist two sequences
s_{n}
and
t_{n}
such that

(10.49)

\displaystyle|t_{{n}}-s_{n}|\leq\tfrac{1}{\lambda(s_{n})^{2}}\quad\text{but}\quad\tfrac{\lambda(s_{n})}{\lambda(t_{n})}+\tfrac{\lambda(t_{n})}{\lambda(s_{n})}\to\infty.

Passing to a subsequence, one can suppose that

\lim_{n\to\infty}\lambda(s_{n})^{2}(t_{n}-s_{n})=\tau_{0}\in[-1,1].

Recall that
\lambda(t)\gtrsim 1
for all
t\geq 0
, by (
10.49
) we observe that

\lambda(s_{n})+\lambda(t_{n})\to\infty\quad\text{as $n\to\infty$.}\quad

Case I.

\lambda(s_{n})\to\infty
as
n\to\infty
. From (
10.49
) we deduce
|t_{n}-s_{n}|\to 0
as
n\to\infty
. Then by the local
theory for (
CNLSa
) and
H_{a}^{1}
-boundedness we may obtain that

\|u(t_{n})-u(s_{n})\|_{\dot{H}^{1}}\lesssim\|u(t_{n})-u(s_{n})\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}\to 0\quad\text{as $n\to\infty$. }\quad

Using the change of variables, we deduce that

\|\lambda(s_{n})^{-{\frac{d-2}{2}}}u(t_{n},\tfrac{\cdot}{\lambda(s_{n})})-\lambda(s_{n})^{-{\frac{d-2}{2}}}u(s_{n},\tfrac{\cdot}{\lambda(s_{n})})\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}\to 0\quad\text{as $n\to\infty$.}\quad

Now, by compactness,
\psi\neq 0
, thus we have

(10.50)

\displaystyle\lambda(s_{n})^{-{\frac{d-2}{2}}}u(t_{n},\tfrac{\cdot}{\lambda(s_{n})})\to\psi\quad\text{in $\dot{H}_{a}^{1}$ as $n\to\infty$}\quad

along some subsequence of
\{s_{n}\}
. Writing
w_{n}(x):=\lambda(s_{n})^{-{\frac{d-2}{2}}}u(t_{n},\tfrac{\cdot}{\lambda(s_{n})})
, we can use the compactness condition again to deduce

(10.51)

\displaystyle\bigl(\tfrac{\lambda(s_{n})}{\lambda(t_{n})}\bigr)^{\frac{d-2}{2}}w_{n}(\tfrac{\lambda(s_{n})}{\lambda(t_{n})}x)=\lambda(t_{n})^{-{\frac{d-2}{2}}}u(t_{n},\tfrac{\cdot}{\lambda(t_{n})})\to\zeta\quad\text{in $\dot{H}_{a}^{1}$ as $n\to\infty$}\quad

for some
\zeta
, which is necessarily nonzero due to conservation of energy. Finally, by (
10.51
) we see that there exists
C_{0},C_{1}>0
so that

(10.52)

\displaystyle\int_{C_{0}\,\tfrac{\lambda(s_{n})}{\lambda(t_{n})}\leq|x|\leq 2C_{0}\,\tfrac{\lambda(s_{n})}{\lambda(t_{n})}}|\nabla w_{n}(x)|^{2}dx\geq C_{1}\mbox{ for all large }n.

However, using (
10.50
), (
10.49
) and Lebesgue’s dominated convergence theorem to deduce that

\int_{C_{0}\,\tfrac{\lambda(s_{n})}{\lambda(t_{n})}\leq|x|\leq 2C_{0}\,\tfrac{\lambda(s_{n})}{\lambda(t_{n})}}|\nabla w_{n}(x)|^{2}dx\to 0

as
n\to\infty
, which is contradict to (
10.52
).

Case II.

\lambda(t_{n})\to\infty
. We will show that
\lambda(s_{n})\to\infty
.

By contradiction, we assume that
\lambda(s_{n})
is bounded. By (
10.49
) we suppose that there exists
\tau\in\mathbb{R}
such that

t_{n}-s_{n}\to\tau
. Since
\lambda(s_{n})
is bounded, by compactness (
10.8
), it is not difficult to verify that up to subsequence,
u(s_{n})\to v_{0}
in
\dot{H}_{a}^{1}
as
n\to+\infty
for some
v_{0}\in H_{a}^{1}
. By conservation of mass, we also have that
u(s_{n})\to v_{0}
in
L^{\frac{2d+2}{d-1}}
. Especially,
E_{a}(v_{0})=E_{a}^{c}(W_{a})
. Furthermore, we have
\delta(v_{0})\geq c>0
by (
3.4
) .

From Corollary
and Lemma
3.4
we deduce that the solution
v
to (
CNLSa
) with initial data
v_{0}
is global and satisfies
\delta(v(t))>0
for all
t\geq 0
. In addition, Lemma
2.11
implies that
u(s_{n}+\tau)\to v(\tau)
in
\dot{H}_{a}^{1}
and so

\delta(u(s_{n}+\tau))\geq c_{0}
for
n
large. From the local
theory for (
CNLSa
) we have
\|u(t_{n})-u(s_{n}+\tau)\|_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}\to 0

as
n\to+\infty
. This implies
\delta(t_{n})\geq c_{0}
for some
c_{0}>0
and all
n
large.

Since
\delta(t_{n})\geq c_{0}
, we deduce that
t_{n}\to t_{*}\in[0,\infty)
along some subsequence from Lemma
10.6
. By continuity and compactness, we obtain that

(10.53)

\displaystyle u(t_{n})\to u(t_{*})\neq 0\quad\text{ strongly in $H_{a}^{1}$,}\quad

(10.54)

\displaystyle\lambda(t_{n})^{-{\frac{d-2}{2}}}u(t_{n},\tfrac{\cdot}{\lambda(t_{n})})\to\zeta\neq 0\quad\text{ strongly in $\dot{H}_{a}^{1}$.}\quad

As
\lambda(t_{n})\to\infty
, (
10.53
) and (
10.54
) together yield a contradiction. Thus
\lambda(s_{n})\to\infty
as
n\to\infty
and hence Scenario I implies (
10.48
).

Step 2.

There exists
\delta_{1}>0
, so that for any
T\geq 0
, either

(10.55)

\inf_{t\in[T,T+\tfrac{1}{\lambda(T)^{2}}]}\delta(t)\geq\delta_{1}\quad\text{or}\quad\sup_{t\in[T,T+\tfrac{1}{\lambda(T)^{2}}]}\delta(t)<\delta_{0}.

Suppose instead that there exist
t_{n}^{*}\geq 0
and

t_{n}
,
t^{\prime}_{n}\in[t_{n}^{*},t_{n}^{*}+\tfrac{1}{\lambda(t_{n}^{*})^{2}}]
so that

(10.56)

\displaystyle\delta(t_{n})\to 0\quad\text{and}\quad\delta(t^{\prime}_{n})\geq\delta_{0}.

Notice that
t_{n}\to\infty
. Indeed, if
t_{n}\leq C
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
, then
t_{n}\to a\in[0,\infty)
up to a subsequence. By continuity and (
10.56
), we derive that
\delta(a)=0
, which contradicts the fact that
\delta(t)>0
for all
t\geq 0
. Thus, as
t_{n}\to\infty
and
\delta(t_{n})\to 0
, Lemma
10.6
yields that

(10.57)

\displaystyle\lambda(t_{n})\to\infty\quad\text{as\ }{n\to\infty}.

On the other hand, we claim that
\lambda(t^{\prime}_{n})
is bounded. In fact, this follows from Lemma
10.6
and (
10.56
) if
t^{\prime}_{n}
is unbounded, and by Step 1 above if
t^{\prime}_{n}
is bounded.

The boundedness of
\lambda(t_{n}^{\prime})
implies that
\lambda(t_{n})
is bounded, which is a contradiction.

Step 3.
In this step, we will prove that there exists a constant
C>0
such that

(10.58)

\displaystyle 0\leq t_{1}\leq t_{1}^{\prime}\leq t_{2}^{\prime}\leq t_{2}=t_{1}+\tfrac{1}{C^{2}_{1}\lambda(t_{1})^{2}}\Rightarrow\left|\tfrac{1}{\lambda(t_{2}^{\prime})^{2}}-\tfrac{1}{\lambda(t_{1}^{\prime})^{2}}\right|\leq C\int^{t_{2}}_{t_{1}}\delta(t)\,dt,

for some
C>0
. Here
C_{1}\geq 1
is the constant given in Step 1.

By Step 2, we can suppose that
\sup_{t\in[t_{1},t_{2}]}\delta(t)<\delta_{0}
or

\inf_{t\in[t_{1},t_{2}]}\delta(t)\geq\delta_{1}
. In the former case, we deduce (
10.58
) via the estimate

\left|\tfrac{\lambda^{\prime}(t)}{\lambda(t)^{3}}\right|\lesssim\delta(t)\quad\text{for}\quad\delta(t)<\delta_{0}.

In the last case, we note that
\int^{t_{2}}_{t_{1}}\delta_{1}\,dt\geq\int^{t_{2}}_{t_{1}}\delta(t)\,dt
and

|t_{1}^{\prime}-t_{2}^{\prime}|\leq\tfrac{1}{C^{2}_{1}\lambda(t_{1})^{2}}\leq\tfrac{1}{\lambda(t_{1}^{\prime})^{2}}.

Thus, by Step 1 we obtain

\left|\tfrac{1}{\lambda(t_{2}^{\prime})^{2}}-\tfrac{1}{\lambda(t_{1}^{\prime})^{2}}\right|\leq\tfrac{2C^{2}_{1}}{\lambda(t_{1}^{\prime})^{2}}\leq\tfrac{2C^{4}_{1}}{\lambda({t_{1}})^{2}}=2C^{5}_{1}|t_{2}-t_{1}|\leq\tfrac{2C^{5}_{1}}{\delta_{1}}\int^{t_{2}}_{t_{1}}\delta(t)dt.

Combining the inequalities above yields (
10.58
).
∎

We now rule out the possibility of infinite-time blowup:

Proof of Theorem
10.3
.

Suppose
u
is a solution as in Proposition
9.1
with
T^{*}=\infty
. In particular,
E_{a}(u_{0})=E_{a}^{c}(W_{a})
,
\|u_{0}\|^{2}_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}<\|W_{a}\|^{2}_{\dot{H}_{a}^{1}}
and
there exists a function
0<\lambda(t)<\infty
for all
t\geq 0
, such that the set

\left\{\lambda(t)^{-{\frac{d-2}{2}}}u(t,\tfrac{x}{\lambda(t)}):t\in[0,\infty)\right\}\quad\text{is pre-compact in $\dot{H}_{a}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})$}.

From Proposition
10.9
, there exists an increasing sequence
t_{n}\to\infty
with
\delta(t_{n})\to 0
. In particular, we can choose
t_{n}
so that

\delta(t_{0})\leq\tfrac{1}{2C^{*}}\quad\text{and}\quad\delta(t_{n})\to 0,

where
C^{*}:=C\cdot\widetilde{C}
, with
C
and
\widetilde{C}
the constants in (
10.24
) and (
10.47
).

Consider
0\leq a\leq b
. For
n
large we see that
b+\tfrac{1}{\lambda(b)^{2}}<t_{n}
. By estimates (
10.24
) and (
10.47
), we find

\left|\tfrac{1}{\lambda(b)^{2}}-\tfrac{1}{\lambda(t_{n})^{2}}\right|\leq C^{*}\sup_{a\leq t\leq t_{n}}\bigl(\tfrac{1}{\lambda(t)^{2}}\bigr)\left\{\delta(a)+\delta(t_{n})\right\}.

Sending
n\to\infty
, it follows that

\sup_{t\geq a}\tfrac{1}{\lambda(t)^{2}}\leq C^{*}\delta(a)\sup_{t\geq a}\tfrac{1}{\lambda(t)^{2}}.

Choosing
a=t_{0}
, we get

\sup_{t\geq t_{0}}\tfrac{1}{\lambda(t)^{2}}=0.

Lemma
10.8
then implies that
\delta(t)=0
for all
t\geq t_{0}
, a contradiction.∎

Proposition
9.1
and Theorems
10.1
–
10.3
now yield Theorem
1.12
(i).
